# Decisions & assumptions (autonomous run)

Appended by agents whenever they choose between options without a human.
One `## ` section per step, oldest first — the orchestrator writes those headings,
so `grep -n '^## '` is the index and a session can read only the part it needs.


## architect


- [architect/design] Ten Django apps under `apps/` exactly as spec §3 names them, plus `config/`; no extra `api` or `reports` app — why: the chart JSON endpoints are three views inside `dashboards` and the export layer is a package next to the tables it mirrors, so a separate app would only add an import boundary — alternatives: separate `api` app, separate `reports` app, a single monolithic app.
- [architect/design] `scope_for_user(user) -> ScopeFilter` is the only authorization path, and every `selectors.py` function takes a `ScopeFilter` instead of starting from `Model.objects.all()` — why: the same data leaves through pages, htmx fragments, chart JSON, tables, CSV and XLSX, and per-view decorators protect the view but not the export — alternatives: per-view `PermissionRequiredMixin`, django-guardian, DB row-level security (impossible on SQLite).
- [architect/design] An out-of-scope project/repository id in a query string is dropped silently rather than answered with 403 — why: a 403 confirms the id exists to a user who should not know it — alternatives: 403, 404.
- [architect/design] `DailyRollup` stores only `counter` values (and ratio numerators/denominators); medians, percentiles and size buckets are computed from raw rows on read; `state` metrics are reconstructed from dates — why: a median of daily medians is not the period median, and spec §8.3 forbids it — alternatives: roll up everything, roll up nothing, per-day t-digest sketches (deferred to ~10× data).
- [architect/design] Rollups are rebuilt from a dirty-days set recorded during post-processing, by deleting and rewriting exactly the affected `(date, scope, scope_id, cohort, metric_key)` rows — why: makes a half-finished rollup self-healing on the next run and makes `recompute --from --to` the single repair tool — alternatives: incremental counter updates, full nightly rebuild.
- [architect/design] Metric cache key includes a `last_data_version` integer incremented at the end of every sync and recompute, rather than deleting cache keys on write — why: one integer to get right instead of a matrix of key patterns to enumerate — alternatives: explicit key invalidation, short TTL, no cache.
- [architect/assumption] `httpx` timeouts are connect 10 s / read 30 s, and retries are 5 attempts with exponential backoff and jitter capped at 60 s — why: spec §5.4 requires retries on 502/503/secondary limits but gives no numbers — alternatives: shorter caps, unlimited retries.
- [architect/assumption] `Repository.sync_cursor` is kept as a field but correctness in v1 rests on the time watermark plus `SYNC_OVERLAP`; `last_synced_at` advances only after a whole repository finishes — why: a per-page watermark trades a cheap re-read for a permanent gap when a page fails after the watermark moved — alternatives: per-page resume, cursor-only resume.
- [architect/design] A nested GraphQL connection (reviews, comments, commits, files) that cannot be fully paginated fails that PR loudly instead of storing a truncated list — why: silently short data corrupts a metric, which is worse than a visible gap in `SyncRun.error_log` — alternatives: store what arrived, retry forever.
- [architect/design] Every huey task is a three-line wrapper over a service function that a management command calls directly, and the worker runs a single thread — why: the operator is one person on a laptop who will find the worker stopped, and SQLite has one writer so a second thread mostly produces `database is locked` — alternatives: Celery+Redis, threads in the web process, cron-only with no worker.
- [architect/assumption] On worker startup, `ExportJob` rows stuck in `running` for more than one hour are marked `failed` — why: otherwise the htmx progress poll never terminates after a killed worker — alternatives: no sweep, a heartbeat column.
- [architect/design] Huey uses its own `DATA_DIR/huey.sqlite3`, separate from `db.sqlite3` — why: frequent short queue writes and rare long domain writes should not share one write lock — alternatives: one database file, a non-SQLite broker.
- [architect/design] Tests run with `HUEY = {"immediate": True}`; the two paths that must not be inline (large-export hand-off, "button enqueues rather than blocks") assert the enqueue instead of the execution — why: keeps task code covered without a queue process in the test run — alternatives: a real consumer in tests, mocking the task decorator.
- [architect/decision] Tailwind version is pinned by the run and mapped onto the token variables explicitly: v4 via `@custom-variant dark (&:where([data-theme="dark"], [data-theme="dark"] *))` + `@theme inline`, or v3 via `darkMode: ['selector', '[data-theme="dark"]']` — why: spec §10.5's snippet is v3 syntax while the current line is v4 (latest release checked: v4.3.3), and only the attribute-bound dark variant satisfies the three-state stored preference — alternatives: Tailwind's default `prefers-color-scheme` dark mode, `class="dark"`, hand-written CSS, Pico.css (rejected at intake).
- [architect/decision] The Tailwind standalone binary is downloaded on demand into a git-ignored `.tools/` by `make css`, not committed — why: a ~100 MB platform-specific artefact does not belong in the repository, while the ~50 KB generated `static/css/app.css` does — alternatives: commit the binary, npm + PostCSS (rejected at intake), a CDN build.
- [architect/design] A test asserts `static/css/app.css` contains every token name defined in `tokens.css` — why: the stylesheet is a generated file under version control and can silently go stale after a template change — alternatives: rebuild CSS in the test gate (needs the binary and a network fetch in CI), no check.
- [architect/decision] `config/settings/e2e.py` is added alongside spec §13's `base`/`local`/`prod`, as prod plus a localhost `ALLOWED_HOSTS` and a separate e2e database — why: the e2e suite must run with `DEBUG=False` so a real 500 is a real error page, and it must not share the developer's database — alternatives: run e2e against `local` (hides 500s behind the debug page), env-var overrides on `prod`.
- [architect/decision] `make e2e-up` / `make e2e-down` are the e2e surface commands, with the Makefile created in phase 0 — why: the orchestrator accepts `make`, and backgrounding a server plus a worker and then blocking until the port answers cannot be expressed as a plain `&&` chain — alternatives: a shell script (refused by the orchestrator), a compose file (no containers in v1), `runserver` in the foreground (never returns).
- [architect/decision] E2E runs on port 8100 and `pytest`'s `testpaths` excludes `e2e/` — why: 8000 is the developer's own `runserver`, and the unit gate must not collect specs that need a live server — alternatives: 8001, a random port, a marker-based exclusion.
- [architect/decision] The lint gate is `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` + a bare `mypy` (with `files` pinned in `pyproject.toml` to `apps/metrics` and every `services.py`/`selectors.py`) + `makemigrations --check --dry-run` + `manage.py check` — why: spec §12 scopes mypy to `services/` and `metrics/`, and a model change without a migration must fail the build rather than surface at runtime — alternatives: `mypy apps` (types the whole project, far more churn), mypy path list on the command line, no migration check.
- [architect/assumption] Non-functional numbers the spec leaves open: 150 repositories and ~60 000 PRs before the design is revisited, 1–5 named users with ≤3 concurrent sessions, a 50-repo incremental sync under 30 minutes, `busy_timeout=5000 ms`, warm-cache dashboard under 300 ms, list page under 800 ms, background export ≤5 min for 200 k rows, churn ≤4 concurrent git workers, `runserver` ready in under 5 s — why: spec §14 phase 10 gives only "50 repos / 20k PRs" and "< 1.5 s", and the rest are needed to size indexes, timeouts and the rollup strategy — alternatives: leaving them unstated and discovering them in phase 10.
- [architect/assumption] Backup and restore is "stop both processes, copy `DATA_DIR`", documented in `docs/SETUP.md`, with `dumpdata` of the operator-owned models as the portable form — why: the spec names no backup story, and the operator-owned data (projects, identity mapping, policy, violation decisions, notes) cannot be recovered by re-syncing GitHub — alternatives: an automated snapshot task, no documented story.
- [architect/assumption] A failed `git fetch` or corrupt bare clone is deleted and re-cloned once, then reported as `ChurnResult.status='error'` — why: clones are disposable derived data and a half-fetched clone would poison every later blame — alternatives: fail immediately, retry indefinitely.
- [architect/design] `PolicyViolation` and `GitHubConnection.last_check_result` store a code plus parameters and never a rendered message; the same holds for sync errors shown in the UI — why: storing English text would freeze the wording at write time and make the Ukrainian UI permanently half-translated — alternatives: store rendered text, store both.
- [architect/design] `ReviewComment` stores only length and metadata, never comment text; `PullRequest.body` is stored because disclosure parsing needs it — why: less data about named people, less to leak, less to explain — alternatives: store all bodies behind `STORE_RAW_PAYLOADS`.
- [architect/design] Auto-resolve moves violations only `open → resolved` with an automatic marker, and never touches `acknowledged` or `waived` — why: those two states are a lead's recorded judgement about a person and must not be overwritten by a re-run — alternatives: auto-resolve any state, no auto-resolve.
- [architect/design] `conftest.py` installs a `respx` default that fails on any unmocked outbound request — why: the intake forbids a live GitHub call at any point, and a test asserting fixtures is not the same as a guarantee that nothing escaped — alternatives: trust the mocks, block the network at the OS level.
- [architect/verification] Environment claims in `.autodev/PROFILE.md` were checked, not assumed: `uv --version` → `uv 0.11.21`; `python3 --version` → `Python 3.13.3`; `git --version` → `git version 2.50.1 (Apple Git-155)`; `python3 -c "import sqlite3; print(sqlite3.sqlite_version)"` → `3.49.1`; `curl -sSI https://pypi.org/simple/` → `HTTP/2 200` and `curl -sSI https://github.com` → `HTTP/2 200` (so the network **is** available to `uv sync`, `make css` and `make vendor` — the no-GitHub-API rule is an intake constraint, not an environment limitation); `ls ~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright/` → `chromium-1234`, `chromium_headless_shell-1234`, `ffmpeg-1011` (browsers already installed). Latest Django 5.2 patch on PyPI: `5.2.17`. Latest Tailwind release: `v4.3.3` with `tailwindcss-macos-arm64` published.
- [orchestrator/architect] lint_command set to `uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check . && uv run mypy && uv run python manage.py makemigrations --check --dry-run && uv run python manage.py check` by this session (was `(none)`)
- [orchestrator/architect] e2e_command set to `uv run pytest e2e -q` by this session (was `(none)`)
- [orchestrator/architect] e2e_up_command set to `make e2e-up` by this session (was `(none)`)
- [orchestrator/architect] e2e_down_command set to `make e2e-down` by this session (was `(none)`)

## roadmap

- [roadmap/decision] The roadmap keeps spec §14's own eleven phases (0–10), numbering and order unchanged, rather than compressing them into the 3–10 range the step suggests — why: intake settled scope as "all phases 0 through 10 of section 14, in order", the order is already dependency-correct, and merging phases would make a phase exceed one focused session — alternatives: merge 0+1 (skeleton+models), merge 7+8 (all UI), re-derive an independent phase order.
- [roadmap/decision] `test_command` is confirmed as `uv run pytest -q`, unchanged from `.autodev/PROFILE.md` — why: it is the whole unit/integration suite from the repository root, non-interactive, and `testpaths = ["apps", "tests"]` keeps `e2e/` (which needs a live server) out of it — alternatives: `uv run pytest`, adding `-x`, including `e2e/` in the default run.
- [roadmap/verification] The command cannot be executed yet because no project skeleton exists: `ls /Users/korkholeh/Projects/pr-radar` → `.autodev  .git  docs` only, no `manage.py`, no `pyproject.toml`. `uv --version` → `uv 0.11.21 (5aa65dd7a 2026-06-11 aarch64-apple-darwin)`, so the runner itself is present. Phase 0 (`skeleton`) is the phase that must make `uv run pytest -q` work on a clean checkout.
- [roadmap/decision] Phases 0, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 9 and 10 are marked `user_facing`; phases 1 (`data-model`) and 6 (`metrics-registry`) are not — why: 1 delivers only migrations plus Django admin and 6 delivers a registry plus a management command, neither of which adds a page or a flow a lead operates, while every other phase ships something visible in the app — alternatives: marking phase 1 user-facing because Django admin is a UI, marking every phase user-facing.
- [roadmap/assumption] Phase 3 owns both identity resolution and the derived PR fields, as spec §14 phase 3 groups them — why: the derived fields depend on identity (rubber stamp, self-merge and review rounds all need "not the author, not a bot"), so splitting them would force phase 4 to redo the wiring — alternatives: identity alone in 3 with derived fields moved into 4.
- [roadmap/assumption] `manage.py seed_e2e` is a separate command from `seed_demo`, created in phase 0 — why: `make e2e-up` in `.autodev/PROFILE.md` calls it in phase 0, while `seed_demo` (spec §5.5) is a phase 7 deliverable sized for UI development and profiling — alternatives: one command with a `--scale` flag from phase 0, e2e writing to the database directly.
- [roadmap/assumption] The `seed_demo --scale` option used for the phase 10 performance target (50 repositories / 20 000 PRs) is added in phase 10, not phase 7 — why: phase 7 needs readable demo data, phase 10 needs volume, and generating 20 k PRs on every phase-7 test run would be pure cost — alternatives: one fixed large seed from phase 7, a separate profiling fixture.
- [roadmap/assumption] Spec §10.6's two export types are split across phases: table export (CSV + XLSX, `columns.py`/`csv.py`/`xlsx.py`) lands in phase 7 with the tables it exports, and the multi-sheet dashboard report plus `ExportJob` background exports land in phase 8 — why: spec §14 already assigns the export layer to 7 and the reports to 8, and a report sheet cannot be written before the pages it summarises exist — alternatives: the whole export layer in 8, the whole export layer in 7.
- [roadmap/assumption] `ci_first_pass_rate` and `followup_fix_rate` are computed in phase 9 alongside churn, but are registered as `MetricDef`s in phase 6 returning `None` until then — why: spec §14 puts both in phase 9 while spec §8.2 lists them in the registry, and a registry with holes would break the `metrics_doc` freshness test and the dashboard KPI row — alternatives: registering them only in phase 9 (registry churn, doc regenerated twice), computing them early in 6 (needs `CheckStatus` semantics and the file-overlap heuristic before their phase).
- [roadmap/assumption] The four leak/isolation guarantees are asserted in the phase that introduces the exit, not once at the end: token leak in 2 (database, log, `error_log`, page) and again in 9 (git argv, remote URL, `.git/config`); project isolation in 8 across pages, chart JSON, CSV and XLSX separately — why: risks #2 and #3 are the two unrecoverable ones, and a single end-of-run audit would find them after ten phases of code assumed them safe — alternatives: one security phase at the end, a single combined leak test.
- [roadmap/assumption] `docs/METRICS.md` is generated in phase 6 and regenerated whenever the registry changes; the remaining `docs/*` user documentation is completed in phase 10, with `SETUP.md`, `CONFIGURATION.md` and `TRANSLATIONS.md` created in phase 0 and `GITHUB_CONNECTIONS.md` in phase 2 — why: a document is written by the phase that makes its content true, and the three phase-0 documents are what an operator needs to run the skeleton at all — alternatives: all documentation in phase 10, a documentation stub per phase.

## p01-plan


- [p01-plan/decision] `mypy.files` is `["apps/*/services.py", "apps/*/selectors.py"]` in phase 1 instead of `.autodev/PROFILE.md`'s longer list — why: mypy errors on a glob that matches nothing and `apps/metrics` does not exist until phase 7, which will append it — alternatives: `files = ["apps", "config"]` (types the whole project, rejected by the architect), passing paths on the command line, an empty stub `apps/metrics` package.
- [p01-plan/decision] `apps/dashboards` is created in phase 1 with a single placeholder Overview view at `/` — why: `base.html`, the navigation, the post-login redirect target and the e2e login spec all need one real page, and any other home would be moved again in phase 8 — alternatives: a `TemplateView` in `config/urls.py`, a home view inside `accounts`, no home page at all.
- [p01-plan/decision] The navigation renders only the items whose pages already exist, growing phase by phase, rather than spec §10.1's full list with dead links — why: a dead nav link answers 404 instead of the login redirect and breaks the parametrized `urlpatterns` test, and it misleads the operator — alternatives: disabled placeholder items, all items pointing at the Overview page.
- [p01-plan/decision] `make vendor` vendors htmx 2.0.10 only; `static/vendor/chart.umd.js` arrives in phase 8 — why: committing a ~200 KB artefact that no page loads and no test can keep honest is pure cost until the first chart exists — alternatives: vendoring both now, a CDN (rejected by the spec).
- [p01-plan/decision] Python/JS duration-formatter parity is proved by a shared case table (`tests/fixtures/duration_cases.json`) asserted in pytest for Python and in a Playwright spec for JS, rather than by running the JS in Node — why: `.autodev/PROFILE.md` states the project has no Node build, and the e2e browser is a JS runtime already in the test path — alternatives: a Node-based unit test, duplicating the expectations in two places, no parity test.
- [p01-plan/decision] SQLite pragmas (`journal_mode=WAL`, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `foreign_keys=ON`, `busy_timeout=5000`) are applied one statement at a time in a `connection_created` receiver in `config/db.py`, with `OPTIONS["transaction_mode"]="IMMEDIATE"` merged only for a SQLite `DATABASE_URL` — why: sqlite3 rejects a multi-statement `init_command`, and every SQLite-specific setting must vanish when `DATABASE_URL` points at PostgreSQL (ADR 0001) — alternatives: a multi-pragma `init_command`, a custom database backend, pragmas in `manage.py`.
- [p01-plan/decision] The colour-literal grep test excludes `static/css/tokens.css` (the token file), `static/css/app.css` (generated by Tailwind, which emits its own palette) and `static/vendor/`; staleness of the generated file is covered instead by the test that every `--token` name in `tokens.css` appears in `app.css` — why: scanning a generated stylesheet for hex would fail on Tailwind's own output and force the rule to be weakened — alternatives: grep `app.css` too (impossible), no exclusion list, no token-presence test.
- [p01-plan/decision] The `admin` and `lead` groups are created empty by an `accounts` data migration in phase 1; permissions are attached in phase 2 when `catalog.manage_settings` exists, and `UserProjectAccess` is deferred to phase 2 because it needs `catalog.Project` — why: keeps phase 1 free of the catalog dependency while `scope_for_user()` and the `ScopeFilter` contract still ship now — alternatives: creating a stub `Project` model early, deferring the groups to phase 2.
- [p01-plan/decision] `accounts.views.set_language` and `set_theme` are `@login_not_required` POST endpoints that write the cookie always and `UserPreference` only when authenticated — why: spec §10.5/§10.7 put both switchers on the login page, where no user exists yet — alternatives: switchers only after login, Django's built-in `set_language` plus a second authenticated view, a GET endpoint (rejected: it mutates).
- [p01-plan/verification] Environment facts this plan relies on, checked on 2026-09-17: `which msgfmt xgettext msgmerge` → `/opt/homebrew/bin/msgfmt`, `/opt/homebrew/bin/xgettext`, `/opt/homebrew/bin/msgmerge` (so `make messages` and `compilemessages` work); `uv --version` → `uv 0.11.21`; `ls ~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright` → `chromium-1234` present; `curl -sSI -L .../releases/download/v4.3.3/tailwindcss-macos-arm64` → `200` (Tailwind standalone v4.3.3 downloadable by `make css`); npm registry reports `tailwindcss` latest `4.3.3` and `htmx.org` latest `2.0.10` — both pinned at those versions.

## p01-implement

- [p01-implement] `UserLanguageMiddleware` overwrites `request.COOKIES[LANGUAGE_COOKIE_NAME]` instead of a session key — why: this Django pin (5.2.17) removed session-based i18n storage entirely; `django.utils.translation.LANGUAGE_SESSION_KEY` no longer exists and `LocaleMiddleware`/`get_language_from_request` resolve language from the cookie, Accept-Language and `LANGUAGE_CODE` only — alternatives: session key (does not exist on this Django version), a custom `process_request` reimplementation of `LocaleMiddleware`.
- [p01-implement] `e2e/support/auth.py::log_in` resets language to English after every login (via the real switcher, not a DB write) but leaves theme untouched — why: `e2e/web` personas are shared database rows reused across specs, so a language change made by one spec would leak into the next; theme is the one property `test_theme.py` itself owns end-to-end (including restoring it to `system` in a `finally` block), so a second reset point would fight the thing being tested — alternatives: reset both preferences on every login (breaks the fresh-login persistence assertion in `test_theme.py`), a fresh persona per spec (needs a second seed command), resetting via direct ORM writes (bypasses the product's own mechanism, against `e2e-authoring.md`).
- [p01-implement] `test_duration_format.py` loads `static/js/formatting.js` into the page with `page.add_script_tag(content=...)` reading the committed file directly, instead of via a `<script src>` tag — why: the file is not wired into any template yet (no chart consumes it until phase 8) and `STATICFILES_STORAGE` is `ManifestStaticFilesStorage`, so an unhashed `/static/js/formatting.js` URL 404s in the e2e environment — alternatives: add a script tag to `base.html` now (premature, no consumer), reference the hashed manifest name from Python (couples the test to Tailwind's hash), copy the file's source into the test (a second copy to keep in sync).


## p01-review_fix1


- [p01-review_fix1] Compile `locale/uk/LC_MESSAGES/*.mo` from a session-scoped autouse fixture in root `conftest.py` using `polib.pofile(...).save_as_mofile(...)` rather than shelling out to `msgfmt` or `manage.py compilemessages` — why: `.mo` is gitignored so a clean checkout has none and the translation tests failed (BLOCKER in REVIEW-r1), and `polib` is already a dev dependency, so this needs no assumption that `msgfmt` is installed on the machine running the tests — alternatives: commit `.mo` files, call `compilemessages` from conftest (recompiles Django's own bundled catalogues too, per the Makefile finding below).
- [p01-review_fix1] Huey immediacy for tests is set by mutating the real `huey.contrib.djhuey.HUEY` singleton's `.immediate` attribute directly in the `conftest.py` fixture (restored after each test), not by assigning `settings.HUEY` — why: REVIEW-r1 verified `djhuey` builds its `HUEY` instance once from the setting at app-ready time, so mutating `settings.HUEY` afterward (the phase-1 approach) never reached the running queue and the old test asserted the fixture's own assignment, not the queue — alternatives: a dedicated `config/settings/test.py`, monkeypatching `HUEY.immediate` per-test instead of via a shared autouse fixture.
- [p01-review_fix1] `apps/accounts/tests/test_settings.py`'s pragma test now calls `config.db.apply_sqlite_pragmas` directly against a real sqlite3 connection (and a fake-postgres connection for the no-op branch), plus a `django.db.connection` pragma check and a `configure_sqlite_transaction_mode` postgres no-op test — why: the old test executed `SQLITE_PRAGMAS` itself via a bare `sqlite3.connect()`, so it passed regardless of whether the `connection_created` receiver was even registered (MAJOR, risk #14 unmitigated) — alternatives: only testing through `django.db.connection` (can't observe `journal_mode=wal` reliably against the test DB, which pytest-django may run in-memory).
- [p01-review_fix1] Replaced `STATICFILES_STORAGE` (a no-op setting on this Django 5.2 pin) with a `STORAGES` dict in `config/settings/prod.py` (manifest storage for staticfiles) and dropped it from `base.py` entirely, so local/test fall back to Django's own default plain `StaticFilesStorage` — why: REVIEW-r1 verified at runtime that `STATICFILES_STORAGE` had zero effect and `settings.STORAGES` never changed — alternatives: keep the dead setting and only fix docs (rejected — the setting doing nothing is the actual bug).
- [p01-review_fix1] Turning manifest storage on for real surfaced a second, previously-masked bug: `collectstatic` crashes post-processing `static/css/src/input.css` because whitenoise's manifest post-processor tries to resolve `@import "tailwindcss";` as a relative file reference. Fixed by adding `--ignore=src` to the `collectstatic` call in `make e2e-up` and documenting the same flag for a future real deployment in `docs/SETUP.md` — why: `static/css/src/` is Tailwind CLI input, never meant to be served, and plain `StaticFilesStorage` never inspected file contents so this never fired before manifest storage was actually active — alternatives: move `static/css/src/` outside `STATICFILES_DIRS` (bigger restructuring of an established layout other tests/docs already reference), a custom `IGNORE_PATTERNS` finder setting.
- [p01-review_fix1] `config/settings/prod.py` now requires `SECRET_KEY` from the environment (`environ.Env().str("SECRET_KEY")`, no default) and raises `ImproperlyConfigured` if unset; `config/settings/e2e.py` sets `os.environ.setdefault("SECRET_KEY", "e2e-not-a-production-secret")` before importing `prod` — why: REVIEW-r1 (MINOR) found prod silently accepted the published dev default; e2e is a disposable localhost surface with no real secret to protect, so requiring an operator-supplied value there would only add friction — alternatives: a `.env` requirement enforced by the Makefile, a random key generated at `e2e-up` time.
- [p01-review_fix1] The CSS freshness guard is now a real checksum (`scripts/css_manifest.py`, sidecar `static/css/.build-manifest.sha256` written by `make css`, compared in `tests/test_css_tokens.py::test_app_css_is_not_stale`) over `tokens.css` + `src/input.css` + every `.html`/`.py`/`.js` under `templates/`, `apps/`, `static/js/` — why: REVIEW-r1 (MAJOR) proved the old test passed by construction since `@import "../tokens.css"` inlines every token into `app.css` regardless of staleness — alternatives: parse templates for utility classes and check each resolves in `app.css` (the review's other suggested fix; more precise but more code to keep correct). Trade-off accepted: this makes `make css` necessary after *any* Python change under `apps/`, not just template edits, which is conservative rather than precise — later phases should expect to run `make css` as part of their own green-gate pass whenever they touch `apps/`.
- [p01-review_fix1] JS duration formatting now uses Django's real i18n machinery: `{% url 'javascript-catalog' %}` is loaded in `base.html` for authenticated requests only (matching `/jsi18n/`'s existing login-required URL contract), and `static/js/formatting.js` calls `gettext`/`ngettext`/`interpolate` instead of a hand-rolled `STRINGS` table — why: REVIEW-r1 (MAJOR) found `djangojs.po` was empty and `/jsi18n/` was dead code, so the JS side of risk #8 (Ukrainian lags) was unguarded — alternatives: keep the hand-rolled table with a hand-written parity test (doesn't close the actual gap: a new JS string still wouldn't need a `.po` entry).
- [p01-review_fix1] The JS-side msgids use `%(days)s`/`%(hours)s`/`%(minutes)s`, not the Python side's `%(days)d`/`%(hours)d`/`%(minutes)d`, e.g. `"%(days)sd %(hours)sh"` vs Python's `"%(days)dd %(hours)dh"` — why: Django's JS `interpolate()` helper only recognises `%(key)s` in its substitution regex, never `%(key)d`, so the `%d` msgid rendered as a literal unsubstituted string in the browser (caught by the e2e round trip after the JavaScriptCatalog was actually wired in) — alternatives: none; this is a hard constraint of `interpolate()`, not a style choice, so the two domains' catalogs necessarily hold different (if same-length) msgids for the same concept.
- [p01-review_fix1] `templates/base.html`'s `<title>` now renders as just `{% block title %}{% translate "PR Radar" %}{% endblock %}` (no more `— PR Radar` suffix appended outside the block) — why: REVIEW-r1 (NIT) found the un-overridden case rendered "PR Radar — PR Radar"; removing the outer suffix is the minimal fix and CLAUDE.md/docs/SPEC.md do not mandate a "Page — App" title format — alternatives: keep the suffix and have every child template's `{% block title %}` include it themselves (touches 8 templates for a NIT).
- [p01-review_fix1] REJECTED: REVIEW-r1's NIT "docs/SPEC.md and ADR 0007 were cosmetically reformatted with no decision logged" — the finding is wrong. Verified: `uv run ruff format .` reformats both files identically to the phase-1 diff (ruff 0.16.8 formats embedded Python code fences in `.md` files by default), so the reformatting is required by the lint gate (`uv run ruff format --check .`), not an unrequested edit. Reverting it to test the claim broke `make lint`; re-applied via `ruff format .` and left as-is.


## p01-review_fix2

- [p01-review_fix2] Committed `locale/uk/LC_MESSAGES/*.mo` (dropped `*.mo` from `.gitignore`) instead of documenting a manual `compilemessages` install step, and removed the conftest.py autouse fixture that recompiled `.mo` from `.po` at test-session start — why: REVIEW-r2 (MAJOR) proved a clean install per `docs/SETUP.md` never compiles the catalogues, so the Ukrainian UI silently falls back to English while the test-only fixture made the suite pass regardless; committing `.mo` (same pattern as `static/css/app.css`) makes the shipped artifact the one the tests check — alternatives: add `compilemessages` to the install docs (review's non-preferred alternative; still requires `msgfmt` on the operator's machine and leaves a second gate no test enforces).
- [p01-review_fix2] Added `tests/test_translations.py::test_mo_catalog_matches_committed_po_source`, comparing `polib.mofile()` entries against `polib.pofile()` entries (msgstr + msgstr_plural) rather than comparing file mtimes — why: mtimes are unreliable after a fresh `git clone`/`checkout` (all files can land with the same or reordered mtimes), so a content-equality check is the only reliable staleness signal for a committed generated file — alternatives: mtime comparison (rejected, unreliable across clones), re-running `compilemessages` inside the test to a temp file and diffing bytes (equivalent but slower and needs `msgfmt` on the test machine, which the content-comparison approach avoids).
- [p01-review_fix2] Narrowed both the CSS staleness manifest (`scripts/css_manifest.py`) and Tailwind's own `@source` scanning (`static/css/src/input.css`, via `@source not "../../../apps/*/migrations"` and `.../tests`) to exclude `migrations/` and `tests/` under `apps/` — why: REVIEW-r2 (MINOR) showed an unrelated model/migration/test edit in phases 2+ would fail `test_app_css_is_not_stale` and force a `make css` rerun (and its Tailwind-binary download) with no template change behind it; Tailwind v4.3.3 accepts `@source not "<glob>"` (verified: `.tools/tailwindcss` builds without error and produces a byte-identical utility set once both exclusions are added) — alternatives: keep the wide scan and accept the false-positive staleness failures, drop `apps/**/*.py` from scanning entirely (would miss a real `gettext_lazy`-adjacent Tailwind class embedded in `services.py`/`views.py`, though none exist today).
- [p01-review_fix2] Discovered (not requested by REVIEW-r2, logged for future phases): Tailwind v4's automatic content-detection scans the whole repository in addition to explicit `@source` directives and picks up class-like substrings from non-source text — a fresh `make css` now embeds an unused `.bg-gray-500` utility because that literal string appears in `.autodev/phases/01-skeleton/REVIEW-r2.md`. This is harmless today (no test asserts `app.css` contains *only* expected classes, and CLAUDE.md's colour-literal test excludes generated `app.css`), so left unfixed — alternatives for a later phase if it grows noisy: `@source not ".autodev"` next to the migrations/tests exclusions, or moving to explicit-only sourcing via `source(none)` plus a full manual `@source` list.
- [p01-review_fix2] `tests/test_urls_login.py` now has `UNREVERSIBLE_URL_NAMES` plus `test_every_named_url_is_reversible_with_the_sample_args` asserting it is empty, instead of an `xfail`-marked parametrization — why: REVIEW-r2 (MINOR) found `_reverse()`'s two bare `except: pass` blocks let a future unreversible URL (e.g. one needing a `pk`) silently vanish from the deny-by-default coverage with nothing in `-q` output; a plain assertion fails loudly and names the URL, which is simpler than wiring `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` per-name — alternatives: `xfail(strict=True)` (review's other suggested fix; more moving parts for the same signal).
- [p01-review_fix2] Added an `--on-accent` token (`#ffffff` light / `#0f1115` dark, both ≥4.5:1 against `--accent` — computed via WCAG relative luminance: light 4.57:1, dark 7.83:1) and replaced `text-white` with `text-[var(--on-accent)]` on the three auth-page primary buttons; extended `tests/test_no_hardcoded_colors.py` with a `TAILWIND_PALETTE_CLASS_RE` check (new `test_no_tailwind_palette_color_classes`) that rejects `bg-white`/`text-gray-500`-style Tailwind palette utilities across templates/Python/JS — why: REVIEW-r2 (MINOR) found `text-white` on the dark-mode `--accent` value was ~2.4:1 (fails AA) and was invisible to the existing hex/rgb/hsl grep — alternatives: pick a single fixed on-accent colour for both themes (fails contrast in one theme by construction, since `--accent` itself changes between themes).

- [p01-review_fix2_audit] `static/css/src/input.css` now imports Tailwind with `@import "tailwindcss" source(none);` instead of the bare `@import "tailwindcss";`, keeping the explicit `@source`/`@source not` list as the only content scan — why: REVIEW-r2-audit (MAJOR) proved Tailwind v4's automatic content detection scans the whole repo regardless of `@source`, so a stray palette-class-like string in `.autodev/DECISIONS.md` (logged by the previous fix round itself) made a fresh `./.tools/tailwindcss` build diverge from the committed `app.css` at byte 6546, silently breaking the byte-identical-rebuild guarantee both prior review rounds verified by hand; verified the fix by rebuilding and `cmp`-ing against the committed file (identical) and confirming `bg-white`/`text-gray-500` no longer appear — alternatives: enumerate every non-source directory with `@source not` (the audit's other suggested fix; strictly more paths to maintain forever, and any future doc/decision file mentioning a Tailwind class name reopens the same hole).
- [p01-review_fix2_audit] Updated `.autodev/ARCHITECTURE.md:148,433`, `.autodev/PROFILE.md:41,131` and `.autodev/phases/01-skeleton/PLAN.md:216,332` to state that `locale/uk/LC_MESSAGES/*.mo` are committed, matching what `p01-review_fix2` actually shipped — why: REVIEW-r2-audit (MAJOR) found these docs still said `.mo` files are "not committed" after the fix round committed them, so a later phase reading ARCHITECTURE.md as source-of-truth could reintroduce the `*.mo` gitignore entry and silently kill the Ukrainian UI again — alternatives: none; the docs were simply wrong and needed correcting to match the shipped repository.
- [p01-review_fix2_audit] Reran `make messages` once and committed the resulting `.mo` binaries (`.po` sources unchanged, confirmed via `git diff` showing zero `.po` hunks) — why: REVIEW-r2-audit (MINOR) found the previously-committed `.mo` files were polib output from the since-deleted r1 conftest fixture, not `msgfmt` output from the documented `make messages` producer, so the first operator following `docs/SETUP.md` would get a gratuitous binary diff with no source change behind it — alternatives: leave the polib-built binaries as-is (rejected — both are semantically valid, but the shipped artifact should be produced by the command the docs tell everyone to run).

## p01-e2e

- [p01-e2e] Moved `apps/accounts/templates/registration/{logged_out,login,password_reset_complete,password_reset_confirm,password_reset_done,password_reset_form}.html` to `templates/registration/` — why: manual QA (curl + Playwright against the running e2e surface) found `django.contrib.admin` ships its own `registration/{logged_out,password_reset_*}.html` templates and, being listed before `apps.accounts` in `INSTALLED_APPS`, Django's app_directories loader picked admin's unthemed, English-only, non-`base.html` templates for 5 of the 6 auth pages regardless of the explicit `template_name=` passed in `apps/accounts/urls.py` (only `login.html` survived, since admin's own login lives at `admin/login.html`, a different name). Proof: `curl http://127.0.0.1:8100/accounts/password_reset/` showed `<title>Password reset | Django site admin</title>` and admin's own CSS before the fix. The project's `templates/` dir is already first in `TEMPLATES[0]["DIRS"]`, which the filesystem loader checks before `APP_DIRS`, so this fix is robust to any future `INSTALLED_APPS` reordering — alternatives: reorder `INSTALLED_APPS` to put `apps.accounts` before `django.contrib.admin` (fragile — breaks again the next time any contrib app ships a same-named template). Added `apps/accounts/tests/test_registration_templates.py` as the permanent regression (asserts `data-theme` is present and "Django site admin" is absent for password-reset/logout pages) and regenerated `static/css/app.css`/`.build-manifest.sha256` via `make css` since the move changed the Tailwind source-scan file set.
- [p01-e2e] Did not add an e2e-level Playwright case for the registration-template-shadowing fix (`e2e/plans/auth.plan.yaml`'s `password-reset-page-not-admin-branded` is `deferred_not_authored`, not authored) — why: `config.settings.e2e` runs with `DEBUG=False`, which makes Django wrap its template loader in `cached.Loader`; the e2e surface for this run was started by the orchestrator via `make e2e-up` *before* this fix, so its long-lived process still has the pre-fix admin template cached for its lifetime, and this step is instructed not to restart the surface. Verified stale, not assumed: `curl http://127.0.0.1:8100/accounts/password_reset/` after the template move still returned `Django site admin`, while a fresh pytest process (new interpreter, `apps/accounts/tests/test_registration_templates.py`) resolves the moved templates correctly and passes — alternatives: restart the surface myself (out of scope per this step's instructions).
- [p01-e2e] Verified all four pre-existing e2e specs (`test_login`, `test_theme`, `test_language`, `test_duration_format`) pass unchanged against the running surface, and manually probed (via ad-hoc Playwright/curl, not committed) the invalid-preference-value 400 fragment, the open-redirect guard on `next=`, and anonymous access to `/jsi18n/` — all matched the spec/plan's intended behaviour, so no new case was authored for them.

- [orchestrator/commit] kept `tests/test_logging.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.

## p02-plan

- [p02-plan/decision] `github_id` is a `CharField(max_length=100)` holding the GraphQL node ID, not an integer, and is unique only on `Organization`, `Repository`, `PullRequest`, `Commit`, `Review`, `ReviewComment`; `Identity.github_id` is nullable and non-unique — why: GitHub's GraphQL v4 returns opaque base64 node IDs, and a `git_email` identity has no node ID at all, so its real contract is `(kind, value)` — alternatives: `BigIntegerField` with the REST databaseId (would need a second REST call per entity), unique on every model (breaks email identities).
- [p02-plan/decision] `metrics.DailyRollup` carries a **second, partial** uniqueness constraint `UniqueConstraint(date, scope_type, cohort, metric_key, condition=Q(scope_id__isnull=True))` beside the spec §4.6 constraint — why: spec §4.6 makes `scope_id` nullable for the global scope, and SQL treats two NULLs as distinct, so the documented constraint alone would accept two global rows for the same day/metric/cohort and `compute()` would double-count; partial unique indexes work on both SQLite and PostgreSQL so ADR 0001's portability rule holds — alternatives: `scope_id` as a non-null integer with `0` meaning global (a magic value the ORM cannot type), `scope_id` as a CharField defaulting to `""` (changes the spec's field type), enforcing it in `metrics.services` only (a bug in a later phase writes duplicates directly).
- [p02-plan/decision] `catalog.Project.color` is a `CharField` whose choices are the eight chart **token names** from `static/css/tokens.css` (`series-1`…`series-8`, default `series-1`), rendered as `var(--{{ project.color }})`, with a test asserting each choice exists as a `--<name>:` declaration — why: spec §4.1 asks for a `color` field, but a hex value in `apps/**/*.py` fails `tests/test_no_hardcoded_colors.py` and would read identically in light and dark mode — alternatives: a free hex field plus an exclusion in the colour-literal test (weakens a gate to fit a field), an integer palette index (nothing ties it to the token file).
- [p02-plan/decision] `ai_detection.AISignal` gets **no** uniqueness constraint — why: spec §4.3 documents none, and one rule can legitimately match two commits of the same PR, so any constraint would have to encode `evidence` and would then differ between runs; re-running detection deletes a PR's signals and rewrites them, which is the phase-5 contract — alternatives: `UniqueConstraint(pull_request, commit, rule)` (rejects legitimate multi-match cases and behaves oddly with the nullable `commit`), including `evidence` in the key (makes every re-run a new row).
- [p02-plan/decision] `policy` ships two migrations: `0001_initial` (`AIPolicy`, `SensitivePathRule`, no FK to `activity`) and `0002_policyviolation` after `activity.0001` — why: the FK graph is genuinely mutual (`activity.PRFile.matched_sensitive_rule` → `policy.SensitivePathRule`, `policy.PolicyViolation.pull_request` → `activity.PullRequest`), and splitting the app's own migrations is the standard resolution — alternatives: drop `PRFile.matched_sensitive_rule` to a plain char column (a dangling rule id would silently mis-label a sensitive path), put `PolicyViolation` in `activity` (breaks the app boundary in ARCHITECTURE.md).
- [p02-plan/decision] `policy.services.current_policy(at=None)` resolves the "singleton" `AIPolicy` (newest row with `effective_from <= at`), and it lives in `services.py`, not `selectors.py` — why: `CLAUDE.md` requires every function in a `selectors.py` to start from `scope_for_user(user)`, and `AIPolicy` is global, unscoped data; putting it in `services.py` keeps the selector rule absolute instead of carving an exception into it — alternatives: a `selectors.py` function with a documented exemption (erodes the one authorization choke point), a model `classmethod` (rules in models, against the conventions).
- [p02-plan/decision] `policy.services.details_hash(params)` (sha256 over `json.dumps(params, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), default=str)`) ships in this phase together with the `(pull_request, rule_code, details_hash)` constraint, even though no evaluator exists yet — why: the constraint is inert without a canonical hashing rule — an unsorted `json.dumps` would produce a different hash for the same violation on a re-run and defeat risk #6's entire mitigation — alternatives: leave the hash to phase 6 (the constraint would ship untested and unproven), hash the repr of the dict (ordering-dependent for the same reason).
- [p02-plan/decision] `normalize_identity_value(kind, value)` (strip + casefold) lives in a new pure module `apps/catalog/normalize.py` and is called from `Identity.save()` — why: GitHub logins and email addresses are case-insensitive, so without normalisation the `(kind, value)` constraint would let `Foo` and `foo` become two people (risk #1); the function cannot live in `catalog/services.py` because that module imports models while `models.py` needs the normaliser, so a separate model-free module keeps the imports acyclic; calling it from `save()` rather than from the sync service makes it one choke point instead of one per writer — alternatives: normalise in the sync/identity service only (any other writer bypasses it), a database-level collation (not portable between SQLite and PostgreSQL per ADR 0001).
- [p02-plan/decision] `ReadOnlyAdminMixin` lives in a new `config/admin.py`, and read-only admins therefore answer 200 on the changelist (superusers hold the view permission) and 403 on the add view — why: `config` already owns the cross-cutting pieces (`logging_filters.py`, `security.py`, `db.py`), and a shared app for one mixin would cost an `INSTALLED_APPS` entry for nothing; the 200/403 split is exactly what the phase's acceptance criterion describes — alternatives: a new `apps/common` app, duplicating the mixin in eight `admin.py` files.
- [p02-plan/decision] `GitHubConnectionAdmin` sets `has_add_permission = False` in addition to excluding `token_encrypted`/`private_key_encrypted` — why: a connection cannot be created meaningfully without a token, and the token entry path with its encryption lives in the app's own form in the next phase (ADR 0004); an admin add form with the token excluded would silently create an unusable row — alternatives: allow adding with a plaintext token field in the admin (directly violates ADR 0004), allow adding a token-less row (creates broken connections).
- [p02-plan/decision] `AppSetting` values are stored in a `JSONField` with a `value_type` discriminator, and the defaults are a frozen `SETTING_DEFS` registry in `apps/catalog/setting_defs.py` seeded by data migration `catalog/0002_app_setting_defaults.py` through the shared, idempotent `seed_app_settings(model)` helper; `get_setting` raises `UnknownSettingError` for an unregistered key but falls back to the code default for a registered key whose row is missing — why: ARCHITECTURE.md asks for "typed rows, editable in the UI, with defaults in code"; a typo must fail loudly while a half-applied database must still render a dashboard; passing the model class into the helper means the historical model in the migration and the real model in tests run the same code — alternatives: one column per type (`int_value`, `str_value`, …), a `TextField` plus per-key parsing at call sites, defaults only in the migration (the code could then no longer answer for a missing row).
- [p02-plan/decision] `DEFAULT_THEME` and `DEFAULT_UI_LANGUAGE` exist as `AppSetting` rows even though `UserPreference` already encodes both as field defaults, with a test asserting the two agree — why: spec §15 names both as defaults and the settings UI has to expose them, but two sources of the same truth diverge silently — the test is what makes the duplication safe — alternatives: only the model defaults (the operator cannot change them, contradicting §15), only the AppSetting rows (a `UserPreference` row created before the settings are read would need a DB query in a field default).
- [p02-plan/verification] `DATABASE_URL` really overrides the default database path, so this phase's "migrate from zero" check is executable: `config/settings/base.py:78` builds `DATABASES["default"]` from `env.db_url("DATABASE_URL", default=…)` and `environ.Env.read_env` does not overwrite an already-set environment variable, so `DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/pr-radar-zero.sqlite3 uv run python manage.py migrate --noinput` migrates a throwaway file. `tests/test_urls_login.py::_named_urls` skips the `admin` namespace (`config/urls.py` mounts `admin.site.urls`), so registering ~20 new models adds no URL to the deny-by-default parametrization and cannot break it.


## p02-implement

- [p02-implement/decision] `tests/test_admin.py`'s "every model is registered and classified" check compares Django's live `admin.site._registry` against two hand-maintained sets (`READ_ONLY_MODELS`, `EDITABLE_MODELS`) hardcoded in the test file itself, rather than walking `django.apps.apps.get_models()` the way `tests/test_factories.py::_discover_factories` walks a discovered per-app factory registry — why: `ReadOnlyAdminMixin` and per-app `admin.py` registration have no code-level "classification" attribute to discover, so there was nothing to walk; the test only proves the registry and the two sets agree with each other, not that every concrete project model is in one of them — alternatives: add a class attribute (e.g. `admin_classification = "read_only"|"editable"`) to every `ModelAdmin` and derive the sets from that plus `apps.get_models()` (would make the check truly deny-by-default, at the cost of one more attribute per admin class), leave the gap and rely on `test_installed_apps.py`/`test_factories.py` to catch a forgotten model some other way (they do not check admin registration at all).
- [p02-implement/decision] `GitHubConnectionAdmin` is classified under `READ_ONLY_MODELS` in `tests/test_admin.py` (because its add view answers 403) but does **not** inherit `config.admin.ReadOnlyAdminMixin` — it is a plain `admin.ModelAdmin` with `exclude`, `readonly_fields` on two fields only, and `has_add_permission` overridden by itself; its change form stays fully editable for `status`, `is_active`, `name`, etc. `tests/test_admin.py::test_readonly_admin_covers_every_editable_field` explicitly excludes `GitHubConnection` from the "every field is read-only" assertion with a comment recording why — why: an operator must be able to toggle `is_active` or correct `owner_login` from the admin without re-running the (not-yet-built) connection-creation flow, so full read-only would block a legitimate edit the plan's own model design assumes is possible — alternatives: apply `ReadOnlyAdminMixin` too and accept that no field is editable post-creation (blocks correcting a bad `owner_login`), introduce a third admin classification bucket in the test (`READ_ONLY_MODELS`/`EDITABLE_MODELS`/`PARTIALLY_EDITABLE_MODELS`) instead of a documented exception to the second one.
- [p02-implement/decision] `apps/catalog/models.py::AppSetting.clean()` imports the underscore-prefixed `apps.catalog.services._validate_type` inside the method body (a function-local import), rather than duplicating the type check in `models.py` or moving it to a third module — why: `catalog/services.py` already imports `AppSetting` from `models.py` at module scope, so a module-level import the other way would be circular; a local import inside `clean()` breaks the cycle while still giving `AppSetting.clean()` and `catalog.services.set_setting()` exactly one type-validation rule to agree on — alternatives: duplicate the `value_type` → Python-type check directly in `models.py` (two rules to keep in sync, the exact failure mode `set_setting`/`clean` are supposed to prevent), move `_validate_type` to a third, model-free module symmetric with `normalize.py` (more indirection for one four-line function).
- [p02-implement/decision] `catalog.services._validate_type` special-cases `bool`: a value typed `int`/`float` is rejected if it is actually a Python `bool`, even though `bool` is an `int` subclass and would otherwise pass `isinstance(value, int)` — why: `AppSetting.value` is a `JSONField`, so `True`/`False` deserialize as Python `bool`, and without the guard `set_setting("BACKFILL_DAYS", True)` would silently succeed and store a boolean where an operator expects an integer day count — alternatives: rely on plain `isinstance` (accepts `True`/`False` for any int-typed setting), coerce `bool` to `int` on write (hides the operator's likely mistake instead of rejecting it).
- [p02-implement/decision] The typed accessors `catalog.services.get_list()` / `get_dict()` narrow `get_setting()`'s `object`-typed return with a bare `assert isinstance(value, list)` / `assert isinstance(value, dict)` rather than raising a catchable exception — why: this is the cheapest way to give mypy the narrowing it needs for the declared `list[Any]` / `dict[str, Any]` return types without introducing a new exception type for a case `set_setting`'s own validation should already prevent — alternatives: raise `TypeError`/`ValidationError` on mismatch (more defensive, catches a row corrupted by a direct DB write instead of `set_setting`, not currently tested either way), `typing.cast` with no runtime check at all (gives mypy the same narrowing with zero safety net).

- [p02-implement/decision] Fixed the gap the previous bullet recorded: `tests/test_admin.py::test_every_model_is_registered_and_classified` now also asserts `_project_models()` (a `django.apps.apps.get_models()` walk filtered to `apps.*`, mirroring `tests/test_factories.py::_project_models`) has no member outside `_project_registered_models()` — why: the original check only proved the two hardcoded sets agreed with the live registry, so a concrete model added later and never registered at all would pass silently, defeating T14's stated "deny-by-default walk, same shape as T13" requirement — alternatives: the discarded per-`ModelAdmin` classification attribute (more invasive for the same guarantee), leaving the gap since no phase-2 model currently triggers it.


## p02-review_fix1


- [p02-review_fix1/fix] Added `pull_request__repository` (in addition to the existing FK) to `list_select_related` on `PolicyViolationAdmin`, `ChurnResultAdmin`, `AISignalAdmin`, `PullRequestCommitAdmin`, `PRFileAdmin`, `ReviewAdmin`, `ReviewCommentAdmin`, `CheckStatusAdmin` — why: review r1 major finding — `PullRequest.__str__` dereferences `.repository`, so rendering `pull_request` in `list_display` without the nested select was one extra query per row (measured 3 rows = 9 queries, 12 rows = 18) — alternatives: none; this is the documented Django fix for the exact shape of N+1.
- [p02-review_fix1/fix] Added `tests/test_admin.py::test_fk_heavy_changelist_query_count_does_not_grow_with_rows`, parametrized over the eight FK-heavy factories, asserting the changelist's query count at 3 rows equals its query count at 12 rows — why: review r1 noted the empty-DB blind spot in the existing changelist tests; CLAUDE.md requires `assertNumQueries`-shaped tests on list views — alternatives: none; this closes the blind spot the review found.
- [p02-review_fix1/fix] `catalog.services.seed_app_settings()` and `set_setting()` no longer write `str(setting_def.description)` into `AppSetting.description`; the column is now left blank at seed and untouched on write. `AppSettingAdmin.list_display` replaced the raw `description` field with a `registry_description` display method that reads the lazy `gettext_lazy` description straight from `SETTING_DEFS` at render time — why: review r1 major finding — persisting a rendered English sentence in a data migration violates CLAUDE.md's "never store a rendered English message" and permanently stranded the 30 Ukrainian translations already written for these strings — alternatives: keep the column and add a second, Ukrainian-only migration later (the exact "expensive to undo" case the review warned about); drop the `description` column outright (kept it, renamed nothing, in case a future phase wants a genuinely operator-owned free-text note under a different name).
- [p02-review_fix1/fix] `catalog/migrations/0002_app_setting_defaults.py` no longer imports `apps.catalog.services.seed_app_settings`; it inlines the same idempotent get-or-create loop against `apps.catalog.setting_defs.SETTING_DEFS` (model-free) directly. The reverse of the seed `RunPython` is now `migrations.RunPython.noop` instead of `AppSetting.objects.all().delete()` — why: review r1 minor findings — importing `services.py` pinned a historical migration to live application code (`services.py` imports the current `AppSetting` model at module scope, so a future rename would break `migrate` from zero at import time); the unscoped delete on reverse discarded operator-tuned settings and any non-registry row, and was redundant besides — the same migration's `CreateModel` already drops the table on reverse — alternatives: scope the delete to `SETTING_DEFS` keys instead of `noop` (more code for the same zero-rows-remain outcome, since `CreateModel` reverses first); keep the `services` import and accept the coupling (the exact fragility the review flagged).
- [p02-review_fix1/fix] `catalog.services.get_setting()` now validates the stored value against `setting_def.value_type` (via the existing `_validate_type`) before returning it, logging a warning and falling back to the code default when the row is corrupt. `AppSettingAdmin.readonly_fields` now covers `key` and `value_type` — why: review r1 minor finding — these are code-side contracts owned by `SETTING_DEFS`, not operator data; leaving them editable let an operator silently orphan a row (rename `key`) or create a `(value_type, value)` pair `clean()` would reject on write but `get_setting()` previously trusted on read — alternatives: enforce only on write (already done via `clean()`/`set_setting()`, but a row can be corrupted by a direct DB edit or a future bulk writer, which is exactly what the review's read-side gap was about).
- [p02-review_fix1/fix] `catalog.Identity` gained a `clean()` method that also calls `normalize_identity_value()`, alongside the existing call in `save()` — why: review r1 minor finding — `save()` is the only normalisation choke point today, but `bulk_create`/`bulk_update`/`QuerySet.update` (the obvious shape for phase 3's batch identity writes) bypass it entirely; `clean()` at least makes `full_clean()`-validated paths safe and gives a documented, tested hook — alternatives: normalise in a `pre_save` signal (fires on `save()` only, same gap for bulk writers); enforce a DB-level `CHECK` that value is already casefolded (SQLite/Postgres portability cost for one string invariant already covered by a Python helper).
- [p02-review_fix1/rejected] Left `tests/test_logging.py` staged for this phase's commit rather than unstaging it, and did not add a separate decision bullet beyond this one — why: review r1's own text confirms the six literals are synthetic token-shape fixtures for `SecretMaskingFilter`/`mask_secrets`, not real secrets, and the file only exercises `config/logging_filters.py` + `config/security.py`, both phase-1 modules already in the repo; re-litigating "phase 1 vs phase 2 owns this file" has no product effect either way, so the practical answer is to stop leaving it untracked (which risks it being silently lost) and record here that the phase-1 hold is released — alternatives: unstage and leave untracked per the plan's literal wording (defers the same non-decision to a still-later phase for no benefit).


## p02-review_fix2

- [p02-review_fix2/decision] Added `raw` to `activity.Commit`, `activity.Review`, `activity.ReviewComment` (all three carry a unique GitHub `github_id`, matching `Organization`/`Repository`/`PullRequest`) but not to `activity.PRFile` or `activity.CheckStatus` — why: those two have no `github_id` of their own in this schema (per PLAN.md's own field table) because they are not independent GraphQL nodes fetched by id, they are rows synthesized from a paginated connection on the owning PR/commit; there is nothing for `STORE_RAW_PAYLOADS` to attach that is not already reachable from the parent PR's `raw` payload — alternatives: add `raw` literally everywhere per PLAN.md's wording (rejected: would store the same GraphQL fragment twice per file/check with no debugging benefit over the parent's payload); narrow PLAN.md's prose to say "every GitHub-owned model with its own node id" instead of "every GitHub-owned model" (deferred — this bullet is the narrowing).
- [p02-review_fix2/fix] Fixed `github_sync.SyncRun.started_at` (`auto_now_add=True` → `default=timezone.now`) so phase 3's sync service can pass an explicit start time; fixed `accounts/0004_admin_permissions.py` to look up the `catalog.manage_settings` permission with `.filter(...).first()` and no-op the grant/revoke when absent instead of `.get()` raising `DoesNotExist` from zero — why: review r2 majors/minors; both `apps/activity/migrations/0001_initial.py` and `apps/github_sync/migrations/0001_initial.py` were hand-edited in place (not a new migration) since neither had shipped to `main` yet — alternatives: a follow-up migration (rejected: this phase's migrations are still unreleased, so squashing avoids a churny two-migration history for a same-phase fix).


- [orchestrator/commit] kept `tests/test_logging.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.

## p03-plan


- [p03-plan/decision] The "one sync at a time" lock is a new `github_sync.SyncLock` model (unique `name`, `acquired_at`, `sync_run` FK) acquired by `objects.create(name="global")` — an `IntegrityError` means another run holds it — released in a `finally`, and stolen when `acquired_at` is older than the new `SYNC_LOCK_STALE_MINUTES` (360) setting — why: ADR 0003 says "a lock row in the database" without naming a mechanism, and `select_for_update()` is silently a no-op on SQLite (ADR 0001 keeps us ORM-only and portable), so a uniqueness constraint is the portable equivalent; a killed worker otherwise leaves the row and wedges the tool forever, which no operator could fix from the UI — alternatives: `select_for_update` on a fixed row (no-op on SQLite, so the second run would proceed), a file lock in `DATA_DIR` (invisible to the UI and useless once the tool moves to a server, §13), reusing `SyncRun(status=running)` as the lock (a crashed run leaves a permanently "running" row and the same staleness problem without a dedicated timestamp).
- [p03-plan/decision] Eight new `AppSetting` keys in the `sync` group seeded by `catalog/0003_sync_settings.py`: `SYNC_OVERLAP_MINUTES=60`, `SYNC_PR_PAGE_SIZE=50`, `SYNC_NESTED_PAGE_SIZE=100`, `RATE_LIMIT_MIN_REMAINING=200`, `SYNC_MAX_RETRIES=5`, `SYNC_RETRY_MAX_SECONDS=60`, `SYNC_LOCK_STALE_MINUTES=360`, `TOKEN_EXPIRY_WARNING_DAYS=14`, `CONNECTION_RECHECK_MIN_MINUTES=60` — why: spec §5.3/§5.4 and ARCHITECTURE.md name every one of these numbers as behaviour the operator observes (and §5.1 makes the 14-day banner and the once-per-hour re-check rules), but phase 2's `SETTING_DEFS` shipped none of them, so without rows they would become magic constants scattered across `client.py` and `services.py` — alternatives: module-level constants in `github_sync` (untunable without a release, and invisible to the settings UI phase 7 builds), Django settings in `base.py` (would need an `.env` edit and a restart for a number an operator legitimately tunes per organisation).
- [p03-plan/decision] `GITHUB_API_BASE_URL` (default `https://api.github.com`) and the derived `GITHUB_GRAPHQL_URL` are **Django settings** read from `.env`, not `AppSetting` rows — why: they are deployment wiring rather than operator policy, and they are what `respx` routes bind to, so a database row could change them under a running test and break the "no unmocked outbound request" guarantee; GHES support later is then one env var — alternatives: `AppSetting` rows (mutable at runtime under the test guard, and meaningless to tune per-day), hardcoding the hostname (no GHES path at all, and nothing for respx to pin explicitly).
- [p03-plan/decision] Connections, repositories and sync runs are read through `services.py`, not a `selectors.py` — why: this follows the p02 precedent set for `policy.services.current_policy()`; CLAUDE.md requires every function in a `selectors.py` to start from `scope_for_user(user)`, and these three are global, admin-scoped configuration rather than per-project data, so keeping them out of `selectors.py` leaves the authorization rule absolute instead of carving a documented exception into the one choke point — alternatives: a `selectors.py` with an exemption comment (erodes the rule the moment a second exception appears), routing them through `scope_for_user()` anyway (a `ScopeFilter` that means nothing for a table with no project FK).
- [p03-plan/decision] `github_sync.pipeline.process_pull_request(pull_request_id)` ships in this phase as a defined, imported, no-op function called via `transaction.on_commit`, with a test asserting it runs exactly once per synced PR — why: spec §5.3 step 4 and ADR 0003 make the hook part of the sync contract, and shipping the call site plus its once-per-PR test now means phases 4–6 add stages without reopening the orchestrator; an empty function with a passing test is a smaller lie than an orchestrator that later grows a call site nobody tested — alternatives: add the hook in phase 4 (the orchestrator and its transaction-boundary tests would be rewritten then), call the four stages directly from `sync_repository` (couples the sync loop to four apps that do not exist yet).
- [p03-plan/decision] The single decrypt accessor is `apps/connections/services.py::plaintext_token(connection)`, with the raw `MultiFernet` work in a model-free `apps/connections/crypto.py`, and a grep test asserting `decrypt_token(` appears nowhere in `apps/` outside those two modules and their tests; `PATAuth` overrides `__repr__`/`__str__` to print only `connection_id` — why: ADR 0004 requires one accessor and plaintext only in a local variable, but "one accessor" is a convention until something enforces it, and the realistic leak path is not a `print()` — it is a token sitting in a dataclass's default `repr` that a traceback or a test failure renders — alternatives: a property on the model (invites `connection.token` in a template or an f-string), rely on review discipline alone (risk #2 is the one whose damage is not undoable).
- [p03-plan/decision] `errors.require(payload, path)` raises `GitHubSchemaError` carrying only the dotted JSON path, and `errors.optional(payload, path)` returns `None` (never `0`); no error message ever embeds the payload — why: the acceptance criterion asks the error to name the JSON path, and a GitHub response body can legitimately contain a token-shaped string (e.g. in a PR body a developer pasted), so quoting the payload into an exception would route untrusted text straight into `SyncRun.error_log` and defeat the leak test — alternatives: include a truncated payload for debuggability (a token fragment fits in any truncation), a generic `KeyError` (the criterion explicitly requires the path).
- [p03-plan/decision] Repository discovery fetches through GraphQL (`repositoryOwner(login:).repositories` when `owner_login` is set, `viewer.repositories` otherwise) and does **not** persist the fetched list; only the repositories an admin ticks become rows — why: a fine-grained PAT is issued per resource owner so `owner_login` is the natural root, classic PATs have no single owner and need `viewer`, and persisting the full list would create `Repository` rows the operator never chose and that `PROTECT` then makes awkward to remove — alternatives: REST `/user/repos` (a second pagination style and a second shape to fixture for no gain), persist everything with an `is_active` flag (spec §5.2 treats adding a repository as a deliberate act, and the repositories list would fill with client repos nobody syncs).
- [p03-plan/decision] `GitHubClient` takes an injected `sleep` callable, and every rate-limit and backoff test asserts the *durations requested* rather than waiting — why: the phase must test a wait-until-`resetAt` pause and a five-attempt exponential backoff capped at 60 s; real sleeps would add minutes to `uv run pytest -q` and make the suite flaky on a loaded machine — alternatives: `freezegun` alone (it does not intercept `time.sleep`), `monkeypatch.setattr(time, "sleep")` per test (the same thing without a declared seam, and it would silently stop working if the client switched to `httpx`'s own retry timing).

## p03-implement

- [p03-implement/fix] Fixed root `conftest.py`'s `_no_live_http_requests` fixture: it called `with respx.mock(assert_all_mocked=True, assert_all_called=False):`, but `MockRouter.__call__` with keyword arguments always branches to a brand-new, unregistered `MockRouter` instance (`respx/router.py`'s `__call__`: "Parentheses used, branch out to new nested instance") — while module-level `respx.get()/post()/...` always add routes to the separate singleton `respx.mock` (`respx/api.py`). The fixture's nested instance was the one patched into `Mocker.routers` and consulted on each request; any route a test registered via `respx.post(...)` landed on the unconsulted singleton, so every first real respx-based test in this repo (`apps/github_sync/tests/test_client.py`) failed with `AllMockedAssertionError` even though the route existed. Fixed by setting `respx.mock._assert_all_mocked`/`_assert_all_called` directly on the singleton and entering it bare (`with respx.mock:`), which per respx's own docstring uses global-singleton semantics instead of branching — why: this bug was latent since phase 0/1 (`tests/test_http_guard.py` only asserted the *unmocked* path, which happens to work under either router since an unregistered router's requests still hit `AllMockedAssertionError` — the bug only manifests once a test tries to register and match a route) and would have silently broken every future respx test in every later phase — alternatives: have each test capture and use the fixture's nested router explicitly instead of the module-level `respx.get/post` API (bigger call-site change across all future GitHub-sync tests for the same fix), monkeypatch `respx.get/post/...` to bind to the nested instance (more indirection to reach the same singleton-based fix).
- [p03-implement/handoff] Session 1 stopped after T7 (client) with T1–T7 done and gated green (`ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy`, `makemigrations --check --dry-run`, `manage.py check`, `make css`, full `pytest -q` all pass on HEAD of this working tree). T8 (connection verification) was started but not finished: `apps/github_sync/queries.py` gained a `totalCount` field on both `REPOSITORIES_BY_OWNER_QUERY` and `VIEWER_REPOSITORIES_QUERY` (for the planned `REPOS_VISIBLE` check's count) — untested but inert until `connections/check_codes.py` and `services.verify_connection()` exist to use it. Next session should: write `check_codes.py` (code → lazy message + hint, per PLAN.md §4's example JSON), implement `verify_connection()` in `apps/connections/services.py` (REST `/user` for `token_login`/scopes/expiry header, the `RATE_LIMIT_QUERY` already in `queries.py`, and the owner/viewer repo query for `REPOS_VISIBLE`), decide a concrete `PERM_CONTENTS` check (a REST `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/` root listing against a small new fixture is the simplest testable option — none exists yet), then continue in PLAN.md order from T8 through T24. `rest_user.json` and `rate_limit.json` fixtures (T5) are already in place for T8's use.
- [p03-implement/handoff] Session 2 finished T8, T13, T14, T15, T16 (skipped ahead of T9–T12 per PLAN.md's own risk note: "if the phase is cut short, the UI tasks are the ones a follow-up absorbs"), leaving T9–T12 and T17–T24 unchecked. New/changed this session: `apps/connections/check_codes.py`, `apps/connections/services.py::verify_connection()` (+ `_parse_token_expiration_header`), `apps/connections/tests/test_verify.py`, two new fixtures (`viewer_repositories.json`, `totalCount` added to both `discovery_repos_page*.json`, both previously unused by any test); `apps/github_sync/mappers.py` + `test_mappers.py`; `apps/github_sync/upserts.py` + `test_upserts.py`; `apps/github_sync/pipeline.py` + `test_pipeline.py`; `apps/github_sync/services.py` (`run_sync`, `sync_repository`, `_watermark`, `SyncAlreadyRunning`) + `test_sync.py`; `apps/github_sync/models.py::SyncLock` + `migrations/0002_synclock.py` + admin registration + `SyncLockFactory`; `tests/test_admin.py` and `tests/test_factories.py` updated for `SyncLock`. Full `pytest -q` is green except the pre-existing, expected `tests/test_css_tokens.py::test_app_css_is_not_stale` (stale because `.py` sources changed since the last `make css`; deferred to T22 by design, not a regression). `ruff`/`mypy`/`makemigrations --check`/`manage.py check` all pass on HEAD of this tree.
- [p03-implement/decision] `run_sync`'s connection loop is a plain sequential `for connection, repos in groupby(...)` — it fully drains one connection's repositories (including any rate-limit wait) before moving to the next, rather than the round-robin "a waiting connection yields to the others" scheduler PLAN.md's design section describes. This is a known gap, not an oversight: implementing real yielding needs a per-connection work-queue scheduler (interleave repos across connections, skip a connection whose budget says wait, only block if *every* remaining connection is waiting) which was sketched but not written or tested before this session's context ran out — alternatives considered: threads/async (bigger change, against the sync, single-process design elsewhere in this codebase), leaving it sequential and documenting the gap (chosen, so a wrong claim of compliance doesn't ship silently).
- [p03-implement/handoff] Next session should resume at **T17** in `apps/github_sync/services.py`: replace the sequential per-connection loop (services.py, the block starting `for connection, repos_iter in groupby(repositories, ...)` inside `run_sync`, roughly lines 218–273) with a round-robin scheduler across per-connection work queues, so a connection whose `RateBudget.should_wait(min_remaining)` is true is skipped for that round instead of blocking, and only actually sleeps when *every* remaining connection is waiting (sleep on whichever has the soonest `seconds_until_reset()`). Then write `apps/github_sync/tests/test_sync_isolation.py` per PLAN.md T17: two connections, a 401 on connection A quarantines only A (marks it `invalid`, skips A's remaining repos) while B keeps syncing and writes rows; same shape for 403+SSO → `degraded`; a connection whose budget is exhausted yields to the other instead of stalling the run (assert via call ordering / `stats_by_connection[...]["rate_limit_waits"]`, not real sleep — inject a controllable `sleep` if the scheduler needs one). After T17, continue in PLAN.md order: T18 (`manage.py sync`), T19 (huey task + Sync page — needs `apps/github_sync/tasks.py`, `views.py`, `urls.py`, templates, nav entry), T20 (the token-leak test, `tests/test_token_leak.py`, per spec §12 — DB introspection walk + log file + `error_log` + rendered pages + raw SQLite grep), then the UI tasks this session skipped: T9 (connections UI: forms/views/urls/templates, `apps/connections/urls.py` and `views.py` do not exist yet), T10 (expiry banner context processor), T11 (`bootstrap_connection` management command — `apps/connections/management/` directory exists but check what, if anything, is in it), T12 (discovery + rebinding view, `catalog.services` additions — the `REPOSITORIES_BY_OWNER_QUERY`/`VIEWER_REPOSITORIES_QUERY` fixtures and `totalCount` field are ready for this). Finish with T21 (docs, incl. `docs/GITHUB_CONNECTIONS.md` documenting every `check_codes.py` code), T22 (`make messages` + `make css` — **required before the final gate**, since several sessions' worth of `.py`/template changes have left `static/css/app.css` and `.build-manifest.sha256` stale; do this only once, near the end, not after every task), T23 (e2e), T24 (gate). Nothing in the working tree is broken or half-edited; every checked-off task's tests pass in isolation and together.
- [p03-implement/decision] `connections.context_processors.connection_alerts` gates on `request.user.is_staff` before calling `has_perm("catalog.manage_settings")`, and costs two queries (the uncached `TOKEN_EXPIRY_WARNING_DAYS` `AppSetting` lookup + the connections query) for an admin, zero for a lead — why: `is_staff` is already loaded on `request.user` by `AuthenticationMiddleware` (no query) and is true only for the `admin` group in this app, so it is a free short-circuit before touching the database at all; `has_perm` on a superuser is also free (`PermissionsMixin` short-circuits it), so nothing beyond the two fixed queries runs, and neither scales with the number of connections. PLAN.md's design section says "one query" for this processor; `apps.catalog.services.get_int()` is uncached everywhere else in the codebase (`client.py`, `services.py` call it per-request too), so getting to a literal single query would mean caching `AppSetting` reads specifically here, which is a bigger, cross-cutting change out of this task's scope — alternatives: cache `AppSetting` process-wide (changes the read-your-writes guarantee the settings UI phase 7 will need, decided there instead), hardcode `TOKEN_EXPIRY_WARNING_DAYS` in this processor only (defeats the point of it being operator-tunable).
- [p03-implement/handoff] Session 3 finished T9, T10, T17, T18, T19 (in that order, T17 first since T11's `bootstrap_connection` and T12's discovery didn't block it) and left T11, T12, T20, T21, T22, T23, T24 unchecked. New/changed this session: `apps/github_sync/services.py::run_sync` rewritten as a round-robin scheduler across per-connection work queues (a connection whose `RateBudget.should_wait()` is true yields to others for a round; forced through only when every remaining connection is waiting) + `apps/github_sync/tests/test_sync_isolation.py` (T17); `apps/github_sync/management/commands/sync.py` + `apps/github_sync/tests/test_command_sync.py` (T18); `apps/github_sync/tasks.py`, `views.py`, `urls.py`, `templates/github_sync/{sync.html,partials/{content,status,runs}.html}` + `apps/github_sync/tests/test_views.py` (T19, the Sync page); `apps/connections/forms.py`, `views.py`, `urls.py`, `templates/connections/{list,form}.html` + `partials/{list_content,row,form}.html`, `apps/connections/templatetags/connection_extras.py` (renders `last_check_result` codes at read time) + `apps/connections/tests/test_views.py` (T9, the connections UI — there is no separate "detail" URL: `/settings/connections/<pk>/edit/` doubles as detail view, showing `last_check_result` alongside the edit form); `apps/connections/context_processors.py` + `templates/partials/connection_alerts.html`, wired into `TEMPLATES` and `base.html` (T10, the expiry/invalid banner).
