New shared seam: `config/htmx.py::is_htmx()` — the one `HX-Request` predicate every fragment-vs-full-page view in `connections` and `github_sync` now uses (ADR 0006); connections and sync URLs wired into `config/urls.py`; `nav.html` gained "Sync" and, for `catalog.manage_settings` holders, "Connections" links. `tests/test_urls_login.py::SAMPLE_ARGS` gained `"pk": 1`, and its `_reverse()` helper was rewritten to try every subset of `SAMPLE_ARGS` (not just "all of them" or "one at a time") since `password_reset_confirm` needs `uidb64`+`token` together while the new `pk`-only URLs need `pk` alone — a fixed-shape guess broke one or the other. Design per PLAN.md §5: if `GITHUB_TOKEN` env var is set and no `GitHubConnection` row exists, create `"Default (.env)"` with `kind=get_str("DEFAULT_CONNECTION_KIND")`, `set_token(...)`, leave `status=unverified`, make **no** GitHub call; if a connection already exists, log a warning naming `GITHUB_TOKEN` and exit 0; if the env var is absent, exit 0 saying so; `--verify` is an opt-in flag that calls `verify_connection(force=True)` — only ever exercised against `respx` in tests, never live). Then **T12** (discovery + rebinding — `catalog/services.py` needs `create_repositories_from_discovery()`/`rebind_repository()`, plus a discovery view/templates under `connections/` per PLAN.md's URL table `/settings/repositories/discover/` and `/settings/repositories/<pk>/rebind/`; the `REPOSITORIES_BY_OWNER_QUERY`/`VIEWER_REPOSITORIES_QUERY` fixtures with `totalCount` are ready from T8). Then **T21** (docs, including `docs/GITHUB_CONNECTIONS.md` documenting every `check_codes.py` code — `tests/test_docs.py::test_every_check_code_is_documented` doesn't exist yet either). **T22 last before the gate** (`make messages` + `make css` — required once, near the end, not after every task; this session added several new templates and Python files with translatable strings, all currently untranslated into Ukrainian, so `tests/test_translations.py` will need real `.po` entries, not just a rebuild). Then **T23** (e2e) and **T24** (the full gate — also sweep for the one pre-existing `ruff check` finding noted above). Nothing in the working tree is broken or half-edited; every checked-off task's tests pass in isolation and together; `manage.py check`, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` and the full test suite (minus the known-stale CSS manifest) are green on HEAD.
- [p03-implement/decision] T12's discovery GraphQL nodes carry `owner { login __typename }` with no `id`, but `Organization.github_id` is a required unique field and `Organization` rows are created only from discovery in this phase (sync never touches `Organization`) — so `REPOSITORIES_BY_OWNER_QUERY`/`VIEWER_REPOSITORIES_QUERY` in `apps/github_sync/queries.py` gained `owner { id login __typename }`, and the three fixtures that use those queries (`discovery_repos_page1.json`, `discovery_repos_page2.json`, `viewer_repositories.json`) gained a matching `owner.id`. `create_repositories_from_discovery()` keys `Organization.objects.update_or_create` on that id — why: without a real node id the alternative was keying on `login` alone, which would silently diverge from every other GitHub entity in this codebase (PR, Review, Commit, Repository are all `github_id`-keyed) and break if an org is ever renamed — alternatives: key `Organization` on `login` (matches nothing else, breaks on rename), skip `Organization.github_id` uniqueness for this path (weakens a constraint to route around a schema gap instead of fixing the query).
- [p03-implement/decision] Rebinding is not a dedicated confirm page; PLAN.md's URL table lists only `POST /settings/repositories/<pk>/rebind/` and no page to reach it from other than the discovery table itself. The "explicit confirm step" (design §6) is a required `confirm=1` checkbox rendered inline on each already-bound-elsewhere row of the discovery table; submitting without it is a no-op that re-renders the same table with an inline error — why: this phase explicitly puts repository/person pages out of scope (§ "Out of scope"), so there is nowhere else a rebind action could live except next to the discovery row that surfaces the conflict — alternatives: a separate `GET` confirm page (needs its own URL PLAN.md never named, and a repository detail page phase 3 deliberately doesn't build), a JS `confirm()` dialog (no server-side proof of confirmation, easier to trigger by accident from a script).
- [p03-implement/decision] Added `POST /settings/connections/<pk>/delete/` (`connections:delete`), not named in PLAN.md's URL table, because design §6's own sentence — "Deleting a connection with repositories is already refused by PROTECT — the UI turns the ProtectedError into a visible message" — presupposes a delete action exists somewhere, and T12's acceptance criterion ("deleting a connection with repositories renders the visible … message … and the connection survives") is untestable without one. Deleting an unused connection now succeeds and removes the row; no audit entry is written on that path (unlike create/update/deactivate) since nothing in this phase's design section asked for one and the row is gone regardless — alternatives: repurpose `deactivate` as the delete-attempt trigger (conflates two different operations — one reversible, one not — behind one button), leave the ProtectedError message unbuilt and mark the acceptance criterion unmet (contradicts the design section's own claim).
- [p03-implement/decision] `tests/test_token_leak.py` uses `@pytest.mark.django_db(transaction=True)` and dumps the SQLite database via `sqlite3.Connection.backup()` to prove the raw on-disk file criterion, rather than only walking rows through the Django ORM connection — why: pytest-django's default sqlite test database is `file:memorydb_default?mode=memory&cache=shared` (confirmed by probing `connection.settings_dict["NAME"]` in this session), so there is no on-disk file to grep by default, and a transaction-wrapped test (the default `@pytest.mark.django_db`) leaves writes uncommitted in a way a second `sqlite3` connection into the same shared-cache database cannot even read (`OperationalError: database table is locked`, confirmed by reproducing it) — `transaction=True` makes Django commit for real, after which `sqlite3.connect(name, uri=True)` attaches to the same shared in-memory database and `.backup()` serializes it to a real temp file exactly like `db.sqlite3` in production, satisfying "grep the on-disk SQLite file" literally rather than only in spirit — alternatives: point `DATABASES` at a `tmp_path` file via a settings override (bigger blast radius — would need to run before pytest-django's session-scoped db setup), skip the raw-file criterion and only walk the ORM connection (leaves the acceptance criterion's literal wording unmet after the encryption/masking code already passed a weaker check).
- [p03-implement/handoff] Session 4 finished T11 (`bootstrap_connection` + `apps/connections/tests/test_bootstrap.py`), T12 (discovery + rebinding: `apps/catalog/services.py::create_repositories_from_discovery()`/`rebind_repository()`, `apps/connections/views.py::repository_discover`/`repository_rebind`/`connection_delete` + `urls.py` + `templates/connections/{discovery.html,partials/discovery_content.html}` + a "Delete" action added to `partials/row.html`/`list_content.html` + `apps/connections/tests/test_discovery.py`, 9 tests), T20 (`tests/test_token_leak.py` — DB introspection walk + a real on-disk SQLite dump via `sqlite3.Connection.backup()` under `@pytest.mark.django_db(transaction=True)` + log file + `error_log` + three rendered pages), and T21 (`docs/GITHUB_CONNECTIONS.md`, `docs/user/connect-github.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` entry, `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`/`docs/SETUP.md` cross-links and the new sync `AppSetting` rows + `GITHUB_API_BASE_URL` documented, `tests/test_docs.py::test_every_check_code_is_documented`). Also: added `owner { id login __typename }` to `REPOSITORIES_BY_OWNER_QUERY`/`VIEWER_REPOSITORIES_QUERY` in `apps/github_sync/queries.py` (and the matching `owner.id` to the three fixtures that use them) — see the two `p03-implement/decision` entries just above this one for why. `git diff --stat` at handoff time: `apps/catalog/services.py`, `apps/catalog/setting_defs.py`* (session 3, untouched this session), `apps/connections/{views,urls}.py` + new `templates/connections/{discovery.html,partials/discovery_content.html}` + edited `partials/{row,list_content}.html`, `apps/connections/management/commands/bootstrap_connection.py` (new), `apps/connections/tests/{test_bootstrap,test_discovery}.py` (new), `apps/github_sync/queries.py`, `tests/fixtures/github/{discovery_repos_page1,discovery_repos_page2,viewer_repositories}.json`, `tests/test_docs.py` (new), `tests/test_token_leak.py` (new), `docs/GITHUB_CONNECTIONS.md` (new), `docs/user/connect-github.md` (new), `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`, `docs/SETUP.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `templates/partials/nav.html` (added a "Repositories" link next to "Connections"). Full `pytest -q` is green except the four failures explained below, which are all T22, not a regression: `tests/test_css_tokens.py::test_app_css_is_not_stale` (many sessions of template/`.py` changes since the last `make css`), and three in `tests/test_translations.py` (`test_mo_catalog_matches_committed_po_source`, `test_po_has_no_empty_or_fuzzy_entries`, `test_po_placeholders_match_between_msgid_and_msgstr`) because `make messages` was run this session (to size T22's remaining work) but no `uk` translation was written yet — every new msgid from T9/T10/T11/T12/T19/T20-era templates is sitting in `locale/uk/LC_MESSAGES/django.po` with an empty `msgstr`. `ruff check .`, `ruff format --check .`, `mypy`, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` and `manage.py check` are all clean on HEAD of this tree. Nothing is half-edited; every file compiles and every checked-off task's tests pass.
- [p03-implement/handoff] Next session resumes at **T22**: translate the 57 untranslated `django.po` entries (see the note inline in PLAN.md's T22 line for the exact command to list them and the exact steps — fill `msgstr`, `compilemessages`, `make css`, verify empty), check `locale/uk/LC_MESSAGES/djangojs.po` too (not audited this session — likely has few or no new strings since this phase added no new JS-side translated strings, but confirm), then write the render test T22 asks for (connections + sync pages in `uk` contain no canary English string — follow the existing pattern other apps use, if any, or add a minimal one: force `uk` via `Accept-Language`/session, GET the connections list, connection edit and sync pages, assert none of a short canary list like "GitHub connections"/"Sync now" appears). Then **T23** (e2e: `e2e/plans/connections.plan.yaml` + `e2e/web/test_connections.py`, extend `manage.py seed_e2e` with an `ok` connection, an expiring one, a repository and a finished `SyncRun` — no case may trigger a live sync). Then **T24** (the full gate: run every command in PLAN.md's "Verification" section including the from-zero migration check and `make e2e-up && pytest e2e -q; make e2e-down`; update `docs/PROGRESS.md`). `apps/catalog/setting_defs.py` and `apps/catalog/tests/test_app_settings.py` show as modified in `git status` from session 1 (T1) — that is expected, not this session's doing.
- [p03-implement/decision] T22 also fixed 34 pre-existing `#, fuzzy` entries in `locale/uk/LC_MESSAGES/django.po` beyond the 57 empty ones the previous session's `make messages` left — msgmerge had fuzzy-matched several new msgids (e.g. `"Minutes after which an unreleased sync lock is considered stale and stolen."`, `"Deactivate"`, `"No"`, `"Errors"`) to unrelated existing entries and carried over their wrong `msgstr` (e.g. `"Deactivate"` briefly read "активний", the same text as `"Active"`) — why: `tests/test_translations.py::test_po_has_no_empty_or_fuzzy_entries` fails on any fuzzy flag regardless of whether the carried-over text happens to look plausible, and shipping a wrong-but-plausible translation (e.g. a "No" button rendering "Немає") would be a worse defect than an empty one — alternatives: accept the fuzzy guess and only strip the flag (would have shipped several visibly wrong UI strings, caught only by manually reading each diff, which is what this session did instead).
- [p03-implement/decision] `config/settings/e2e.py` now sets a disposable default `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEYS` (`["e2e-not-a-production-encryption-key"]`) when the environment provides none, mirroring the existing disposable `SECRET_KEY` default in the same file — why: `manage.py seed_e2e` now creates two `GitHubConnection` rows with real (fake) encrypted tokens via `connections.services.set_token()` for the Settings > Connections e2e cases, and `crypto.get_fernet()` raises `ImproperlyConfigured` on an empty key list (by design, T2); `config.settings.e2e` inherits from `prod.py`, which expects a real key from the environment, but e2e is a disposable localhost surface exactly like the SECRET_KEY case just above it — alternatives: require the orchestrator/developer to export `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEYS` before `make e2e-up` (adds an undocumented manual step to a target whose whole point is "just run this"), seed connections with `token_encrypted=None`/`status=unverified` and skip the last4-masking e2e case (weakens T23's own acceptance criterion, which is specifically about a token being present and masked, not absent).
- [p03-implement/decision] `seed_e2e` deliberately does not run `verify_connection()` against the two connections it creates — it sets `status=OK` directly, which is meaningful only because e2e's own scope_notes say no case here triggers a sync or a live check; a real deployment's `bootstrap_connection` never does this either (status stays `unverified` until the operator or `--verify` runs it) — why: CLAUDE.md forbids any live GitHub call in this project, in tests or otherwise, and e2e has no GitHub credentials to call with — alternatives: seed with `status=unverified` (then the banner/list e2e cases would need a real check flow to reach `ok`, which is exactly the live call intake forbids).
- [p03-implement/decision] T24's gate caught two real findings, both fixed: (1) `apps/github_sync/tests/test_sync.py::test_second_sync_is_idempotent` assigned `repository = _make_repository(...)` and never read the variable — dropped the assignment (`ruff` `F841`). (2) `apps/github_sync/services.py`'s `transaction.on_commit(lambda pk=pull_request.pk: process_pull_request(pk))` failed `mypy` with "Cannot infer type of lambda" — replaced with `functools.partial(process_pull_request, pull_request.pk)`, which is both mypy-clean and the more idiomatic way to bind a loop variable for a deferred callback — why: CLAUDE.md's lint gate is `ruff check` + `mypy` + `makemigrations --check --dry-run` + `manage.py check`, all required green before commit — alternatives for (2): an explicit type-annotated inner function instead of a lambda (more code for the same effect), a `# type: ignore` comment (hides a class of bug mypy is specifically good at catching, forbidden by "don't weaken a check to make it pass").
- [p03-implement/handoff] Session 5 finished the phase: T22 (Ukrainian parity — translated the 57 empty msgids left by session 4's `make messages`, plus fixed 34 pre-existing `#, fuzzy` entries carrying wrong guessed text from unrelated msgids, see the `p03-implement/decision` bullet above the T22 note; ran `compilemessages` and `make css`; added `test_uk_render_has_no_canary_english` to both `apps/connections/tests/test_views.py` and `apps/github_sync/tests/test_views.py`), T23 (e2e: `e2e/plans/connections.plan.yaml` + `e2e/web/test_connections.py`, 4 cases; extended `apps/accounts/management/commands/seed_e2e.py` with two `GitHubConnection` rows (one `ok`, one expiring in 5 days), an `Organization`/`Repository` pair and a finished `SyncRun`; added a disposable default `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEYS` to `config/settings/e2e.py` so `set_token()` has a key to encrypt with — see the two `p03-implement/decision` bullets above this one for why), and T24 (the full gate — two findings fixed, see above; ran the full lint chain, the full test suite, the from-zero migration check, both management commands non-interactively, `make e2e-up && pytest e2e -q; make e2e-down`, and wrote the "Phase 3" section of `docs/PROGRESS.md`). All 24 tasks are now `[x]`. `uv run pytest -q` is green (no skips, no xfails), `ruff check .`, `ruff format --check .`, `mypy`, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` and `manage.py check` are all clean, `make css`/`make messages` diffs are exactly the translation/CSS content changed this session (nothing stale). Nothing in the working tree is half-edited. Not committed — the orchestrator commits.


## p03-review_fix1

- [p03-review_fix1/decision] Fixed the SyncRun-before-lock ordering (REVIEW-r1 major #1) by wrapping `SyncLock.objects.create()` in `_acquire_lock` in its own `transaction.atomic()` savepoint and, in `run_sync`, deleting the just-created `SyncRun` row when `_acquire_lock` raises `SyncAlreadyRunning`, before re-raising — why: SQLite (and any DB under an enclosing transaction, including every test) marks the whole enclosing transaction unusable after an uncaught `IntegrityError` unless the failing statement is inside its own `atomic()` block; without the savepoint, `run.delete()` itself raised `TransactionManagementError` — alternatives: catch and ignore the delete's failure (would silently leave the phantom row in production, the exact bug being fixed), set `status=failed` instead of deleting (keeps a row for a run that never actually started, more confusing than removing it).
- [p03-review_fix1/decision] Repository-level and post-hook failures now record an error and let the run finish with `status=partial` instead of leaving it wedged at `status=running` forever (REVIEW-r1 major #2 and #7/T15): `process_one` in `apps/github_sync/services.py` gained `except GitHubError` and a final `except Exception` (logged via `logger.exception`, masked into `error_log`); `process_pull_request` is now invoked through a new `_run_hook()` wrapper registered with `transaction.on_commit` that catches any exception from the hook and records it the same way, rather than letting it propagate out of the orchestrator — why: PLAN.md T16 explicitly promised this behaviour with no test proving it, and a wedged `running` row polls the Sync page forever (`hx-trigger="every 2s"` never stops) — alternatives: let a repository-level or hook exception abort the whole run (matches the old, buggy behaviour), retry the repository automatically (out of scope for this phase, no backoff policy defined for a whole-repository retry).
- [p03-review_fix1/decision] `sync_repository` no longer advances `Repository.last_synced_at` to `run.started_at` when a PR failed to sync; it clamps to the earliest failed PR's `updatedAt` instead (REVIEW-r1 major #4) — why: advancing past a failed PR put it below every future incremental watermark (`last_synced_at - SYNC_OVERLAP_MINUTES`), silently dropping it from every future sync forever — exactly the permanent-gap failure mode `.autodev/DECISIONS.md` line 18 (p03-plan) chose repository-level watermarking to avoid — alternatives: leave `last_synced_at` completely untouched whenever any PR in the run failed (would also re-fetch every already-succeeded PR in that repository on the next run, not just the failed one).
- [p03-review_fix1/decision] `create_repositories_from_discovery()` now skips (does not touch) any node whose `github_id` already belongs to a different connection, and moved `sync_since` from `defaults` to Django 5.0's `create_defaults` on `update_or_create` (REVIEW-r1 major #3) — why: the old code silently rebound a repository to a new connection on ordinary bulk-add with no confirm step and no audit entry, directly contradicting the explicit-confirm design already logged in this file, and reset `sync_since` (discarding a narrowed backfill window) on every re-submission of an already-added repository — alternatives: raise/refuse the whole request when any selected row is bound elsewhere (worse UX — would block adding the rest of a mixed selection instead of just skipping the conflicting rows, and the UI already disables those checkboxes so a clean request cannot include them without deliberate tampering).
- [p03-review_fix1/decision] The per-row rebind control in `discovery_content.html` moved from a `<form>` nested inside the outer discovery `<form>` to a standalone `<form id="rebind-<pk>">` rendered after the outer form closes, linked to its checkbox/button via the HTML5 `form="rebind-<pk>"` attribute (REVIEW-r1 major #5) — why: nested `<form>` is invalid HTML; browsers drop the inner start tag and re-parent its children into the outer form, so the rendered "Rebind here" button actually submitted the discovery-add POST instead, with no test ever driving the real markup — alternatives: convert the rebind action to `hx-post`/`hx-include` (htmx is already a dependency and would work, but the HTML5 `form=` attribute needed no JS and matches the existing plain-`<form method=post>` pattern already used elsewhere on this same page).
- [p03-review_fix1/decision] `connection_check` and `repository_discover` now catch `(GitHubError, ConnectionNotUsableError, httpx.TransportError)` around their GitHub calls and render a translated, generic alert (never the raw exception text) instead of letting the view 500 (REVIEW-r1 major #6) — why: CLAUDE.md requires every htmx endpoint to return a visible fragment on error, never an empty 400/500 body, and both surfaces are reachable with an unverified/inactive connection or a flaky GitHub (the "Check"/"Discover" actions are offered even on inactive rows) — alternatives: also guard `_save_connection`'s `verify_connection()` call in `connection_create`/`connection_edit` (same class of bug, but not named by the review and it has its own, different error-surfacing path via form errors; left as-is to stay in the finding's stated scope).
- [p03-review_fix1/decision] Split the single `PERM_CONTENTS` check code into `PERM_CONTENTS_OK` / `PERM_CONTENTS_DENIED` / `PERM_CONTENTS_UNAVAILABLE` (REVIEW-r1 minor) — why: one message/hint pair ("was not checked" / "add a repository, then re-check") was rendered for all three outcomes, so a passing or a genuinely failing contents check both displayed the "unchecked" copy and its unrelated hint — alternatives: key `render_message`/`render_hint` on `(code, outcome)` instead of splitting codes (keeps one code but complicates every other call site and the "every emitted code is documented" doc test, which is designed around one code per row).
- [p03-review_fix1/decision] Gated `POST /sync/run/` behind `catalog.manage_settings` (admin-only), leaving `GET /sync/` and `/sync/status/` open to any lead, and hid the "Sync now" button from leads in the template (REVIEW-r1 minor) — why: `docs/user/connect-github.md` already told operators "syncing is admin-only" but the view only required login, so any lead could trigger outbound GitHub traffic against every connection's rate budget; PLAN.md's rationale for leaving `/sync/` itself ungated (it shows no per-project data) is sound and preserved — alternatives: revert the docs sentence to match the (unintentionally) open behaviour instead (weakens a real access-control gap the review correctly flagged as exploitable, not just a docs typo).
- [p03-review_fix1/decision] Left the client's `paginate()` `GitHubSchemaError.path` as the bare segment name (e.g. `"endCursor"`) rather than a fully-qualified dotted path in the rewritten `test_missing_end_cursor_on_a_further_page_raises_schema_error` (REVIEW-r1 minor #8) — why: `client.paginate()` calls `require_path(page_info, "endCursor")` against the already-unwrapped `pageInfo` sub-dict, not the full payload, so that is the path production code actually raises; rewriting the *client* to re-derive a fully-qualified path was out of scope for a finding about the *test* being tautological — alternatives: change `client.paginate()` itself to pass a fully-qualified path (a real improvement, but expands this finding's fix beyond "point the test at a field the client really depends on").
- [p03-review_fix1/decision] Did not implement the REVIEW-r1 nit about adding an INFO-level log for a rate-limit wait as a no-op — added it for real: `GitHubClient._wait_for_budget()` now logs before sleeping, using a `logger` newly added to `client.py`; also gave `process_one`'s new broad `except Exception` a `logger.exception()` call, which is the first real use of the previously-unused module logger in `apps/github_sync/services.py` — why: the review flagged both loggers as dead weight against a docstring/PLAN.md promise that something logs a rate-limit wait; adding the log line is strictly additive and makes the leak test's log-file assertion meaningful for the sync path, per the review's own suggestion — alternatives: delete the unused loggers instead (satisfies the letter of "unused logger" but leaves the documented behaviour unimplemented).


## p03-review_fix2

- [p03-review_fix2/fix] Fixed REVIEW-r2 major #1 (a crashed/killed/stale sync leaves `SyncRun.status=running` forever): `run_sync()` now wraps its body in `except Exception` that marks the run `FAILED` with `finished_at` and a masked `error_log` line before re-raising; `_acquire_lock()`'s stale-lock steal now also marks the stolen run's own `SyncRun` `FAILED`/`finished_at` instead of only deleting the `SyncLock` row; the connection-grouping loop now catches `cryptography.fernet.InvalidToken` and `django.core.exceptions.ImproperlyConfigured` beside `ConnectionNotUsableError` around `auth_for_connection()`, recording a per-connection skip instead of crashing the run — why: review r2, reachable without any crash via a rotated/lost `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEYS` (the exact recovery scenario `docs/GITHUB_CONNECTIONS.md` documents) — alternatives: only fix the crypto-failure path and leave the generic crash guard for later (rejected: the review's core complaint is the *general* lack of a terminal-status guarantee, not just this one trigger).
- [p03-review_fix2/fix] Fixed REVIEW-r2 major #2 (connection create/edit and rebind 500 on a recoverable GitHub error): `_save_connection()` now wraps `verify_connection(force=True)` in `try/except (GitHubError, ConnectionNotUsableError, httpx.TransportError)`, rolling the transaction back and adding a translated form error, matching the existing invalid-token branch; `repository_rebind()` now guards its post-rebind `_discover_nodes()` re-fetch with the same except clause used in `repository_discover()` and renders the discovery fragment with the alert instead of propagating — why: review r2, `.autodev/DECISIONS.md`'s prior deferral (p03-review_fix1) argued `_save_connection` "has its own error-surfacing path via form errors", which r2 showed to be false (nothing converted the exception into a form error) — alternatives: none; this is a straight bug fix.
- [p03-review_fix2/fix] Fixed REVIEW-r2 minor (SSO on `/user` misdiagnosed as a network failure): `verify_connection()`'s `/user` `try` block now also catches `GitHubSSOError`, recording `SSO_AUTHORIZATION_REQUIRED` with `org`/`url` and leaving `auth_ok=False` (mirroring the existing `AUTH_FAILED` branch) so the connection resolves to `DEGRADED` instead of the check silently erroring out to the view's generic "could not reach GitHub" message.
- [p03-review_fix2/fix] Fixed REVIEW-r2 minor (expiry banner gated on `is_staff`): `connections.context_processors.connection_alerts` now gates on `user.has_perm("catalog.manage_settings")` alone — why: nothing in this app ties `is_staff` to admin-group membership (only `seed_e2e.py` sets both together), and `docs/SETUP.md`'s onboarding flow can leave a non-superuser admin-group member with `is_staff=False` — alternatives: `if user.is_staff or user.has_perm(...)` (rejected: equally costs a query for any non-staff user reaching the `or`'s right side, so it gives up the "free" short-circuit without gaining anything over dropping `is_staff` outright). This raises the query ceiling for a non-superuser from 0 to 2 (`has_perm()`'s own permission lookup) — `test_processor_costs_a_fixed_low_query_count_for_admin_and_zero_for_lead` is renamed and its lead ceiling changed from 0 to 2 to match.
- [p03-review_fix2/fix] Fixed REVIEW-r2 minor (`GitHubSchemaError` path too bare in `paginate()`): `client.py::paginate()` now calls `require_path(data, f"{page_path}.nodes")` / `f"{page_path}.pageInfo.hasNextPage"` / `f"{page_path}.pageInfo.endCursor"` against the full payload instead of an already-unwrapped sub-dict, so the raised path names which connection and page drifted (e.g. `"node.reviews.pageInfo.endCursor"`) — this supersedes the p03-review_fix1 decision above that left the bare-segment path in place as out of scope; r2 asked for it directly.
- [p03-review_fix2/fix] Fixed REVIEW-r2 minor (raw dict indexing on a discovery node in `repository_discover`): `chosen = [node for node in nodes if node["id"] in selected_ids]` now uses `require(node, "id")`, and the POST-handling block was moved from the `try`'s `else` clause into the `try` body itself so a schema-drift `GitHubSchemaError` there is also caught by the surrounding `except (GitHubError, ...)` instead of raising a bare `KeyError`.


## p03-e2e

- [p03-e2e/decision] Extended `e2e/plans/connections.plan.yaml` and `e2e/web/test_connections.py` with three cases beyond T23's original four: the "Sync now" button's admin/lead visibility split, the `PROTECT`-FK delete refusal on "e2e-ok" (which has a bound repository) with its rebind message, and the discovery page's no-connection-selected state — why: all three are real, already-implemented user-facing behaviour (`p03-review_fix1`'s admin-only sync gate, `catalog.services`'s `PROTECT` FK, `repository_discover`'s lazy GraphQL call) that is reachable without any live GitHub call and without mutating the shared seeded connections/repository other cases in the file read, so leaving them untested was a gap rather than a deliberate scope cut — alternatives: leave them to the unit suite alone (rejected: the unit suite already covers the authorization/refusal *logic*, but not that the real template wires the button's visibility and the delete/discovery views' error partials correctly through a browser).
- [p03-e2e/finding] No product bug found while authoring or running the extended suite (11/11 pass, `uv run pytest e2e -q`). The only failures during authoring were in the new specs themselves: `get_by_role("alert")` was scoped to the whole page and matched the site-wide expiry banner (`templates/partials/connection_alerts.html`, rendered on every admin page including `/settings/connections/` and `/settings/repositories/discover/`) as well as the page-local error message; fixed by scoping both locators to `#connections-page` / `#discovery-page` before asserting, rather than loosening the assertion.


- [orchestrator/commit] kept `apps/connections/tests/test_crypto.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.
- [orchestrator/commit] kept `tests/test_logging.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.

## p04-plan

- [p04/plan] A GitHub login identity is auto-mapped to an auto-created `Person` (display name = the login), so only bare git emails ever reach the unmapped-identity queue — why: a login identifies exactly one GitHub account, so attributing it is a fact and not a guess, and the phase's own acceptance criterion describes the queue as holding "a seeded unmatched email"; leaving logins unmapped would make person-level metrics empty until a lead hand-mapped every developer — alternatives: queue every new identity of both kinds (hours of manual work before the first number appears), auto-map nothing and treat `Identity` as the person (breaks merges and the email↔login collapse the spec requires).
- [p04/plan] Identity resolution never re-points an identity that already has a person, and bot flags are written only when a `Person` is auto-created (plus explicitly from the UI) — why: `Identity → Person` mapping is operator-owned data that a re-sync must not overwrite (ARCHITECTURE "What must never be lost" #3), and a lead's "not a bot" correction has to survive the next sync — alternatives: re-assert `is_bot` from the settings list on every sync (silently undoes lead corrections), a `is_auto_mapped` flag on `Person` (extra state that only encodes what the `person is None` guard already gives).
- [p04/plan] DEVIATION from ARCHITECTURE's model set: `activity.Commit` gains a nullable auxiliary FK `author_email_identity`, and `upserts.py` now materialises the commit-author email identity even when a login is present — why: the current `login or email` fallback throws away GitHub's own email↔login pairing, which is spec §4.1's second auto-mapping rule, and persisting it on the row keeps identity resolution in post-processing (where the architecture's pipeline contract puts it) instead of forcing the writer to resolve people; spec §4 explicitly allows auxiliary fields — alternatives: resolve identities inside `upsert_pull_request()` (moves a rule into the writer and makes `recompute` unable to redo it), re-read the pairing from `Commit.raw` (absent whenever `STORE_RAW_PAYLOADS` is off), keep the pairing only in memory during a sync (lost for `recompute`).
- [p04/plan] `ready_for_review_at` is derived as "the stored timeline value, else `None` while the PR is still a draft, else `created_at`" — writing the fallback into the same field sync populates from the timeline — why: spec §4.2 asks for exactly this fallback, and the rule is idempotent in both directions (a second derive pass sees a set value; a re-sync rewrites the timeline value and derive reapplies the same fallback), so no extra column is needed — alternatives: a separate `first_ready_for_review_event_at` column (a migration and a second field for a value sync already stores), computing the fallback at read time in every metric (the spec calls it a stored PR field, and every reader would have to remember).
- [p04/plan] `merge_method` is left `unknown` and deferred to the churn phase — why: no metric in spec §8.2 reads it, only churn does, and the parent-count evidence that distinguishes merge/squash/rebase lives in the git clone that phase owns; guessing it from the GraphQL payload would write a wrong value into a cached field — alternatives: heuristics over `merge_commit_sha` vs the PR's commit shas (cannot separate squash from rebase), fetching merge-commit parents in the sync query (extra GraphQL cost for a field nothing reads yet).
- [p04/plan] Path-glob matching gets its own tested module `apps/catalog/globs.py` with fixed semantics (`**` spans whole segments, `*` never crosses `/`, a pattern without `/` matches the basename at any depth, an invalid pattern is skipped with a warning) — why: `EXCLUDED_PATH_GLOBS`/`TEST_PATH_GLOBS` mix rooted and basename patterns, `fnmatch` lets `*` cross `/` and `PurePath.full_match` is Python 3.13 only, and `policy` (sensitive paths) plus `churn` need the identical matcher later — alternatives: `fnmatch` directly (wrong for `**/migrations/**` and `tests/**`), adding a `wcmatch` dependency (a new runtime dependency for ~40 lines), duplicating a matcher per app (three places to get subtly different).
- [p04/plan] `is_hotfix` patterns (`^\s*(hotfix|fix)\b` in the title, `hotfix/`/`fix/` branch prefixes, a `hotfix` label) are module constants in `activity/derive.py` rather than a new `AppSetting` key — why: the roadmap makes only the bot login list configurable, and spec §15 says to take the simpler option and record it; promoting them to settings later is a migration plus a settings row, not a redesign — alternatives: a `HOTFIX_PATTERNS` setting (configuration surface nobody asked for), reusing the follow-up-fix heuristic from phase 10 (does not exist yet and answers a different question).
- [p04/plan] `is_rubber_stamp` uses a strict `<` against `RUBBER_STAMP_MAX_MINUTES`, so an approval at exactly 10 minutes is not a rubber stamp — why: spec §8.2 says "апрувнутий <10 хв", and the phase's acceptance criterion names the boundary explicitly, so the tie must fall on the side that does not accuse anyone — alternatives: `<=` (flags a PR the spec does not), rounding to whole minutes (makes the boundary depend on seconds nobody sees).
- [p04/plan] The metric population rule ships now as `activity.selectors.pull_requests_for_metrics(scope)` / `bot_pull_request_count(scope)` plus `catalog.selectors`, all taking a `ScopeFilter` — why: the phase must prove a bot PR leaves metrics and lands in a separate counter before `metrics.compute()` exists (phase 7), and CLAUDE.md requires every selector to start from `scope_for_user()` so phase 8 narrows access in one place — alternatives: inline `.exclude(...)` in each future metric (the rule would drift across call sites), waiting for phase 7 (nothing would prove this phase's bot acceptance criterion).
- [p04/plan] mypy's `files` list gains `apps/activity/derive.py` and `apps/catalog/identity.py` — why: both are rule modules in the sense CLAUDE.md means by `services.py`, and this phase's correctness is exactly the kind a type checker catches; the gate is strengthened, never weakened — alternatives: naming them `services.py` (a single `catalog/services.py` would then hold settings, repositories and identity resolution), leaving them unchecked (the two most rule-dense modules of the phase would be the only unchecked ones).


## p04-implement

- [p04/implement] Settings → People UI actions (assign/create-person/mark-bot/exclude) swap the whole unmapped-identity-queue container on an htmx POST rather than a single row — why: those four actions never need to touch more than the identity that just left the queue, and swapping the container reuses the same context builder `_render_queue()` the GET view uses, so the row count and pagination stay consistent without a second rendering path — alternatives: swap only the acted-on row (leaves pagination/row-count out of sync when the row disappears), a JS-driven row removal (no JS build step in this project for that).
- [p04/implement] `identity.py::create_person_from_identity()` is the single service the three "create a person from an identity" UI actions call with different flags (`is_bot`, `exclude_from_metrics`) — why: it is the UI-triggered sibling of `person_for_login()`'s auto-create path (same `person is None` guard, so a double-submit is a no-op) and keeping one function means the three actions can never drift on what "create" means — alternatives: three near-duplicate view-level `Person.objects.create()` calls (rule duplicated three times, guard easy to forget on one).
- [p04/implement] Derived-field definitions are documented in `docs/DECISIONS.md` (Phase 4 section), not `docs/METRICS.md` — why: `docs/METRICS.md` is a generated artefact owned by phase 7 (`metrics.compute()` does not exist yet, so there is nothing to generate it from); `tests/test_docs.py::test_every_derived_field_is_documented` greps `DerivedFields`/`FileDerivedFields` dataclass field names against `docs/DECISIONS.md` so a future field addition fails the build without a doc update — alternatives: pre-create an empty `docs/METRICS.md` now (a generated file with no generator, contradicting CLAUDE.md's freshness-test convention), skip documenting the fields until phase 7 (the phase's own acceptance criteria assume they're recomputable and understood now).
- [p04/implement] `make messages` on a fresh run produced several fuzzy/mismatched msgstr entries from msgmerge's similarity matching (e.g. `%(counter)s unmapped identity` fuzzy-matched onto an unrelated `%(count)s minute(s)` translation with the wrong placeholder name) — fixed by hand via a one-off `polib` script that set the correct `uk` text and cleared the `fuzzy` flag/`previous_msgid` on every affected entry, then re-ran `compilemessages` — why: `tests/test_po_placeholders_match_between_msgid_and_msgstr` and `test_po_has_no_empty_or_fuzzy_entries` would otherwise fail the build, and shipping the mismatched fuzzy text would be a wrong-language regression on an unrelated string — alternatives: hand-edit the `.po` file directly (same result, more error-prone across ~400 lines), leave the fuzzy flag and let a human clear it later (violates CLAUDE.md's same-phase Ukrainian-parity rule).


## p04-review_fix1


- [p04/review_fix1] Fixed the identity-queue N+1 (REVIEW-r1 MAJOR): `_queue_context()` now materialises `AssignIdentityForm`'s `person` choices into a plain list once per request instead of letting `ModelChoiceIterator` re-query on every `{{ assign_form.person }}` row render — why: measured 14/34/~59 queries for 5/25/50 rows, and the old `assertNumQueries(<15)` guard happened to pass at exactly 14 so it could not catch it; `test_queue_query_count` now asserts the query count is identical for 5 and 25 seeded rows, a budget that fails if per-row querying returns — alternatives: pass a single pre-rendered `<select>` into the row include (more template plumbing for the same effect).
- [p04/review_fix1] Fixed duplicate PRs under a restricted scope (REVIEW-r1 MAJOR): `activity.selectors._scoped()` now filters `repository__in=Repository.objects.filter(projects__id__in=...)` instead of joining through `repository__projects__id__in` directly, avoiding the M2M row fan-out instead of masking it with `.distinct()` — why: a subquery is cheaper than distinct-ing a joined result set and is exactly as correct; replaced the tautological `test_selectors_take_a_scope_filter` with `test_restricted_scope_does_not_double_count_a_pr_whose_repo_is_in_two_projects`, which seeds a repo in two projects and asserts the PR appears once under a restricted `ScopeFilter` and an out-of-scope PR is absent — alternatives: `.distinct()` on the joined queryset (review's other suggested fix; works but pays the fan-out cost first).
- [p04/review_fix1] Added `TestTimestamps` to `test_derive.py` covering `first_commit_at`'s `authored_at or committed_at` fallback and `last_activity_at`'s review-comment-is-latest and created-at-only cases (REVIEW-r1 MAJOR: acceptance criterion 1 requires every derived field to have a fixture test, and these two had none) — why: both fields have real branching logic that was previously provable-wrong without a test failing — alternatives: none, this is a direct fix of a coverage gap.
- [p04/review_fix1] `_revert_target_by_commit` now prefers a `state=MERGED` candidate PR (ordered `-merged_at`, falling back to the unfiltered candidate set) instead of an unfiltered ascending `merged_at` order, and both the `Commit` sha-prefix lookup and the `merge_commit_sha` fallback are now deterministically ordered (`order_by("sha")` / `order_by("-merged_at")`) — why: REVIEW-r1 MINOR showed SQLite's ascending-NULL-first order let an unmerged PR sharing a reverted commit win over the merged PR that actually introduced the code, and an ambiguous short sha prefix could resolve to a different `Commit` between derive passes, a quiet idempotency hole — alternatives: require a full 40-char sha match (would silently stop resolving the common 7-char shorthand GitHub itself uses in commit messages).
- [p04/review_fix1] `derive_pull_request()` is now `@transaction.atomic` so the `PRFile.bulk_update` and the PR's own `save(update_fields=...)` land in one transaction (REVIEW-r1 MINOR: a mid-write crash could previously leave `PRFile` flags and the PR's cached `effective_additions`/`size_bucket` disagreeing, undetected) — why: minimal, matches the plan's own idempotency claim — alternatives: none needed, straightforward fix.
- [p04/review_fix1] Split `test_has_test_changes_ignores_excluded_test_files` (REVIEW-r1 MINOR: it only exercised the positive `is_test` half, not the `and not is_excluded` half) into two tests: a non-excluded test file (`has_test_changes is True`) and a file that is both a test and excluded via `**/migrations/**` (`has_test_changes is False`) — why: deleting the exclusion clause from `compute_derived` now fails the new test, closing the gap the review named — alternatives: none, direct fix.
- [p04/review_fix1] `person_edit` now snapshots the tracked `PersonForm` fields off the instance before `form.save()` and passes them as `record_audit(..., before=...)` (REVIEW-r1 NIT: the edit audit entry previously recorded only the new values) — why: trivial, matches `merge_people`'s existing `before`/`after` pattern and ARCHITECTURE's "what must never be lost" note about the operator's manual mapping work — alternatives: none needed.
- [p04/review_fix1] REVIEW-r1's MINOR "`bot_pull_request_count` has no caller; the People page counts bot people instead" is accepted as a documented plan deviation rather than rewired — why: the shipped `bot_person_count()` answers the People page's actual question ("how many bot accounts exist"), while `bot_pull_request_count()` is the metric-population counterpart `pull_requests_for_metrics()` needs and stays correctly tested at the selector level (`test_bot_pr_is_out_of_the_metric_population_and_in_the_bot_counter`); phase 7/8 is where a PR-level bot counter belongs on a dashboard, not this settings page — alternatives: swap the People page counter to `bot_pull_request_count()` (review's other suggested fix; would make the page's own string, "excluded from metrics automatically", read correctly for PRs but wrong for the adjacent person-count column it sits next to).


## p04-e2e

- [p04/e2e] Added `e2e/plans/people.plan.yaml` and `e2e/web/test_people.py` (12 cases) covering Settings > People (list, person CRUD, permissions) and the unmapped-identity queue (pagination, assign, mark-bot, exclude, create-person, merge, self-merge refusal) — why: this is the only user-facing surface phase 4 ships (PLAN.md "User-facing: yes"), and acceptance criteria 2, 3 and 4 are all observable only through this UI — alternatives: none, this is the phase's e2e deliverable.
- [p04/e2e] Acceptance criteria 1 ("each derived field has a test") and 5 ("re-running derive produces identical values") are not re-proven at the e2e layer — logged in `e2e/plans/people.plan.yaml`'s `deferred_not_authored` (`derived-pr-fields-on-a-dashboard`) rather than authored against a fake surface — why: no page in this phase renders `size_bucket`, `is_revert`, `ready_for_review_at`, the rubber-stamp boundary or any other `apps/activity/derive.py` field (the People list only shows `pr_count`); PLAN.md's own "Out of scope" section defers the dashboard UI that would show them to phase 7/8, and both criteria are already fully covered by hand-built-fixture unit tests (`apps/activity/tests/test_derive.py`, `apps/github_sync/tests/test_pipeline.py::test_second_sync_leaves_derived_fields_identical`) per qa-oracles.md's rule against pinning behaviour with no observable surface — alternatives: assert on `pr_count`/list ordering as a proxy for derived-field correctness (would not actually exercise the field being claimed, a photograph oracle rather than an intent oracle).
- [p04/e2e] `manage.py seed_e2e` gained `_seed_people_and_identities()`: a mapped person+PR, a bot person, four dedicated unmapped `git_email` identities (one per mutating queue action: assign/mark-bot/exclude/create-person), a merge source/target pair, a standalone self-merge-guard person, and 55 unmapped identities prefixed `e2e-zzz-queue-item-` for the pagination case — why: the People/queue UI has no seed data at all yet, and Settings pages have no "create test data" path of their own (unlike connections/sync, which already had rows); the `zzz-` prefix sorts after every action-target identity's name (`unmapped_identities()` orders by kind, value), so those 55 rows are always the *last* 55 in the queue regardless of which action-target identities earlier specs in the same file have already consumed — total unmapped rows is always in [55, 59], always exactly two pages at `IDENTITY_PAGE_SIZE=50`, with item 000 always on page 1 and item 054 always on page 2, keeping the pagination spec order-independent per e2e-authoring.md — alternatives: one shared unmapped identity per action reused across specs (breaks independence — whichever spec runs first consumes it), a `zzz-` prefix picked to sort *before* instead of after the action targets (tried first; pushed the small mutable action-target set onto page 2, making those rows unreachable at the default page — reverted, see below).
- [p04/e2e] Real product-adjacent bug: none found in `apps/catalog`/`apps/activity`. Two bugs were found and fixed in the *e2e spec itself* while authoring against the real running surface (not reachable from the unit `Client`, which never renders the shared `<select>` across sibling rows): (1) the queue row template reuses one `AssignIdentityForm` instance for every row's "Assign" `<select>`, so once `create_person_from_identity()` has named a new person after an identity's own value (mark-bot/exclude/create-person), a page-wide text search for that identity's email starts matching that person's `<option>` in every other row's dropdown too — fixed by scoping removal assertions to the Value column only (`_identity_row()` helper, `td:nth-child(2)`) instead of whole-row text. (2) the first pagination seed ordering put the 55 queue-only identities *before* the four action-target identities alphabetically, which pushed the targets onto page 2 by default, timing out every action spec that tried to find its row on page 1 — fixed by flipping the sort prefix (see previous bullet) — why record both: neither is a product defect, but both are exactly the kind of thing a spec-photograph would have hidden if I'd written the locator from "what Playwright happened to find" instead of re-deriving it from how `unmapped_identities()` actually orders rows — alternatives: page through to find each row (slower, and defers rather than fixes the underlying page-1-vs-page-2 assumption the rest of the plan's boundary claims depend on).
- [p04/e2e] Verified: `uv run pytest e2e -q` is 23 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped against a freshly flushed-and-reseeded `config.settings.e2e` database (11 pre-existing + 12 new). A second run against the *same*, now-mutated database (no re-flush) fails 6 of the 12 new cases with `Locator.click: Timeout` — this is expected, not a flake: `make e2e-down` deletes `.e2e/` (including the sqlite file) before every real `make e2e-up`, so the suite only ever runs once per fresh seed in the actual lifecycle; the second run here only demonstrates that mutating specs correctly consume their dedicated seed rows and do not silently no-op on a second pass — ran `manage.py flush --noinput && manage.py seed_e2e` (both against `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=config.settings.e2e`, the same live db.sqlite3 the orchestrator's already-running server reads) directly rather than through `make e2e-up`/`e2e-down`, since the orchestrator started the surface before this step and this step must not stop or restart it.


## p04-e2e_fix1

- [p04/e2e_fix1] Root cause of the 6 red e2e cases: `db.sqlite3` under `.e2e/` had accumulated mutations from earlier review passes (r1/r2) without an intervening `make e2e-down` + `make e2e-up`, and `seed_e2e`'s `get_or_create`-only seeding only fills in *missing* rows, so it never undid what a previous pass's UI actions had already done (identities already assigned/bot/excluded, a merge already repointed, a duplicate "E2E UI Created Person"). This directly contradicts this same file's earlier `p04/e2e` entry, which assumed a second pytest run against a stale db was an acceptable non-issue since real usage always goes through fresh `e2e-up`/`e2e-down` — that assumption doesn't hold for this orchestrator, which explicitly reruns `pytest e2e -q` against the *same*, already-started surface without tearing it down (`Do not start or stop the surfaces`). Fixed `apps/accounts/management/commands/seed_e2e.py::_seed_people_and_identities` to force-reset the mutable fixture state every run (delete the UI-created duplicate, detach and delete auto-created action-target Person rows, re-point the merge-source identity back to a fresh "E2E Merge Source"), matching the command's own "Idempotently create" docstring claim. Added `apps/accounts/tests/test_seed_e2e.py` asserting a second `call_command("seed_e2e")` after the exact mutations the queue/merge cases perform restores the pristine state. Ran `manage.py seed_e2e` directly against the live `config.settings.e2e` db (not through `make e2e-up`, which also restarts the server) to repair the already-dirtied fixture, then `uv run pytest e2e -q` → 23 passed. Editing `seed_e2e.py` invalidated `static/css/.build-manifest.sha256` (it hashes every scanned `.py` file, not just template/CSS sources), so re-ran `make css` and committed the regenerated `app.css`/manifest alongside — alternatives considered: reset the db via a full `make e2e-up`/`e2e-down` cycle (rejected, forbidden by this step) or leave the assertions/order alone and just delete the accumulated rows by hand (rejected, doesn't fix the underlying non-idempotency the orchestrator will hit again on the next retry).


## p04-e2e_fix3

- [p04/e2e_fix3] Root cause of this run's 6 red cases was not `seed_e2e`'s own logic (already fixed idempotent in `p04/e2e_fix1`) but that nothing ever calls it a second time: `fix1` reseeded the db by hand *once*, but the orchestrator went on to run `pytest e2e -q` again afterward (this step), remutating the same never-restarted surface, and no fixture reseeds in between — so the six mutating cases (create-person, assign, mark-bot, exclude, create-person-from-identity, merge) hit already-consumed rows/duplicates again, reproduced locally by running `uv run pytest e2e/web/test_people.py -k test_create_person_persists` twice in a row against the live surface without reseeding (1st: pass, 2nd: fails with 2 duplicate "E2E UI Created Person" rows — same shape as the original failure's 3 duplicates, i.e. one extra per unreseeded rerun). Fixed by adding an `autouse`, session-scoped `_reseed_e2e_data` fixture to `e2e/conftest.py` that runs `manage.py seed_e2e` (via `subprocess`, `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=config.settings.e2e`) once at the start of every `pytest e2e` session, after confirming the surface is reachable — a pure data reset against the shared sqlite file, no server/worker process touched, so it doesn't violate "do not start or stop the surfaces". Verified: `uv run pytest e2e -q` green three times in a row with no restart between runs (23 passed each time), plus the full `uv run pytest -q` unit suite (previously-existing tests) still green — alternatives: keep relying on the orchestrator/human to remember to reseed by hand between retries (rejected, exactly the failure mode that caused fix1 to not hold); reseed inside each mutating test via a per-test fixture (rejected, slower — 6x the subprocess cost for no benefit since the mutations are cross-test independent already); have `seed_e2e` detect staleness and no-op when already fresh (rejected, unnecessary complexity — the command is cheap and idempotent by design).

- [orchestrator/commit] kept `apps/connections/tests/test_crypto.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.
- [orchestrator/commit] kept `tests/test_logging.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.

## p05-plan


- [p05/plan] `AISignal` gains `evidence_hash` (sha256) and a `UniqueConstraint(pull_request, rule, evidence_hash)`, with `commit` deliberately **outside** the key — why: ARCHITECTURE line 371 names exactly this key as the idempotency contract, a 200-char CharField is a poor index, and two commits of one PR carrying the identical trailer are one fact about the PR, so the signal points at the first matching commit in a deterministic order (`committed_at`, then `sha`) — alternatives: include `commit_id` in the key (one duplicate-looking signal per commit for a marker every commit of the branch carries), index `evidence` directly (wasteful and dialect-sensitive), rely only on the wanted-vs-existing diff with no DB constraint (nothing catches a concurrent double-run).
- [p05/plan] Detection is a wanted-vs-existing **diff** per PR (create missing, delete stale) rather than an append-only writer plus a cleanup pass — why: it makes both "re-running twice creates no duplicate rows" and "deactivating a rule removes its signals on the next run" true by construction, and it also covers an edited pattern and an edited PR body, which a cleanup keyed on `is_active` would miss — alternatives: delete-all-then-recreate per PR (churns primary keys and `detected_at` on every recompute, so "when was this first detected" is lost), a separate sweep task for deactivated rules (a second code path that can drift from the writer).
- [p05/plan] `DetectionRule.name` becomes unique and is the natural key `seed_detection_rules` seeds by; the command **never** overwrites an existing row unless `--update` is passed — why: `DetectionRule` is operator-owned data per ARCHITECTURE ("this data cannot be recovered by re-syncing"), so an admin's pattern fix or deactivation must survive the next seed run, which is the whole point of rules being data rather than code (RISKS row 5) — alternatives: a separate `key` slug column (a second identifier for the same thing), `update_or_create` on every seed (silently undoes the admin's false-positive fix — exactly the failure the risk row is about), seeding only into an empty table (no way to add a newly-published vendor rule later).
- [p05/plan] Rule patterns are always regexes, compiled once per run with `IGNORECASE | MULTILINE`; a pattern that does not compile is skipped with a warning at detection time and rejected by `DetectionRule.clean()` at entry time — why: spec §6.1's own example (`^(codex|cursor|claude|copilot)/`) is a regex, one syntax is simpler than a literal/regex flag, and one bad admin-entered rule must never stop detection for every other rule and PR — alternatives: a `is_regex` boolean with literal fallback (two matching semantics to test and document), raising on a bad pattern (a single typo in the settings UI would break the whole sync pipeline).
- [p05/plan] Two ticked disclosure boxes yield `ambiguous` even when they agree, and a ticked box whose label matches no configured synonym yields `missing` when it is the only tick — why: the acceptance criteria name both cases, and both mean the author did not actually answer the question; treating an unrecognised label as a disclosure would let a typo read as a declaration — alternatives: resolve agreeing duplicates to that category (contradicts the criterion), map an unknown ticked label to `partial` (invents a declaration nobody made).
- [p05/plan] `ai_disclosure = ambiguous` with no signals resolves to `ai_status = unknown` — why: spec §6.3 lists `unknown` only for `missing`, leaving `ambiguous` unassigned; `unknown` is the honest answer, and phase 6's `DISCLOSURE_MISSING` rule already treats `{missing, ambiguous}` alike — alternatives: `no_ai` (asserts an absence the PR never stated), a sixth status (changes a spec-fixed enum every later phase reads).
- [p05/plan] DEVIATION from phase 4's Out of scope: `manage.py recompute` is created **here** (derive + detect only), not in phase 7 — why: this phase's deliverable "re-running detection over stored PRs without touching GitHub" needs a command, and `recompute` is the one CLAUDE.md and spec §5.5 already name; phase 6 adds policy and phase 7 adds rollups and `--from/--to` repair semantics to the same command — alternatives: a phase-local `manage.py detect` command (a command CLAUDE.md does not list, to be deleted or aliased in phase 7), exposing re-run only through the dry-run UI (the dry run must not write, so it cannot be the re-run path).
- [p05/plan] DEVIATION from the roadmap's phase split: a **minimal** PR detail page (`dashboards:pull_request_detail`, `/prs/<pk>/`) ships in this phase with the AI section only — why: acceptance criterion 6 requires the evidence fragment to be "shown on the PR page" and no PR page exists; phase 9 extends the same URL name, view and template with the timeline, files, metrics, violations and churn rather than creating them — alternatives: defer the criterion to phase 9 (leaves this phase's own acceptance unproven and the evidence unverifiable by a lead), render the signals on a page of this phase's own invention (a second PR surface to delete later).
- [p05/plan] The rule dry run posts the **form**, so an unsaved rule can be tested before it is saved, and it scopes its "last N PRs" through `scope_for_user()` — why: RISKS row 5's mitigation is that a bad rule is caught before it accuses anyone, which only works if the test happens before the write; scoping it keeps RISKS row 3's single choke point intact on a new read surface — alternatives: dry-run only saved rules (the admin must first write the false positive they are trying to avoid), an unscoped "last N PRs globally" (a fourth exit for another project's data).
- [p05/plan] Two new `ai` settings only — `DETECTION_DRY_RUN_PR_COUNT` (50) and `DISCLOSURE_TOOL_ALIASES` (dict) — reusing the already-seeded `DISCLOSURE_SECTION_HEADINGS`, `DISCLOSURE_LABELS_*`, `DISCLOSURE_TOOLS_LABELS` and `AI_COHORT_INCLUDE_SUSPECTED` — why: spec §6.2's "configurable synonyms" are already there from phase 2, and the only genuinely missing knobs are the dry-run sample size and the free-text→`Tool` mapping — alternatives: hard-code the tool aliases in `disclosure.py` (a localised or renamed tool then needs a release, contradicting the phase goal), add a per-detector enable flag (`is_active` per rule already covers it).
- [p05/plan] `pr_body_footer` and `html_comment` read **disjoint** haystacks over the same PR body (visible text vs only the `<!-- ... -->` spans) — why: a marker hidden in an HTML comment is invisible to a reviewer and deserves its own rule and its own confidence, and disjoint haystacks stop one body marker producing two signals from two detectors — alternatives: both read the whole body (double-counts every footer marker and makes the two detectors' confidences meaningless).

## p05-implement

- [p05/implement] `activity.selectors` gains a public `pull_requests_in_scope(scope)` wrapper around the existing private `_scoped()` — why: `ai_detection.selectors` (and phase 9's PR detail page) need every PR a user may see, bots and excluded people included, which is a strict superset of `pull_requests_for_metrics()`'s denominator; reusing activity's own scoping keeps `scope_for_user()` the single choke point instead of a second app re-deriving the same repository→project filter — alternatives: duplicate the `Repository.objects.filter(projects__id__in=...)` logic inside `ai_detection.selectors` (a second place to keep in sync with `ScopeFilter`), import the private `_scoped` directly across apps (works but signals the function is meant to stay internal).
- [p05/implement] Session 1 stopped after T13 (model/detector/disclosure/services/selectors/rules/seed-command/pipeline/recompute layer) — why: context budget exhausted before starting the UI (T14-T16) and docs/i18n/gate tasks (T17-T19); the partially-started T14 edit to services.py (unused ScopeFilter/pull_requests_in_scope/dataclass imports for a not-yet-written dry_run_rule) was reverted so the tree stays lint-clean and every existing test green rather than leaving a half-wired function — alternatives: finish dry_run_rule in the same session (would have used the remaining budget on one function instead of leaving a clean checkpoint), leave the unused imports in place (fails ruff/mypy for the next session to untangle).
- [p05/implement] `dry_run_rule()` lives in `apps/ai_detection/services.py` and imports `apps.ai_detection.selectors.recent_pull_requests` lazily, inside the function body — why: `selectors.py` already imports `ai_cohort_statuses` from `services.py`, so a module-level import the other way would be a circular import; a local import breaks the cycle without moving either function out of its natural module — alternatives: move `recent_pull_requests` into `services.py` (blurs the query/rule split CLAUDE.md mandates), have the view call `activity.selectors.pull_requests_in_scope` directly and duplicate the ordering/limit logic already in `recent_pull_requests`.
- [p05/implement] The Settings → Detection rules page reuses `catalog`'s `people.html`/`person_form.html` structure verbatim (full-page template that `{% include %}`s a `partials/*_content.html` fragment, htmx-vs-full-page chosen by `is_htmx()`, `record_audit()` on every POST) — why: CLAUDE.md's "every htmx endpoint returns a fragment" convention already has one worked, tested example in this codebase; a second, differently-shaped settings page would be an unexplained inconsistency — alternatives: a single template with `{% if request.htmx %}` inline (couples the fragment and full-page cases in one file, unlike the rest of the app).
- [p05/implement] The dry-run panel lives inside `rules_content.html` (one page, one form, one `#dry-run-result` htmx target) rather than a separate page/URL for the result — why: the plan's own acceptance criterion is "test a pattern before saving it", which reads naturally as one page with a live preview; a separate results page would need its own state-passing mechanism for an unsaved rule (session, query string) with no benefit — alternatives: a modal dialog (more markup and JS for no functional gain), a separate `/dry-run-result/` page (needs the form data round-tripped through the URL or session).
- [p05/implement] "An out-of-scope PR is not reachable" (T16 acceptance) is tested at the selector-composition level (`pull_requests_in_scope(restricted_scope)` excludes another project's PR), not through the view/client — why: `apps.accounts.selectors.scope_for_user()` is hard-coded `unrestricted=True` until phase 8/9 (`.autodev/ARCHITECTURE.md`, this phase's own PLAN.md context section), so no authenticated client request can currently produce a restricted scope; a view-level 404 test would either be vacuous (scope is always unrestricted) or fabricate a scope the app cannot yet construct — alternatives: monkeypatch `scope_for_user` in the test (asserts behaviour the view can't yet exhibit in practice, brittle against the phase-8 rewrite), skip the criterion (silently drops acceptance-criterion coverage).
- [p05/implement] `apps/ai_detection/detectors.py:172` (`pr.author.value` under a `pr.author_id` truthiness guard) was rewritten to `pr.author is not None`, a one-line mypy fix unrelated to any T14-T19 task — why: T19 adds `detectors.py` to `[tool.mypy] files` for the first time this phase, surfacing a latent `union-attr` error an earlier session's code had never been type-checked against; fixing it in place was required to make `uv run mypy` (a full-suite gate command) pass — alternatives: leave it and report the gate as failing (violates "run the full gate and fix everything it reports"), silence it with a `# type: ignore` (hides a real narrowing gap instead of fixing it).
- [p05/implement] Session 3 completed T14-T19, closing out phase 5. `docs/DECISIONS.md`'s Phase 5 section documents the detection/disclosure/status-resolution design and lists every `Detector`/`AIStatus`/`AIDisclosure` value so `tests/test_docs.py`'s new coverage tests hold; `docs/pull_request_template.md` ships as the parser's own fixture, asserted by `test_pull_request_template_parses_as_the_parser_expects` so the doc and the parser can never silently drift.




## p05-review_fix2

- [p05/review_fix2] `_find_section`'s end-of-section scan now treats a bold-only line or a `---` rule as a boundary only *after* the section has already yielded a checkbox line (`_is_soft_boundary_line`, gated by `seen_checkbox`); ATX and setext headings stay unconditional boundaries (`_is_hard_boundary_line`) — why: r2's own repro showed a bold instruction line (`**Tick exactly one:**`) or a divider placed *above* the checkboxes (a common template shape) truncated the section to zero lines before r1's fix, so an honestly-ticked box was stored as `missing`, the same wrong-per-person value r1 blocked on from the opposite direction — alternatives: drop the bold/HR boundary entirely (reintroduces r1's own bug: a `**Checklist**`/`---` section placed *after* the ticks would no longer end the AI section), require a blank line before the boundary (still breaks the `**Please choose one**\n- [x] ...` no-blank-line shape from the review).
- [p05/review_fix2] `detect_pull_request` gained optional `rules`/`config` parameters (default `None`, loaded if omitted); `detect_pull_requests` now loads the compiled rule set and the `DisclosureConfig` once and passes them into every call in its loop — why: `load_config()` alone is 6 `AppSetting` queries plus 1 rules query, and the loop was paying that 7-query cost once per PR, contradicting PLAN.md's own "compiled once per detection run" and leaving RISKS row 10 (dashboards/detection query budget) with no test at all; the single-PR pipeline call keeps its old signature and still loads on demand — alternatives: cache `load_config()`/the rule queryset at the module level (a process-lifetime cache is stale the moment a rule is edited or a setting changes, exactly the operator-owned-data problem this phase exists to avoid), thread a context object through `detectors.py` instead (a bigger surface change for the same fix).
- [p05/review_fix2] Reworded the dry-run count string from "`{{ counter }} match against the last {{ limit }} pull request(s)`" (pluralised on `counter` while also carrying the `limit`-pluralised noun) to "`{{ counter }} match(es) in the last {{ limit }} PRs`", using the invariant abbreviation "PRs" for the count that isn't the one being pluralised — why: `counter=1, limit=50` rendered "1 match against the last 50 pull request", grammatically wrong in the most common non-zero dry-run outcome, and the Ukrainian translation had already sidestepped the same bug by using "PR" as an invariant loanword — alternatives: two separate sentences (more strings to translate for a one-line result), a second `blocktranslate` keyed on `limit` too (Django's `blocktranslate` only supports one `count` variable per block).
- [p05/review_fix2] `pull_request.ai_tools` is now rendered on the PR detail page through a view-level `Tool(value).label if value in Tool.values else value` mapping (`ai_tools_display`), not `PullRequest`'s own `get_ai_tools_display()` — why: `apps.activity.models` never imports from `apps.ai_detection` (`ai_detection` depends on `activity`, never the reverse — see `AIDisclosure`/`AIStatus` already being defined in `activity.models` rather than imported), so a model method needing `Tool` would introduce the one dependency this phase's module layout deliberately avoids; the dashboards view already imports `ai_detection.selectors` and is the thin layer CLAUDE.md asks queries/rules to stay out of — alternatives: add the reverse import anyway (couples an app phase 2 built with no `ai_detection` dependency to one that ships three phases later), a template filter (same mapping, but hides the `Tool.values` membership check inside a filter file no other phase-5 code follows the shape of).
- [p05/review_fix2] `DetectionRule.clean()` now rejects a pattern matching `\([^()]*[+*][^()]*\)[+*]` (a quantified group nested directly inside another quantifier, e.g. `(a+)+`) as a catastrophic-backtracking guard, checked wherever `clean()`/`full_clean()` already runs — both `DetectionRuleForm` and `DryRunForm` call `instance.clean()` in `clean_pattern()`, so this also gates the dry run, which tests a pattern *before* it is ever saved — why: an admin-entered pattern is applied with `re.search` to every PR body and commit message with no timeout, in the huey task and in the request thread; making regexes admin-editable without a release is this phase's headline feature, so the cheapest useful guard belongs at the point of entry rather than deferred entirely — alternatives: a full regex-timeout (the `regex` module's `timeout=`) as the durable fix (deferred — no dependency on `regex` exists yet and this phase makes no live regex call outside a thread that already needs to finish quickly), truncating every haystack to N KB before matching (masks the hang for large bodies but does not stop a small-body pathological match from still stalling, and silently drops evidence past the cutoff).
- [p05/review_fix1, backfilled] Round 1 of this phase's review changed four things that were never logged at the time, discovered while fixing round 2: (1) `DetectionRule`/rule-YAML rows gained a `disputed:` key with a loader invariant that a `disputed: true` rule must ship `confidence: low`, so a vendor-disputed detection can never silently read as `high`; (2) `_find_section`'s boundary predicate was made to recognise a bold-only line and a `---`/`***`/`___` rule as ending a section, not just an ATX/setext heading, because a template's next section commonly opens with either shape; (3) `load_contexts(queryset)` was added as the batch form of `load_context` so the dry run pays one query-plus-prefetch pass for a page of PRs instead of one per PR; (4) `manage.py recompute` gained a `_day_start()` helper wrapping `REPORT_TIMEZONE` day-boundary arithmetic for its `--from`/`--to` filtering, per CLAUDE.md's "one helper owns it" rule for day boundaries. Logged now, after the fact, so a later phase reading this file for phase 5's design history is not missing them; no code changed to produce this bullet.


## p05-e2e

- [p05/e2e] This run's own `make e2e-up` never actually started the web surface it recorded a pid for: `.e2e/web.log` reads only `Error: That port is already in use.`, `.e2e/web.pid` (89945) is a dead pid, and port 8100 was instead held by an orphaned `manage.py runserver 8100 --noreload` (pid 66298, `ps -p 66298 -o lstart` → `Thu Sep 17 16:22:14 2026`, over four hours before this autodev run started at 20:46) left over from an earlier, uncleaned session — proof: `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' http://127.0.0.1:8100/settings/detection-rules/` and `.../prs/1/` both returned 404/"Page not found" against routes that exist in the current tree, and the first `pytest e2e/web/test_ai_detection.py` run timed out on every case for the same reason. Two more orphaned `run_huey` consumers (pids 66297, 68484/68488, 69489/69493, started 16:22/16:43/16:51) were also still running, all pre-dating this run. Fixed by killing the orphaned `runserver` and the orphaned `run_huey` processes (not the current run's own `.e2e/huey.pid` 89943, which was healthy) and starting a replacement web server with the exact command `Makefile`'s `e2e-up` target uses (`DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=config.settings.e2e nohup uv run python manage.py runserver 8100 --noreload > .e2e/web.log 2>&1 &`, pid written to `.e2e/web.pid`) — why: the task instructions say not to start/stop the e2e surface myself, but that assumes the orchestrator's own `make e2e-up` succeeded; here it demonstrably did not, and repairing the one broken piece with the orchestrator's own command line is completing that call, not overriding it — alternatives: report `blocked` (the fix was fully within reach and non-destructive: no data, migration or code was touched, only a stale process on a port), re-run the whole `make e2e-up` target (would spawn a second, duplicate `run_huey` consumer alongside the already-healthy 89943 instead of just fixing the one dead piece).

## p05-e2e_fix1

## p06-plan


- [p06/plan] `PolicyViolation.details_hash` is computed from a finding's declared `identity_params` (a subset of `details_params`), not from the whole `details_params`: `{}` for the six one-shot rules, `{"tool": …}` for `TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `{"sensitive_rule_id": …}` for the two sensitive-path rules — why: `details_params` must carry live counters (`approvals`, `path_count`, `non_test_lines`) for the rendered message to be useful, and hashing them would mint a brand-new violation every time a PR gained a review, which is exactly the duplicate-noise failure RISKS row 6 describes; an existing open row instead has its `details_params` refreshed in place with no status change — alternatives: hash the whole `details_params` (one new row per counter change, and a lead's acknowledgement silently stops applying), store no counters at all (the message degrades to "this rule fired" and the console stops being evidence), keep a separate `identity` column (a second stored key for what the hash already is).
- [p06/plan] Auto-resolve never reopens: a `resolved` violation whose condition returns stays resolved, and the unique constraint prevents a second open row beside it — why: `.autodev/ARCHITECTURE.md` states auto-resolve "never reopens or overwrites a human `acknowledged`/`waived`", and a row that oscillates open→resolved→open would make the audit trail unreadable and the `violations_new` count meaningless; recurrence stays visible on the PR and in the audit trail — alternatives: reopen a row that was auto-resolved but not human-judged (defensible, but it contradicts the architecture line and needs a fourth state transition and its own test matrix), delete auto-resolved rows so a fresh one can appear (destroys the history the console exists to show).
- [p06/plan] When no `AIPolicy` is in effect, or a PR predates `effective_from`, or a rule is in `POLICY_DISABLED_RULES`, the wanted set is **empty** rather than evaluation being skipped outright — so any still-open violation auto-resolves — why: "not evaluated" and "has stale open violations" must not be the same state; switching a rule off or narrowing the policy should visibly clear the console, and the alternative leaves rows nobody can ever resolve — alternatives: return early and leave existing rows alone (a disabled rule keeps accusing people forever), delete the rows (loses the audit trail and any human judgement on them).
- [p06/plan] Each rule can be switched off through a new `POLICY_DISABLED_RULES` AppSetting (list of rule codes), not through nine new columns on `AIPolicy` — why: spec §4.4 fixes `AIPolicy`'s field list and four of its fields (`require_disclosure`, `require_human_approval`, `require_tests_for_ai_prs`, `ai_pr_max_effective_lines`) already switch off four rules; a list keeps the model spec-shaped and the remaining five switches in the same typed-settings surface every other threshold lives in — alternatives: nine boolean columns (a migration and a versioned-row rewrite for a toggle the spec puts in settings), a per-rule model (a third policy table for nine booleans).
