- [p06/plan] "AI PR" for the six AI-only rules is `ai_status in ai_detection.services.ai_cohort_statuses()`, the same cohort the metrics use, and "human approval" counts distinct `Person`s with an `APPROVED` review who are neither the author's person nor a bot — why: two different definitions of "AI PR" in one product guarantees a dashboard and a violation that contradict each other, and counting reviews rather than people would let one reviewer's three approvals satisfy `min_human_approvals=3` — alternatives: `ai_status == ai_explicit` only (drops every self-disclosed AI PR from policy, so disclosure would reduce scrutiny), count `Review` rows (a re-approval after a change request would silently clear the rule).
- [p06/plan] `TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED` fires nothing while `AIPolicy.allowed_tools` is empty — why: an empty list is the pre-configuration state (the field defaults to `[]`), and reading it as "no tool is allowed" would flag every AI PR in the system the moment the first policy version is saved, burying the eight real rules in noise — alternatives: treat empty as deny-all (a console full of one rule on day one), add a separate "enforce tool allow-list" boolean (a toggle whose only state the empty list already encodes).
- [p06/plan] Sensitive-path matching (`services.match_sensitive_paths`) writes `PRFile.matched_sensitive_rule` as part of policy evaluation, and the two sensitive-path rules read the marks it just wrote — why: the column was created in phase 2 and has never been written; the glob rules live in this app, `activity/derive.py` has no `policy` dependency, and having one writer means the PR file list, the console and the violation can never disagree about which rule matched — alternatives: match inside `derive.py` (adds an `activity → policy` import that phase 4 deliberately avoided), compute matches on the fly per read (the same globs recompiled on every page render, and the stored column stays dead).
- [p06/plan] `AIPolicy` is edited by **creating a new row** with `effective_from = now` (`services.save_policy_version`), `effective_from` becomes `unique=True`, and the settings page lists the history read-only — why: the deliverable is "singleton with versioning", spec §7 makes `effective_from` the cut-off for which PRs get evaluated, and editing a row in place would retroactively re-judge PRs under a policy that did not exist when they were written — alternatives: update the single row in place (silently rewrites history, the failure `effective_from` exists to prevent), let the admin pick an arbitrary past `effective_from` (backdating a policy over already-judged PRs, same problem with extra steps).
- [p06/plan] DEVIATION from CLAUDE.md's "`metrics.compute()` is the only read entry point": the console's compliance KPIs, chart series and mismatch list come from `apps/policy/selectors.py` — why: the metrics registry and `compute()` do not exist until phase 7, and this phase's deliverable includes the KPIs; the four selectors are written as the data source phase 7's `violations_open` / `violations_new` / `violations_by_rule` calculators call, so the registry inherits tested queries instead of new ones — alternatives: build a minimal registry here (pulls phase 7's headline deliverable forward, including `DailyRollup` storage rules and caching), ship the page without KPIs (fails this phase's own acceptance).
- [p06/plan] DEVIATION: the violation distribution is a server-rendered horizontal bar list plus a `<table>` text alternative, not a Chart.js canvas — why: `static/vendor/chart.umd.js` and `static/js/charts.js` do not exist and are phase 8 deliverables (with `make vendor`, runtime colour reads and a `themechange` redraw); building that for one bar chart would duplicate a later phase, while `selectors.violations_by_rule()` already returns exactly the series a phase-8 JSON endpoint will serialise — alternatives: vendor Chart.js now (pulls phase 8's chart infrastructure forward, plus a network fetch this run does not need), ship no chart (fails the deliverable).
- [p06/plan] DEVIATION: the Policy console's views, templates and table live in `apps/policy`, not in `apps/dashboards` where ARCHITECTURE puts "views, templates, tables" — why: every action on the page is a policy-domain **write** (acknowledge, waive, and the two settings pages already belong to this app), and `apps/ai_detection` set the precedent that an app owns its own settings and inspection UI; putting the console in `dashboards` would make that app import policy services to mutate rows — alternatives: console in `dashboards` with actions in `policy` (one page split across two apps and two url namespaces), all of it in `dashboards` (a read-only app performing domain writes).
- [p06/plan] DEVIATION: the violation table uses a plain `ViolationFilterForm` + Django's `Paginator` rather than the installed-but-never-used `django-filter` / `django-tables2` — why: every existing page in the tree (catalog people, identities, detection rules) is a plain form plus a template fragment, the table needs a checkbox column and an htmx-swapped action bar that django-tables2 does not help with, and django-tables2's actual value (sorting, pagination config, export columns) is phase 8's table/export deliverable — alternatives: adopt both libraries here (the first use in the codebase, shaped by one page, then reshaped in phase 8 when export columns arrive), hand-parse `request.GET` (violates ARCHITECTURE's "nothing is read from a query string without a form or typed parser").
- [p06/plan] DEVIATION: the `REPORT_TIMEZONE` day-boundary helper is extracted now into `apps/metrics/timeframe.py` (`day_start`, `day_end_exclusive`), and `recompute._day_start` is deleted in favour of it — why: CLAUDE.md says one helper owns day boundaries, and this phase's period filter would otherwise be the second copy next to the local one phase 5 left in `recompute` with a "phase 7 replaces this" note; extracting two functions now is smaller than deleting a third copy later — alternatives: a third local copy in `policy/selectors.py` (three implementations of the rule the spec singles out as easy to get wrong), wait for phase 7 (the console ships with a boundary bug nobody tests until then).
- [p06/plan] `details_params` caps its `paths` list at `POLICY_VIOLATION_PATHS_IN_PARAMS` (20) and keeps the real total in `path_count` — why: a sensitive-path glob like `**/*.py` on a 900-file PR would otherwise write tens of kB of JSON per violation and render an unreadable sentence; the count is what the message needs and the file list is on the PR page in full — alternatives: store every path (unbounded row size, and the console message becomes a wall of text), store only the count (a lead cannot see *which* file tripped the rule without opening the diff).
- [p06/plan] Acknowledge/waive is allowed from `open` and between `acknowledged` and `waived`, but a `resolved` row is refused with a visible, counted notice — why: a resolved violation is history (the condition is gone), so judging it is meaningless, while a lead re-deciding between "we know" and "we accept this" is a normal correction that must stay auditable — alternatives: allow any transition including from `resolved` (lets a lead attach a waiver to a violation that no longer exists), allow only `open → *` (a mis-click on acknowledge is then permanent).
- [p06/plan] Auto-resolve writes `AuditEntry(actor=None, action="policy_violation.auto_resolve")` alongside `resolved_automatically=True` — why: the acceptance criterion is "**every** status change writes an AuditEntry recording who, when and before/after", and the honest answer to "who" for an automatic transition is nobody, which `AuditEntry.actor` already models as nullable — alternatives: skip the entry for automatic transitions (the trail then has gaps exactly where a lead asks "why did this disappear?"), invent a system user row (a fake account in a product whose only accounts are real leads).

## p06-implement

- [p06/T3] `SensitivePathRule.clean()` rejects an empty/whitespace glob and (defensively) an empty `compile_globs()` result, but drops the plan's literal "`[unclosed` raises" test case — why: `catalog.globs.compile_globs()`'s translator escapes every literal character via `re.escape` before ever calling `re.compile`, so for any `str` input it is provably failure-proof (verified interactively: `compile_globs(["[unclosed"])` returns one compiled pattern, not an empty list); `SensitivePathRule.glob` is a `CharField`, always `str`, so the "cannot compile" branch is unreachable in practice, unlike `DetectionRule.clean()` which validates a real regex — alternatives: write a second, stricter glob parser just to have something that can fail on `[unclosed` (duplicates the matching dialect, so validation and matching could disagree), validate via `fnmatch.translate` (also does not raise on an unterminated bracket, confirmed interactively, so it would not fix the test either).
- [p06/T4-T7] Session 1 handoff: T1-T7 done and green (settings, `metrics/timeframe.py`, model hardening + migration `0003_policy_engine`, `messages.py`, `rules.py` with all nine evaluators inline (built ahead of schedule since the registry test needs all nine present at once), `services.match_sensitive_paths`). `test_messages.py::test_message_renders_in_ukrainian` is red on purpose until T20 compiles the `uk` catalog — every other test in `apps/policy`, `apps/catalog`, `apps/metrics`, `apps/github_sync` is green. Next session starts at **T8** (merge/test/size evaluators already implemented in `rules.py` — `_no_human_approval`, `_self_merge`, `_no_tests`, `_ai_pr_too_large` — so T8 is really just writing `apps/policy/tests/test_rules_merge.py`), then T9 (`services.evaluate_pull_request`/`evaluate_pull_requests`, the wanted-vs-existing diff — **not started**, `apps/policy/services.py` currently has only `details_hash`/`current_policy`/`match_sensitive_paths`), T10 (wire into `pipeline.py` and `recompute.py` — recompute's `evaluate_pull_requests` call was deliberately left out in T2 until T9 exists, so `recompute.py` still only does derive+detect), then T11-T20 (selectors, status-change services, forms, console+settings views/templates, `details_params` prose test, docs, i18n/css/mypy gate) are all **not started**. `ruff check --fix`/`ruff format` already applied to touched files; full `uv run pytest -q` and the lint gate were not run repo-wide this session (only the policy-adjacent apps), so run both before trusting a clean state.
- [p06/T12] `apply_status_change`/`apply_bulk_status_change` take an already-`violations_in_scope`-filtered iterable and have no `ScopeFilter` of their own, so "an out-of-scope id is refused" is a `BulkViolationActionForm` test (T13/T15), not a `test_status_change.py` one — why: scope enforcement lives once at the selector/form boundary per CLAUDE.md's single authorization choke point; giving the service layer its own scope check would duplicate that choke point and let the two drift — alternatives: pass `scope` into the service and re-filter there (a second copy of the authorization rule), skip the out-of-scope test entirely (loses the coverage, just moved to the right layer instead).
- [p06/T12] A resolved violation is refused via a raised `ViolationResolvedError`, counted by `apply_bulk_status_change` into `BulkStatusChangeResult.skipped_resolved` rather than silently continuing — why: the plan requires a resolved row to be "refused and reported, not silently skipped," and an exception the bulk wrapper catches keeps the single-violation path's contract simple (either it changes, or it raises) while still producing a countable, renderable result for the view — alternatives: return a per-row status enum from `apply_status_change` instead of raising (forces every caller, including the trivial single-violation call, to branch on a result), swallow resolved rows with no count (fails the "reported" requirement).
- [p06/T13-handoff] Session 2 handoff: T1-T13 done and green (settings, day-boundary helper, model hardening, messages, all nine rule evaluators + `load_context`, `match_sensitive_paths`, `evaluate_pull_request`/`evaluate_pull_requests` with the wanted-vs-existing diff and auto-resolve, pipeline + `recompute` wiring, `selectors.py`, `apply_status_change`/`apply_bulk_status_change`, `forms.py`). `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy`, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` and `manage.py check` are all clean repo-wide. `uv run pytest -q` is green except two tests pinned red on purpose until T20: `test_messages.py::test_message_renders_in_ukrainian` (uk catalog not compiled yet) and `test_css_tokens.py::test_app_css_is_not_stale` (new `.py`/template files since the last `make css`) — both expected, not regressions. Next session starts at **T14** (`apps/policy/views.py::console` + `urls.py` + `templates/policy/console.html` and the four partials, wired into `config/urls.py` and `templates/partials/nav.html` — nothing written yet, only research done: reviewed `apps/ai_detection/views.py`+`urls.py`+templates as the fragment/full-page pattern to mirror, `config/htmx.py::is_htmx`, `static/css/tokens.css` for severity colours (`--bad`=high, `--warning`=medium, `--neutral`=low), and confirmed no KPI-card or filter-form-over-querystring precedent exists yet in the tree — this phase is first). Then T15 (bulk action bar wired to `apply_bulk_status_change`), T16 (Settings → AI policy, `services.save_policy_version` — not written), T17 (Settings → Sensitive paths CRUD+toggle), T18 (`details_params` prose test), T19 (docs), T20 (uk translations, `make css`, mypy file list, full gate — fixes the two pinned-red tests above).
- [p06/T14-T17] Policy console's rolling KPI/chart/mismatch period defaults to the last 30 days (today-29..today) when the filter form supplies no explicit date range — why: `metrics.compute()` (phase 7) always takes an explicit date range, but this console has no caller-supplied default yet and needs one to render on first load — alternatives: current calendar month (arbitrary cutoff on the 1st), no default (an empty KPI section on first visit), a configurable setting (over-engineered for a value phase 7's registry will own).
- [p06/T14] The by-rule distribution chart counts violations of every status in the period (not just `open`) — why: it is a "what fired" distribution, not a live backlog view (that's `violations_open`), and the console's own violation table already lets a lead narrow by status — alternatives: open-only (hides resolved/acknowledged history from the same period), a status toggle on the chart (phase 8 owns interactive chart controls).
- [p06/T15] The bulk-action fragment's error/notice area is a single `role="alert"`/`role="status"` block rendered from `BulkViolationActionForm.errors` and a plain translated sentence, not per-field inline errors — why: the only field that can fail from this UI is `comment` (ids are pre-validated by the checkbox set, action by a fixed `<select>`), so a single visible block satisfies "never an empty 400 body" without templating per-field placement for a form with effectively one user-editable field.
- [p06/T17] `SensitivePathRuleForm`'s "uncompilable glob" test path is unreachable through the form for any string input — `apps/catalog/globs.py::compile_globs()` regex-escapes every literal character and only raises on a non-`str` pattern, so no user-typed glob text ever fails to compile. The view test (`test_bad_glob_renders_visible_field_error`) and `test_forms.py::test_sensitive_path_rule_form_rejects_an_empty_glob` both exercise the *empty*-glob path instead, which is the one a form's `required` validation (and `clean()`) can actually hit — why: matches the already-committed T13 test, which uses the same empty-glob case — alternatives: none found; an uncompilable-but-well-typed glob does not exist in this dialect.
- [p06/T19] Session 3 stopped mid-T19 with context nearly full: `docs/POLICY.md` and its `tests/test_docs.py` additions are done and green; `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`, `docs/user/handle-policy-violations.md`, the `docs/DECISIONS.md` phase 6 section and `CHANGELOG.md` are not started. T20 (Ukrainian parity, `make css`, mypy files, full gate) has not been started at all — `test_message_renders_in_ukrainian` is expected red until then (noted already in T4). Next session: finish T19's remaining docs, then do T20 in full before calling the phase done.
- [p06/T19] Session 4: finished T19's remaining docs (`docs/CONFIGURATION.md`'s `policy` settings rows and its stale "phase 7 adds" `REPORT_TIMEZONE` note now pointing at `apps/metrics/timeframe.py`, `docs/user/handle-policy-violations.md`, the `docs/DECISIONS.md` phase 6 section, `CHANGELOG.md`) — why: these were plain remaining-work items with no design decision left to make, matching the phase 5 section's depth per the plan's instruction.
- [p06/T20] `RULE_MESSAGES`' five plural entries were never reaching the translation catalog at all — `render_violation` calls `ngettext(entry["singular"], plural, count)` with dict-derived variables, and `makemessages`/`xgettext` only extracts literal string arguments, so it saw nothing there. Fixed with `messages._register_plural_forms_for_makemessages`, a never-invoked function holding literal `ngettext(singular_literal, plural_literal, 1)` calls using the exact same strings as `RULE_MESSAGES`, purely so extraction records the msgid/msgid_plural pairing and the four Ukrainian forms — Python's gettext catalog is keyed by string content, not call site, so `render_violation`'s dynamic call finds the same entry at runtime — why: matches the codebase's own established convention for dynamic-count messages (`apps/dashboards/formatting.py`, `apps/policy/views.py::_bulk_notice` both use literal `ngettext()` already; the dict-driven `RULE_MESSAGES` render path is the only place that didn't) rather than inventing a second untested translation mechanism — alternatives: wrap `RULE_MESSAGES`' plural strings in `gettext_noop()` like the four singular-only rules (tried first; `xgettext` warns "used without plural and with plural" and produces an undefined merge for the one case tested, because the same msgid then appears both as a flat singular occurrence and as an `ngettext` pair — removed the `gettext_noop()` wrapping from the five plural entries to avoid the collision, keeping it only on the four singular-only ones), hand-editing the compiled `.po` into a merged plural entry with no matching source-literal pairing (works once but a future `make messages` run would silently split it back into two flat entries per its own extraction, since `msgmerge` follows the newly extracted POT's shape — not durable, and CLAUDE.md requires generated files to regenerate cleanly from source).
- [p06/T20] `&#9432;` (the Policy console's ⓘ info-icon entity, `apps/policy/templates/policy/partials/kpis.html`) is a false positive for `tests/test_no_hardcoded_colors.py`'s `#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}` regex — a decimal HTML entity's digits look like a hex color literal to a regex that only checks digit-class and length, not context. Switched to the hex-form entity `&#x24D8;` (same glyph, U+24D8): the regex requires a hex digit immediately after `#`, and `x` isn't one, so it no longer matches — why: fixing the glyph's encoding is simpler and more honest than special-casing the color-literal test for one string — alternatives: widen the regex to require `#` not inside `&...;` (couples an unrelated test to HTML-entity syntax for one call site), use the named entity `&#9432;`'s Unicode character directly in the template source (works too, but the hex-entity form was the more minimal diff and keeps the file ASCII-only like its neighbours).
- [p06/T20] Full gate also caught two ruff violations left over from T14-T17 (an unsorted import block in a T14 test, two `E501`-over-110 lines in `views.py`/`test_docs.py`) that had escaped an earlier session because — per the T13 handoff note — the gate was last run clean at T13 and not repeated after; wrapped the long lines and let `ruff check --fix` resort the import block, no behavior change.
- [p06] **Phase 6 complete.** All 20 tasks done; full `uv run pytest -q` and the lint gate (`ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy`, `makemigrations --check --dry-run`, `manage.py check`) are green repeat-clean; `make messages` and `make css` both leave the tree clean on a second run (verified).


## p06-review_fix1


- [p06/review_fix1] Fixed the review's blocker: `evaluate_pull_request`/`evaluate_pull_requests` now judge a PR under the `AIPolicy` version in effect at that PR's own `created_at` (via a new `_policy_at(moment, versions)` picking from all versions loaded once, newest-first) instead of `current_policy()` (the newest version overall) — why: gating on the newest version meant saving any second version pushed every existing PR before the new `effective_from`, auto-resolving the entire open-violation backlog irreversibly (resolved rows never reopen, and the unique constraint blocks a replacement); the `policy` kwarg on `evaluate_pull_request` is renamed `policy_versions` (a `Sequence[AIPolicy]`) accordingly — alternatives: keep `current_policy()` and special-case a grace window (arbitrary cutoff, doesn't fix the underlying bug for a policy saved long after old PRs), re-run evaluation only for PRs created after each new version (still degrades to "only PRs after the last save are ever judged", the exact failure mode the review named).
- [p06/review_fix1] `AIPolicyForm` no longer exposes `effective_from`; `save_policy_version` always stamps `timezone.now()` — why: the form's editable field contradicted this phase's own logged decision ("writes a new row with effective_from = now", explicitly rejecting "let the admin pick an arbitrary past effective_from"); an editable field let an admin backdate a policy over already-judged PRs or silently schedule one for the future with no marker in the history list — alternatives: keep the field but validate `>= now` and render a "not yet in effect" marker (more surface for a feature nothing in the spec asks for), leave it editable and rely on the blocker fix alone (still allows backdating, just no longer irreversibly destructive).
- [p06/review_fix1] `compliance_kpis.disclosure_mismatch_count` now calls `disclosure_mismatch_pull_requests(scope, start, end).count()` instead of running its own `PolicyViolation.created_at`-anchored query — why: the KPI card and the PR list beneath it disagreed on the same page because one was anchored on the violation's `created_at` and the other on the PR's; reusing the list's own query makes them provably identical rather than independently-maintained duplicates — alternatives: anchor both on `PolicyViolation.created_at` (the list function is a `PullRequest` queryset already used elsewhere as-is; switching its anchor is a bigger change), leave them different with a comment explaining why (a lead reading two numbers on one page has no way to see that comment).
- [p06/review_fix1] `ViolationFilterForm`'s `rule_code`/`severity`/`project`/`repository` filters are now `<select>`s (`_LenientChoiceField`, `_LenientModelChoiceField`) instead of plain text inputs parsed as raw ids/enum values — why: `docs/user/handle-policy-violations.md` advertises filtering by rule/severity/project/repository as a normal workflow, but a lead had to type a numeric project id or a literal string like `NO_TESTS` to use it; the lenient variants preserve the existing "unknown/out-of-scope value is dropped, not a form error" contract (RISKS row 3) by overriding `validate`/`to_python` to swallow an invalid choice instead of raising — alternatives: leave them as text inputs (matches nothing a real lead would type), make them hard validation errors (contradicts the already-tested "dropped silently" behavior for a query-string filter).
- [p06/review_fix1] Rewrote the two near-unfalsifiable console tests instead of leaving them: `test_chart_table_alternative_matches_violations_by_rule` now regex-extracts the `#policy-rule-chart-table` fragment and asserts its rows equal `violations_by_rule()`'s series exactly (label+count), rather than checking `str(row.count) in content` against the whole page (which the pagination block's "Page 1 of 1" text already satisfied by coincidence); `test_low_sample_kpi_is_marked` now builds 3 AI-cohort PRs (below `MIN_SAMPLE`=5) so a real, greyed compliance rate is exercised and asserts `data-low-sample` specifically (previously ran with zero AI PRs, so the `or data-compliance-rate="none"` half always passed and the greyed-branch was never actually reached) — why: a test that can't fail on the bug it names isn't coverage; the review is right that both would still pass with the feature broken — alternatives: leave both as looser smoke tests with a comment (doesn't fix the falsifiability problem), drop the whole-page substring approach for a `assertContains`-based check (still can't distinguish the chart table from the pagination block without scoping to the fragment).
- [p06/review_fix1] Left one review point as-is, doc-only: `docs/POLICY.md`'s effective_from paragraph and the `docs/DECISIONS.md` phase 6 section are both rewritten to say "the version in effect at the PR's own `created_at`", matching the blocker fix; no code beyond the blocker fix itself was needed for this finding.


## p06-review_fix2

- [p06/review_fix2] Fixed the round-2 major finding: `save_policy_version` now stamps the *first* `AIPolicy` version ever saved (no prior row exists) at `PullRequest.objects.aggregate(Min("created_at"))` instead of `timezone.now()`, falling back to `now()` only when no PR has been synced yet; every later version still stamps `now()` — why: `evaluate_pull_request` judges a PR under the version in effect at the PR's own `created_at`, so a first save at `now()` governed zero already-synced PRs (every PR predates the save it was meant to be judged by) and the Policy console stayed empty forever for existing history; a version saved before any PR exists still legitimately wants "now" so it isn't retroactively broadened later — alternatives: an explicit "applies to PRs created since" date field on `AIPolicyForm` (more surface and a second way to get the date wrong, for a case the earliest-PR default already covers correctly), leaving it undocumented as a known limitation (the console being permanently empty after the very first setup step is the opposite of the console's purpose).
- [p06/review_fix2] Updated `docs/POLICY.md` and `docs/user/handle-policy-violations.md` to state the corrected `effective_from` behavior (first version covers everything already synced; every later version takes effect only from the moment it is saved) and to scope the `recompute`-after-a-settings-change advice to sensitive-path rules/`POLICY_DISABLED_RULES` only, since an `AIPolicy` change is no longer something `recompute` can retroactively apply beyond what the version's own `effective_from` already covers — why: the round-2 review found both docs asserting the pre-fix (and, for the "choose a date" claim, never-shipped) behavior verbatim.
- [p06/review_fix2] Fixed the round-2 minor finding on `test_evaluate_pull_requests_loads_policy_and_settings_once`: the assertion `len(two) < 2 * len(one)` reduces to "at least one query is PR-count-independent", which holds even if the shared load were repeated per PR — rewrote it to count only queries touching `policy_aipolicy`/`catalog_appsetting` and assert that count is identical (and nonzero) for one PR and for two, which fails if either load stops being batched. This surfaced a real, if minor, instance of the bug the test's name already claimed didn't exist: `rules.load_context` called `ai_detection.services.ai_cohort_statuses()` (one `AppSetting` read for `AI_COHORT_INCLUDE_SUSPECTED`) unconditionally per PR even inside the "loaded once" bulk path — added an `ai_cohort_statuses` parameter to `load_context`/`evaluate_pull_request`, computed once in `evaluate_pull_requests` and threaded through, matching the existing pattern for `policy_versions`/`sensitive_rules`/the three settings — alternatives: leave the one-read-per-PR behavior and just correct the docstring's claim (review's other suggested option; rejected because the fix was equally small and removes a real per-PR query instead of documenting it away).
- [p06/review_fix2] Fixed the round-2 minor finding on `violation_bulk_action` always returning the bare `#violations` fragment regardless of `HX-Request`: split the full-page context building out of `console()` into `_console_page_context()` and reused it so a non-htmx POST (JS disabled, or a stray mid-load submit) now renders the complete `policy/console.html` page with the notice/errors instead of a bare table fragment with no `<html>`, nav or styling, matching CLAUDE.md's "same URL returns a full page for a normal request" rule already followed by every other htmx endpoint in this phase.
- [p06/review_fix2] Fixed the round-2 minor finding on `ViolationFilterForm`'s `project`/`repository` fields using unscoped `Project.objects`/`Repository.objects` querysets: added `policy.selectors.projects_in_scope(scope)`/`repositories_in_scope(scope)` (both starting from `ScopeFilter`, per CLAUDE.md's single-choke-point rule) and an optional `projects=`/`repositories=` kwarg on `ViolationFilterForm.__init__` that the view now passes; direct construction with no kwarg (existing `test_forms.py` usage) keeps the previous unscoped class-level default, so the lenient-drop contract and its tests are unchanged. Harmless in practice until phase 9 turns scope narrowing on (`scope_for_user()` is unrestricted today), but the dropdown would otherwise leak every project/repository name to a restricted lead once it does.
- [p06/review_fix2] Left the round-2 nit as a comment rather than a deletion: `current_policy()` has no production caller (evaluation uses `_policy_at` over all loaded versions), but it is `apps/policy`'s designated read-side "what applies right now" helper for a future settings-page banner or metrics calculator per the phase-2 decision that put it in `services.py` specifically so a global, unscoped row wouldn't need a `selectors.py` exemption — deleting it would just mean re-deriving the same one-liner later; added a docstring saying so and removed `save_policy_version`'s stale "current_policy() keeps working unchanged" reference (the actual stale-doc part of the finding) — alternatives: delete it and its two tests per the review's other option (rejected: the phase-2 ADR's reasoning for keeping policy reads out of `selectors.py` still applies, and the helper is one line with two passing tests, not dead weight worth the churn).
- [p06/review_fix2] Unrelated to the review but found while getting the full suite green: six pre-existing `apps/policy` tests (`test_selectors.py` x5, `test_views_console.py` x2) computed "today" via `timezone.now().date()`/`timezone.localdate()` (Django's active `TIME_ZONE`, which is UTC) and then queried a `REPORT_TIMEZONE`-Kyiv-day-boundary function with it — correct all day except during the ~1-3 hours UTC evening where Kyiv's calendar date has already rolled over past UTC's, which is exactly the real wall-clock window this fix ran in, so they failed for real. Pinned each with `@freeze_time("2026-06-15T12:00:00Z")` (a instant far from any TZ boundary, the same pattern `test_violations_new_counts_inside_kyiv_period_and_excludes_one_second_before` in the same file already used) instead of computing "today" from the real clock — why: a test asserting same-day behavior must not depend on which day, or which few hours of one day, it happens to run in; freezing is the existing convention for exactly this, not a new one — alternatives: derive "today" through `apps.metrics.timeframe`'s own Kyiv-aware helper instead of freezing the clock (works but is a bigger diff across two files for the same fix `freeze_time` gives in one line per test), leave them flaky (contradicts "never weaken a test to get there" by leaving a real, if narrow-window, bug in place that would keep failing this same run). Proof this was the real clock, not a guess: `date -u && TZ=Europe/Kyiv date` at the time these tests first failed printed `Thu Sep 17 22:48:34 UTC 2026` / `Fri Sep 18 01:48:34 EEST 2026` — UTC's calendar date (17th) trailed Kyiv's (18th) by exactly the gap the six tests' `timezone.now().date()` vs. `day_start()`/`day_end_exclusive()` mismatch needed to fail.



## p06-e2e


- [p06/e2e] Built three new plans (`policy_console`, `ai_policy_settings`, `sensitive_paths_settings`, 20 cases total) and their specs against the running `make e2e-up` surface, per this phase's own "seed_e2e policy rows and the e2e specs" deferral. `manage.py seed_e2e` gained `_seed_policy`: one `AIPolicy` pinned at a fixed 2020-01-01 `effective_from` (idempotent across reseeds) plus six `PullRequest` rows run through the real `evaluate_pull_request()`/`detect_pull_request()` — not hand-inserted `PolicyViolation` rows — chosen so exactly two rule codes plus one real `DISCLOSURE_MISMATCH` signal are reachable, giving the filter/severity cases an unambiguous, deterministic set of rows; three action-target groups have their violations deleted and re-evaluated on every reseed so a previous run's own Acknowledge/Waive click never leaks into the next session (same reset shape as ai_detection's toggle rule) — why: matches every existing seed_e2e convention in this file rather than inventing a new one — alternatives: hand-insert `PolicyViolation` rows directly (faster, but then the console would show data the pipeline itself never produces, defeating the point of an e2e check).
- [p06/e2e] Found and fixed a real product bug via e2e, not caught by the unit suite: `apps/policy/views.py::_period()` computed "today" with `django.utils.timezone.localdate()` (uses `settings.TIME_ZONE`=UTC) instead of `REPORT_TIMEZONE`=Europe/Kyiv, so for ~3 hours every evening UTC, violations created after Kyiv's midnight were silently excluded from the "New violations" KPI and the "Violations by rule" chart (both period-filtered) while still counting in "Open violations" (no date filter) — caught because seeding ran inside that exact window. This is the same UTC/Kyiv day-boundary bug class `p06-review_fix2` already found and fixed in six *test* call sites (`timezone.now().date()` there); this one remaining *production* call site in `views.py` had been missed. Fixed by adding `apps/metrics/timeframe.py::today()` (the shared helper CLAUDE.md says must own every day boundary) and switching `_period()` to it, plus a regression test freezing the clock inside the exact failure window (`test_today_uses_report_timezone_not_the_process_timezone`) — why: a helper function is the one-line fix CLAUDE.md already prescribes for this bug class, and it closes the loophole for any future caller too, not just this one call site — alternatives: freeze the console's own "now" via `freeze_time` in a *test* only (would have caught this one call site but leaves the same footgun for the next `timezone.localdate()` call someone writes), inline a `ZoneInfo` conversion directly in `_period()` (works but doesn't fix the same mistake if repeated elsewhere, unlike a shared helper).
- [p06/e2e] Found, but did not fix, a design gap: `SensitivePathRule.clean()`'s "reject an uncompilable glob" branch (and the PLAN's `[unclosed`-style example) is unreachable — `apps/catalog/globs.py::compile_globs()` runs every character except `*`/`?`/`/` through `re.escape()`, so no string can fail to compile in this glob dialect (confirmed by hand: `[unclosed` saves successfully). Not fixed here because tightening the dialect to make something genuinely invalid (e.g. rejecting an unmatched `[`/`]`) changes matching semantics for every existing glob, which is a product decision beyond a QA pass — logged as an open question and as a `findings` entry in `e2e/plans/sensitive_paths_settings.plan.yaml`; the e2e case was retargeted to the dialect's one actually-reachable rejection (a whitespace-only glob, matching the model's own `not self.glob.strip()` check) instead of asserting a behavior that cannot occur.
- [p06/e2e] Confirmed `violation_bulk_action`'s htmx response (`#violations` only) never refreshes `#policy-kpis`, so "Open violations" visibly lags by one full reload after an Acknowledge/Waive click even though the database is correct immediately — left as a `findings` entry in `e2e/plans/policy_console.plan.yaml` rather than fixed, since compliance KPIs are this phase's own logged deviation (borrowed early from the not-yet-built phase-7 metrics registry) and wiring an OOB swap for them now would duplicate work phase 7/8 already owns.
- [p06/e2e] `uv run pytest e2e -q`: 53 passed (33 pre-existing + 20 new), run twice against the same seeded surface with no teardown in between to confirm `_seed_policy`'s reset-on-reseed idempotency; full `uv run pytest -q` (all green) and the lint gate (`ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy`, `makemigrations --check --dry-run`, `manage.py check`) both repeat-clean after `make css` picked up the `.py` changes.

- [orchestrator/commit] kept `apps/connections/tests/test_crypto.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.
- [orchestrator/commit] kept `tests/test_logging.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.

## p07-plan

- [p07/plan] `MetricDef.calculator` holds one of four strategy dataclasses (`CounterCalc`, `RatioCalc`, `DistributionCalc`, `StateCalc`) and `kind` is validated against the strategy's type at registration — why: RISKS row 7 (a distribution silently rolled up) becomes structurally impossible rather than a convention, since `rollups.py` iterates only `CounterCalc`/`RatioCalc` defs and `write_rollups()` raises `NonAdditiveMetricError` on anything else — alternatives: a plain callable plus a `kind` string the author must keep in sync (exactly the drift the risk describes), four separate registries per kind (breaks spec §8.1's single `MetricDef` shape and `metrics_doc`'s single iteration).
- [p07/plan] A ratio metric stores its numerator and denominator as `DailyRollup` rows under the reserved metric keys `"<key>__num"` and `"<key>__den"`, rather than referencing two separately registered counter MetricDefs — why: several ratio components (`ai_prs_merged`, `review_rounds_sum`, `test_lines`) are not metrics anyone asks for, and registering them would put internal keys into `docs/METRICS.md`, the ⓘ tooltips and the XLSX glossary; rollup rows are not user-visible, so a composite key costs nothing — alternatives: register internal counters with a `hidden=True` flag (a second visibility concept for the docs generator and every caller to respect), store the computed ratio per day (mathematically wrong — a ratio of daily ratios is not the period ratio).
- [p07/plan] `last_data_version` lives in a singleton `metrics.DataVersion(id=1, version, updated_at)` row bumped with an atomic `F("version") + 1`, not in an `AppSetting` or in the cache itself — why: it must be shared between the web process and the huey worker and must survive deleting `DATA_DIR/cache/` (which ARCHITECTURE.md declares safe at any time); `AppSetting` is operator-facing configuration with `SETTING_DEFS` and a settings UI, and a machine counter there would appear as a tunable — alternatives: a cache entry (resets to 0 on a cache wipe and is invisible to the admin), an `AppSetting` row (pollutes the settings screen and the `SETTING_DEFS` contract), a per-write key-deletion scheme (rejected already in ADR 0007).
- [p07/plan] `DailyRollup` rows are written only when `sample_size > 0`; an absent row reads back as `None`, never `0` — why: spec §15 requires "no data → None, not 0" and this makes the table proportional to actual activity instead of to scopes × cohorts × metrics × days (a 90-day rebuild over 50 repos would otherwise write hundreds of thousands of empty rows every night, RISKS row 10) — alternatives: write a zero row for every combination (huge, and `0` would become indistinguishable from "no data" on read), write rows lazily on first read (a read path that writes, and a cache that has to be invalidated by it).
- [p07/plan] DEVIATION from the roadmap assumption "`ci_first_pass_rate` and `followup_fix_rate` are registered as MetricDefs returning `None` and computed in a later phase": only `followup_fix_rate` is deferred; `ci_first_pass_rate` and `churn_21d` are implemented in this phase — why: `CheckStatus.is_first_ci_commit` is already populated by `github_sync/upserts.py` and `ChurnResult.churn_ratio` is already modelled, so both calculators are a ratio and a median over stored rows, while `followup_fix_rate` needs a genuinely new 14-day / ≥50 %-file-overlap service that is phase 10's own deliverable with its own acceptance criteria — alternatives: defer all three (leaves three holes in a registry whose completeness `metrics_doc` and the phase's own "every metric of §8.2 implemented" deliverable assert), implement `followup_fix_rate` too (pulls a phase-10 heuristic and its test matrix forward).
- [p07/plan] `reviewer_response_p50` is measured from `ready_for_review_at` (falling back to `created_at`) to that reviewer's first review, not from the review request, and its `description` states the approximation — why: no review-request timestamp is stored anywhere (`activity` has no `ReviewRequest` model and the GraphQL query never fetched one), so the spec §8.2 definition is not computable from the current data, and the description is what `docs/METRICS.md`, the ⓘ tooltip and the XLSX glossary all render, so the caveat travels with the number — alternatives: add `reviewRequestedEvent` to the sync query and a new model (a phase-3 change plus a re-sync, out of this phase's scope), register the metric returning `None` (a permanently blank card the spec explicitly lists).
- [p07/plan] `ai_active_people` is a `distribution`, `review_load_share` is a `distribution`, and `wip_per_person` is a `state` metric, despite all three sounding like counts — why: a sum of daily distinct-people counts is not the period's distinct count, top-N reviewer selection is defined over the whole period, and WIP is a snapshot reconstructed from dates; rolling any of them up would produce a plausible wrong number, which RISKS row 1 calls the worst failure this tool has — alternatives: roll them up as counters (wrong), store a per-day people set (a JSON blob in `DailyRollup.value`'s place, breaking the additive-float contract).
- [p07/plan] `violations_open` is a `state` metric defined as "violations created on or before the end of the period that are still open now", not "open as of that date" — why: `PolicyViolation` keeps only `status` and `updated_at` with no status history, so a historical open-count is not reconstructable, and this definition is exactly what `policy/selectors.py::compliance_kpis` already shows on the console, so the console and the registry cannot disagree — alternatives: a `PolicyViolationStatusChange` history table (a phase-6 model change to serve one metric), approximate with `updated_at > end_of_day` (wrong for any violation touched twice).
- [p07/plan] The metric cache key includes an access fingerprint derived from the caller's `ScopeFilter` (`"*"` when unrestricted, else the sorted `project_ids`) beside `last_data_version` — why: phase 9 turns per-project narrowing on, and a cache shared across `ScopeFilter`s would serve an admin's cached numbers to a restricted lead, which is RISKS row 3's exact failure through a path that is not a page — alternatives: cache per user id (one entry per user for identical data, and no reuse between two leads with the same access), cache only unrestricted results (silently disables caching for every restricted lead once phase 9 lands).
- [p07/plan] A `metrics.DirtyDay(date unique)` model materialises ADR 0007's dirty-days set; `metrics.mark_dirty(pr)` records the Kyiv days of `created_at`/`merged_at`/`closed_at`/each review, and `run_sync()` calls `rollups.rebuild_dirty()` then `bump_data_version()` before returning — why: ARCHITECTURE.md's pipeline contract ends the post-processing chain with `metrics.mark_dirty` and a sync must not rebuild 180 days of rollups to record one new PR — alternatives: rebuild the min..max touched range per sync (re-reads whole months when one old PR is back-filled), an in-memory set on the sync run (lost if the process dies, and unusable from the `recompute` command).
- [p07/plan] Two new `metrics`-group AppSettings: `METRICS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` (3600) and `DEFAULT_PERIOD_DAYS` (30), with `apps/policy/views.py`'s hard-coded 30-day console default repointed at the latter — why: the p06 decision deferred exactly that default to "a value phase 7's registry will own", and a cache TTL is an operator-observable knob like every other number in `SETTING_DEFS` — alternatives: module constants in `apps/metrics` (untunable without a release, invisible to the settings UI), Django settings in `base.py` (needs an `.env` edit and a restart).

## p07-implement

- [p07/T1] `config/settings/e2e.py` re-points `CACHES["default"]["LOCATION"]` at its own `DATA_DIR` after importing `prod` (which already inherited base's `CACHES` pointing at the pre-e2e `DATA_DIR`) — why: without this, an e2e run would write metric cache files into the developer's own `data/cache/metrics`, breaking the "e2e never touches your own DATA_DIR" guarantee `PROFILE.md` states for every other subdir — alternatives: none considered, this mirrors the existing `STATIC_ROOT`/`LOGGING` re-point already in that file.
- [p07/T4] `apps/metrics/selectors.py` narrows `ScopeType.PROJECT/REPO/PERSON` with a shared `_narrow_by_scope(queryset, scope, prefix)` helper reused across `scoped_pull_requests`/`scoped_reviews`/`scoped_review_comments`/`scoped_violations` (via a field-path `prefix`, e.g. `"pull_request__"`) rather than four independent implementations — why: all four models reach `PullRequest` through either no relation or a single `pull_request` FK, so the same three filter shapes apply everywhere; a fifth caller with a different path would need its own — alternatives: one bespoke filter per selector (more code, same logic duplicated four times, the actual RISKS-row-7-adjacent failure mode this phase is trying to avoid elsewhere).
- [p07/T4] `metrics.services.scope_for()` only checks `ScopeFilter.project_ids` membership for `PROJECT` scope directly; for `REPO`/`PERSON` scope it checks membership via a DB query (`Repository`/`PullRequest` joined through `projects__id__in`) rather than requiring `ScopeFilter` to carry repo/person id sets — why: `ScopeFilter` (ADR 0002, phase 1) only ever carries `project_ids`, and phase 9 (which turns real restriction on) is out of this phase's scope, so this is the one place that has to translate "is this repo/person id reachable from an allowed project" until phase 9 revisits `ScopeFilter`'s shape — alternatives: extend `ScopeFilter` now (a phase-9 change pulled forward with no phase-9 caller to validate it against), skip the check for REPO/PERSON (silently lets an out-of-scope id through once phase 9 lands, RISKS row 3).
- [p07/T7-T8 (in progress)] `calculators/base.py` gained `count_value(count)`, applied to every counter/state calculator: a zero count becomes `MetricValue.empty()` (None, 0), not `MetricValue(0.0, 0)` — why: CLAUDE.md and this plan's own acceptance criterion #6 state the "no data -> None, never 0" rule without carving out counters, and the rollup storage rule ("a row is written only when sample_size > 0") already treats a zero-count day as "no row"; keeping the calculator-level return value consistent with what gets stored means `compute()` can sum existing rollup rows and get the right answer for both "genuinely zero all period" and "no data at all" without a separate flag — alternatives: let counters return a real 0.0 at sample_size 0 (breaks the MetricValue invariant `value is None iff sample_size == 0` that T3's own test asserts, and makes an empty-period sum ambiguous between "0" and "None" at read time).
- [p07/T7-T8 (in progress)] `ratio_value()` treats a `None` numerator as `0.0` when the denominator has data, instead of asserting the numerator is present — why: with `count_value()` in place, a numerator MetricValue is `None` whenever nothing matched that day/period, and under the two-rollup-row ratio storage design (`<key>__num`/`<key>__den`) a period with real denominator activity but zero numerator activity legitimately has no numerator rows to sum, which must resolve to "0 matched, not unknown" — alternatives: assert non-None and require calculators to never return an empty numerator (impossible to guarantee once `count_value()` is applied uniformly, would crash `ai_pr_share` on any day with 0 AI merges among nonzero total merges).
- [p07/T7-T8 (in progress)] `registry.DistributionCalc` gained an optional second field, `breakdown: Callable[[PeriodContext], tuple[BreakdownItem, ...]] | None = None`, beyond the plan's literal single-field `period: Callable[[PeriodContext], MetricValue]` — why: a `unit="breakdown"` metric (`ai_status_breakdown`, `ai_tool_breakdown`, `violations_by_rule`) needs to return one value per label, which `MetricValue` (a single scalar + sample_size) cannot carry; `period` keeps returning the scalar total/sample_size compute() needs for `below_min_sample`, `breakdown` returns the per-label items compute() copies into `MetricResult.breakdown` — alternatives: overload `MetricValue` to sometimes hold a breakdown (loses the "one clear return type per strategy" property the four strategy dataclasses exist for), a fifth `BreakdownCalc` strategy (breakdown metrics are still period-computed distributions in every other respect — `kind="distribution"` already — so a new top-level kind would be redundant with `DistributionCalc`).
- [p07/T7-T8 (in progress)] `disclosure_rate`'s "valid disclosure" is defined in code as `ai_disclosure in {AIDisclosure.PARTIAL, AIDisclosure.SUBSTANTIAL}` — why: the plan's own text only glosses this metric as "ratio (valid disclosure / all)" without a literal definition to cite, and `AIDisclosure.NONE`/`MISSING`/`AMBIGUOUS` are not affirmative disclosures of AI assistance, so excluding them from the numerator while keeping them in the denominator (all AI-cohort PRs) matches every other ratio in this phase (numerator ⊆ denominator's population) — alternatives: none evaluated against spec text, since `docs/SPEC.md` §8.2's exact wording was not re-read for this one metric; the next session should confirm against `docs/SPEC.md` before writing `test_metrics_adoption.py` and adjust if the spec is more specific.
- [p07/T7-T8 (in progress)] `disclosure_mismatch_count`'s daily calculator re-implements `policy/selectors.py::disclosure_mismatch_pull_requests`'s filter shape (same `rule_code`/`status`/date-range conditions) against `scoped_pull_requests()` instead of calling that function directly — why: the policy selector scopes only by `ScopeFilter` (global access), not by this phase's narrower `Scope` (project/repo/person id), and it starts from `pull_requests_in_scope` (bots included) rather than `pull_requests_for_metrics` (bots excluded, CLAUDE.md's metrics population rule) — alternatives: call the policy selector directly and accept bots in the metric and no project/repo/person narrowing (breaks the "every selector starts from scope_for_user" and "bots excluded from metrics" rules this same phase's `selectors.py` enforces everywhere else).
- [p07/T9-T10] `flow.py::_reviews_given_in_scope(scope, cohort)` narrows PERSON scope by `reviewer__person_id` instead of reusing `selectors.scoped_reviews()`'s author-based PERSON narrowing — why: `scoped_reviews()` (T4) narrows PERSON scope through the *reviewed PR's author*, which is right for "reviews received on this person's PRs" but wrong for `reviews_given`/`review_load_share`/`reviewer_response_p50`, which are about the *reviewer's* activity; at PROJECT/REPO/GLOBAL level the two narrowings agree (both go through the PR's repository) so this only branches for PERSON — alternatives: give `scoped_reviews()` a `by` parameter (author vs reviewer) and thread it through every caller (a wider T4 API change for a need only three T10 metrics have), add a second selector in `selectors.py` (kept local to `flow.py` instead since no other module needs "reviews by this reviewer").
- [p07/T10] `review_load_share` and `reviewer_response_p50` restrict `levels` to `{global, project, repo}` and `{person}` respectively, rather than both defaulting to every level — why: "top-2 reviewers' share of all reviews" is meaningless for a single person, and "median response time" needs one specific reviewer to measure from, matching spec §8.2's own "(для людини)" note on `reviewer_response_p50` — alternatives: register both at every level and let a phase-8 view simply never request the nonsensical combination (leaves a footgun in the registry with no structural guard, unlike `MetricNotAvailableAtLevel`, which now raises for `review_load_share` at person scope).
- [p07/T11] State calculators (`open_prs`, `stale_prs`, `waiting_review_24h`, `wip_per_person`) reconstruct "open at a past date" purely from `created_at`/`merged_at`/`closed_at` against `_as_of(date) = day_end_exclusive(date)`, but use the PR's *current* `is_draft` value rather than a point-in-time one — why: no history table records when a PR stopped being a draft, so "was it a draft on that past date" is not reconstructable, matching the same limitation already accepted for `violations_open` (T8) — alternatives: add a `ready_for_review_at`-based proxy for draft status at a past date (already used for `ready_for_review_at` itself; using it a second time to fake historical `is_draft` would silently disagree with the real flag once a PR is un-drafted and re-drafted, which GitHub allows).
- [p07/T12] All ten quality `RatioCalc`/`DistributionCalc` metrics (`review_rounds_avg` through `churn_21d`) are attributed by **merged_at day/period**, not `created_at` — why: every one of them measures something only knowable once a PR is merged (review rounds to get there, whether it was reverted, CI on the merge commit's ancestry, churn after merge), so `created_at`-day attribution would count a PR before the fact it measures exists yet; this also matches the flow distributions' (T10) same choice, keeping one attribution rule for every non-counter metric in the registry — alternatives: `created_at` attribution (wrong for revert_rate/churn_21d, which are defined *after* merge), a metric-by-metric mixed choice (breaks the "one rule, no surprises" property RISKS row 7 already flagged as this phase's central risk).
- [p07/T12] `ci_first_pass_rate`'s population is CheckStatus rows with `is_first_ci_commit=True`, not the merged-PR population — a PR with no such row is excluded from both numerator and denominator, per the plan's own instruction — why: CLAUDE.md's "no data -> None" rule extended to a ratio's denominator too: a PR github_sync never recorded a first-CI-commit check for (e.g. synced before CI integration, or CI never ran) has no fact to measure, so it must not silently count as a "did not pass" in the denominator — alternatives: use the merged-PR population as denominator and treat "no CheckStatus row" as a fail (systematically drags the rate down for repos where CI observation is incomplete, punishing missing data collection rather than reporting it as missing).
- [p07/T13] `rollups._build_rows_for_day()` derives the day's scopes once (`_scopes_for_day`, one set of queries over PRs/reviews/violations touched that day) and then calls each `CounterCalc`/`RatioCalc` def's already-tested per-`DayContext` function once per `(scope, cohort)`, rather than a true single pass that buckets raw rows into `(scope, cohort, metric)` accumulators inline — why: the plan's literal "one pass over that day's rows" would require rewriting all ~20 counter/ratio calculators (built and tested in T7-T12) into row-accumulator form; reusing the tested per-metric functions keeps T7-T12's test coverage valid for what gets stored, and scoping the *candidate scope set* to the day's actual activity (not the full universe of projects/repos/people ever synced) already avoids the combinatorial blow-up RISKS row 10 worries about for a quiet day — alternatives: true single-pass bucketing (more code, duplicates every calculator's filter logic a second time, the actual correctness risk RISKS row 7 warns about), iterate every scope that ever existed regardless of that day's activity (correct but wasteful, and contradicts "a day with no activity writes no rows" staying cheap). Profiling at 50 repos/20k PRs is explicitly phase 11's task per the plan's own text.
- [p07/T13] `write_rollups()` validates every row's `metric_key` against `rollup_metric_keys()` (the literal set of valid bare/`__num`/`__den` keys) rather than stripping a suffix and looking up the base key's kind — why: the two checks are equivalent (a key is valid iff it's in that set) and the direct set-membership form needs no suffix-stripping helper — alternatives: none, this is the same check expressed with less code.
- [p07/T14] `services.compute()` fetches every requested counter/ratio metric's rollup rows in one query spanning `[previous_from, date_to]` (grouped in Python by `(metric_key, cohort)`), then reuses that same in-memory set to sum the current period, the previous period and every series bucket — why: those three are all subranges of one fetched span, so one query serves all of them instead of one per period/bucket, which is what keeps `test_compute_query_count_is_bounded_for_a_multi_metric_call` at 2 queries (the settings read for `MIN_SAMPLE` plus this one) regardless of how many counter/ratio keys or buckets are requested — alternatives: a separate query per period/bucket (simpler code, but query count scales with bucket count, working against RISKS row 10's 1.5s budget this task exists to protect).
- [p07/T1 (fix)] `pyproject.toml`'s mypy `files` list had both `apps/metrics` (a whole-directory entry, added at the end of session 2) and the pre-existing globs `apps/*/services.py`/`apps/*/selectors.py`, which both matched `apps/metrics/services.py`/`selectors.py` — mypy fails outright on a duplicate module path, so it had never actually run over `apps/metrics` despite CLAUDE.md's lint gate listing it; replaced with explicit non-overlapping entries (`types.py`, `registry.py`, `rollups.py`, `timeframe.py`, `calculators/`) — why: the glob patterns already cover `services.py`/`selectors.py`, so listing the directory too created the collision; discovered only now because this is the first session to run the full lint gate on `apps/metrics` end to end — alternatives: drop the two globs' metrics coverage and list every file explicitly (loses the glob's "any new services.py/selectors.py is covered automatically" property for every other app, not just metrics). Fixing this surfaced two real, pre-existing type errors from earlier sessions (T5, T10) — `flow.py::_reviews_given_in_scope` filtering `reviewer__person_id` on a statically `int | None` `scope.scope_id` without narrowing (fixed with an `assert scope.scope_id is not None`, safe because the branch is already gated on `scope_type == PERSON`), and `services.py::mark_dirty` reading `.values(...)` (typed `dict[str, Any]`/`object` by django-stubs) instead of `.values_list(...)` (fixed by switching to `values_list` plus an explicit `is not None` filter for the reviews loop, matching django-stubs' inability to know a `submitted_at__isnull=False` filter narrows the Python type) — both fixed rather than left for a later phase, since CLAUDE.md's lint gate is meant to catch exactly this.
- [p07/T15 (in progress)] `data_version()`/`bump_data_version()` are cached under a fixed key in the same `FileBasedCache` `compute()` reads from, not just read from the `DataVersion` DB row — why: the cache key `compute()` builds embeds `data_version()`'s current value, so without this a "second identical compute()" would still cost one DB query forever (violating T15's own acceptance criterion, `assertNumQueries(0)`); caching it in the same backend `bump_data_version()` already writes to keeps it correct across a cache wipe (falls back to the DB row) and shared between the web process and the huey worker, same as the DB row alone was designed to guarantee — alternatives: accept 1 query per `compute()` call for the version check alone (defeats the stated acceptance criterion), thread the version down from the caller instead of `compute()` looking it up itself (pushes a `data_version()` call to every call site instead of one place).
- [p07/T15 (in progress)] `MetricResult.definition` (a `MetricDef`) is stripped before caching and re-attached from `REGISTRY` on every read (`_serialize_result`/`_deserialize_result` in `services.py`), rather than caching the `MetricResult` objects as-is — why: several `RatioCalc`/`CounterCalc` fields across `calculators/quality.py` are `lambda`s (e.g. `_register_ratio_over_merged_prs`'s callers), and `lambda`s are not picklable, so `FileBasedCache.set()` on a raw `MetricResult` would crash the moment a cached metric's `calculator` is a closure instead of a module-level function — alternatives: convert every `lambda` calculator to a named module-level function (touches T7-T12's already-tested code for a caching concern), use a non-pickle cache serializer (FileBasedCache's backend is pickle-based by design, not swappable per-key).
- [p07/T16] `mark_dirty` and `rebuild_dirty()+bump_data_version()` are called unconditionally inside `run_sync`'s existing terminal `try`/`except` block (both the success path before `return run` and the failure path before `raise`), not behind a `try/except` of their own — why: a rollup bug should be as visible as any other sync bug (it already inherits the same "leaves a terminal SyncRun status" guarantee the surrounding block exists for), and the plan explicitly calls for the failure path too ("a partial sync's rollups are not left stale") — alternatives: swallow rollup-rebuild errors so a rollup bug can never fail a sync (hides a real correctness bug behind a green sync run, the opposite of what RISKS row 7 cares about).
- [p07/T17] `recompute`'s rollup-rebuild range defaults independently of `--repo`/`--project`: with no `--from`/`--to`, `rollup_from` is the earliest PR's `created_at` day across the whole database (not narrowed by `--repo`/`--project`) and `rollup_to` is `timeframe.today()` — why: `rollups.rebuild()` has no scope parameter (a day's rebuild always covers every scope visible in that day's rows, per T13), so `--repo`/`--project` can only narrow the derive/detect/evaluate step, never the rollup step; defaulting to the whole history keeps `recompute` (no args) equivalent to "rebuild everything from scratch", matching acceptance criterion #4 — alternatives: give `rebuild()` a scope filter (a much larger T13 API change with no other caller needing it), silently skip the rollup step when `--repo`/`--project` is given (surprising: a lead narrowing to one repo would stop seeing that repo's rollups update at all).
- [p07/T17] `recompute --from`/`--to` values arrive as ISO strings when the command is invoked through `call_command(**kwargs)` (bypasses argparse's `type=datetime.date.fromisoformat`, same gotcha `timeframe._as_date()` documents) — added a local `Command._as_date()` static method rather than importing the private `timeframe._as_date` — why: `_as_date` is module-private and has no other cross-module caller yet; duplicating the four-line normalization keeps `timeframe.py`'s privacy boundary intact for one call site — alternatives: make `_as_date` public (a wider API change for a single caller), require callers to always pass `datetime.date` (impossible to enforce against `call_command`'s kwarg path, which is exactly what broke `test_from_to_narrows_the_set` before T17 touched this file).
- [p07/T18] `docs.py::render_metrics_doc()` groups metrics into Adoption/Flow/Quality by inspecting the `__module__` of each `MetricDef`'s calculator callable (`calculators.adoption`→Adoption, `.flow`/`.state`→Flow, `.quality`→Quality) rather than adding a `group` field to `MetricDef` — why: the module split already matches the plan's own Adoption/Flow/Quality table exactly (T8's policy metrics live in `adoption.py` and are grouped under Adoption there too, `state.py`'s open/stale/waiting/wip metrics are grouped under Flow in the same table), so no registry change was needed for a doc-only concern, and it can't drift silently the way a hand-maintained mapping could — every metric is registered by exactly one module, so every key gets exactly one group with no separate list to keep in sync — alternatives: add `MetricDef.group: str` (a schema change touching all 39 `_register()` calls purely for rendering), hardcode a key→group dict in `docs.py` (the exact "second list to keep in sync" this approach avoids).
- [p07/T19] `make messages` also surfaced four pre-existing untranslated strings unrelated to this phase — `apps/policy/forms.py`'s filter placeholders "All rules"/"All severities"/"All projects"/"All repositories" (p06 code that apparently shipped without a `make messages` run) and `apps/metrics/tests/test_registry.py`'s own test-fixture strings ("Test metric"/its description, picked up because `gettext_lazy` calls in test source are still real calls) — translated all of them in the same pass rather than leaving them fuzzy/empty, because `tests/test_translations.py::test_po_has_no_empty_or_fuzzy_entries` gates the whole catalogue, not just this phase's additions, and `make messages -a` is what surfaced them regardless of whose task this is — alternatives: leave them and let the pre-existing gate fail (breaks the full-suite run this step must leave green), hand-edit the `.po` to drop just those four entries back to fuzzy/empty (defeats the point of the gate and would need `--no-obsolete` bookkeeping for no benefit).
- [p07/T20] `tests/test_css_tokens.py::test_app_css_is_not_stale` fails on the base commit (`5f96b3b`, before this session touched anything): `git stash && uv run pytest tests/test_css_tokens.py::test_app_css_is_not_stale -q` → same `AssertionError: static/css/app.css is stale`, hash mismatch unrelated to any phase-7 diff (verified, then `git stash pop`) — pre-existing, out of this phase's scope (no template/CSS/JS file is touched by phase 7); needs a `make css` + commit whenever CSS is next touched.
- [p07/T20] `apps/metrics/tests/test_registry.py`'s `_register()` calls mutated the module-global `_REGISTRY` permanently, leaking test-only keys (`test_metric_key_unique_xyz`, `dup_key_xyz`) into `tests/test_docs.py::test_every_metric_key_is_documented` when the full suite ran (only visible in a full run, not a narrow `apps/metrics` run, which is why sessions 1-4 never saw it) — fixed with an autouse fixture in that test file that snapshots and restores `_REGISTRY` around each test, rather than changing `_register()` itself (the mutation is legitimate registry behaviour; the leak was test isolation, not a product bug).
- [p07/T20] `uv run ruff format --check .` failed on `.autodev/phases/07-metrics-registry/PLAN.md`'s embedded Python code fences (ruff 0.16 formats fenced code blocks inside Markdown by default) — ran `uv run ruff format .` to reformat just that block; content unchanged, whitespace only.

## p07-test_fix1

- [p07/test_fix1] `tests/test_css_tokens.py::test_app_css_is_not_stale` failed on a clean `uv run pytest -q` — manifest hash (`static/css/.build-manifest.sha256`) was stale relative to source files touched by phase 7 (templates/py under Tailwind's scan globs changed, even though no visual CSS class was added). Fixed by running `make css`, which only regenerated the manifest hash (`app.css` bytes unchanged) — why: this is the documented remediation in CLAUDE.md's generated-file convention, not a test change — alternatives: none (weakening/deleting the freshness test is disallowed).


## p07-review_fix1

- [p07/review_fix1] Fixed the restricted-`ScopeFilter` leak (review MAJOR 1): `services._compute_uncached()` now branches on `scope.access.unrestricted` for every `CounterCalc`/`RatioCalc` metric — unrestricted callers still read `DailyRollup` (unchanged, fast path), a restricted caller instead calls the metric's own `daily()`/`numerator()`/`denominator()` function once per day in the range and sums the results in Python (`services._counter_ratio_value_from_raw`) — why: rollups are always written under a hardcoded unrestricted access (`calculators.base.GLOBAL_SCOPE`), so reading them back for a narrowed caller silently returned the unrestricted total; the calculator itself already composes on `scope.access` via `selectors.py`, so calling it directly per day is correct, just O(days) queries instead of O(1) for that one restricted read — alternatives: sum PROJECT-scope rollups over `access.project_ids` (the review's other suggested fix) — rejected because it needs a repo-in-two-projects dedup step and still can't answer a REPO/PERSON-scope restricted read from PROJECT rows, whereas calling the calculator directly is correct at every scope type with one code path. Also rewrote `test_compute_cache.py::test_two_scope_filters_do_not_share_an_entry` (previously asserted the leaked `3.0` for both restricted filters, i.e. baked the bug in as intended behaviour) to use two distinct projects/repos and assert the two restricted reads return different, correctly-narrowed values; added `test_compute.py::test_restricted_scope_filter_narrows_a_counter_metric_...` and `..._a_ratio_metric` as direct regression tests.
- [p07/review_fix1] Fixed `rebuild()`'s O(scopes × cohorts × metrics) query blow-up (review MAJOR 3, measured 4,539 queries for one day / 20 PRs): every `CounterCalc`/`RatioCalc` metric now exposes a `batch(cohort, date)`/`numerator_batch`/`denominator_batch` function (new required fields on the `CounterCalc`/`RatioCalc` dataclasses) that computes the metric for *every* scope a day's rows touch in a fixed number of grouped-aggregate queries (`.values(repo_field/person_field).annotate(Count()/Sum())`, PROJECT values summed from REPO values in Python), instead of calling the existing per-`DayContext` function once per scope. `rollups._build_rows_for_day()` now calls each metric's batch function once per cohort and writes whatever scopes come back, so `_scopes_for_day()` (which discovered the candidate scope list up front) is no longer needed and was deleted. New generic helpers (`calculators/base.py::batch_count`/`batch_sum`/`batch_value_over_population`) cover all 20 additive metrics' three aggregation shapes (plain count, sum+count over the same queryset, sum/count over a *joined* queryset with sample_size from the PR population) — why: a true "one Python pass over the day's raw rows" rewrite (the plan's original wording) would require reimplementing every calculator's filter logic a second time as row predicates, duplicating exactly the logic RISKS row 7 worries about diverging; grouped SQL aggregation reuses each metric's own filter chain (only the terminal `.count()`/`.aggregate()` call differs, extracted into a small number of shared population functions) and cuts the measured cost by ~92% (352 queries for the same 3-project/10-repo/20-PR dataset) — alternatives: the "at minimum" fallback the review itself offered (batch by `.values().annotate(Count())` per metric, leaving the rest per-scope) — implemented for every metric instead of a subset, since once the grouping mechanism existed it was no more work to apply it uniformly, and a partial fix would have left quality.py's 10 ratios (the largest share of the original query count) unaddressed. Correctness is guarded by a new oracle test, `test_rollups.py::test_rebuild_matches_the_per_scope_calculator_for_every_additive_metric` — a rich multi-repo/multi-project/multi-person dataset where every rollup row `rebuild()` writes is asserted equal to calling the metric's own (already unit-tested) per-scope function directly, for every touched scope and cohort — plus `test_rebuild_query_count_is_bounded_regardless_of_scope_count` (a regression guard bounding the query count for a 3-project/10-repo/20-PR dataset well under the old per-scope cost).
- [p07/review_fix1] Fixed `mark_dirty()` never nominating a `PolicyViolation`'s own `created_at` day (review MAJOR 4): it now also adds `day_of(violation.created_at)` for every violation on the synced PR, alongside the PR's own dates and its reviews' `submitted_at` days — why: `violations_new` is keyed on `PolicyViolation.created_at` (`auto_now_add`, unrelated to the PR's own dates), so a PR created long ago that gains a violation today left `DirtyDay` without today's date and `rebuild_dirty()` never rebuilt it — alternatives: none; this is a straight omission fix mirroring the existing review-day handling. New test: `test_data_version.py::test_mark_dirty_records_the_violation_creation_day`.
- [p07/review_fix1] Closed the acceptance-criterion-1 test gap (review MAJOR 2): added the four missing hand-computed cases (`lead_time_p90`/`time_to_first_review_p90` via a 5-value interpolated-p90 dataset, `pr_size_p50` over known effective lines, `reviewer_response_p50` at PERSON scope including the `ready_for_review_at is None → created_at` fallback and a `MetricNotAvailableAtLevel` check at GLOBAL) to `test_metrics_flow_distributions.py`, and added the promised `test_registry.py::test_every_registered_metric_has_a_test` gate (parametrized over `REGISTRY`, greps the six metric-test modules' source text for each key as a quoted string) so a future metric with no test fails the build instead of shipping silently untested.
- [p07/review_fix1] Fixed the UTC/Kyiv date mix in `test_recompute_command.py::test_from_to_rebuilds_only_that_range` (review MINOR): both the `--from`/`--to` command arguments and the rollup-existence assertions now go through `apps.metrics.timeframe.day_of()` instead of `datetime.now().date()`/`instant.date()` — why: rollups are keyed by Kyiv day, so a UTC-date test can look up the wrong day and flake for a few hours every evening, exactly the class of bug this phase's day-boundary discipline exists to prevent.
- [p07/review_fix1] `catalog.services.set_setting()` now calls `metrics.services.bump_data_version()` (lazy import, avoiding a `catalog`↔`metrics` circular import) whenever the changed setting's group is `"metrics"` (review MINOR) — why: several `metrics`-group settings are baked into `compute()`'s cache (`MIN_SAMPLE`'s `below_min_sample` flag) or into stored rollups (`AI_COHORT_INCLUDE_SUSPECTED`, size/review-load/stale/waiting-review settings feed calculators); bumping the version at least makes the change visible immediately for the cache-only case (`MIN_SAMPLE`) instead of waiting out `METRICS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`, and `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` now tells the operator the cohort/bucket-affecting settings still need a `manage.py recompute` to rewrite existing rollup rows (bumping the version alone cannot retroactively fix numbers already baked into stored rows) — alternatives: also auto-trigger a `recompute` from `set_setting()` (a settings-page click silently kicking off a full historical rebuild is a much bigger, riskier behavior change than this review round's scope, and phase 8/9 owns the settings UI this would surface in).
- [p07/review_fix1] Wrapped the failure-path `rebuild_dirty()`/`bump_data_version()` calls in `github_sync/services.py::run_sync`'s `except` block in their own `try/except Exception: logger.exception(...)` (review MINOR) — why: the `SyncRun` row already has its terminal `FAILED` status saved by that point, so the rebuild is best-effort; without this, a rebuild failure (e.g. a concurrent `DirtyDay` write) replaced the original sync exception on its way out, hiding the real failure from the caller. New test: `test_sync.py::test_a_failing_post_sync_rebuild_does_not_mask_the_original_sync_exception`.
- [p07/review_fix1] Nits fixed as suggested: `CHANGELOG.md` now says "39 metrics" (was "~40"); `apps/metrics/docs.py` added to `pyproject.toml`'s mypy `files` list, which surfaced one real (pre-existing, now-fixed) type error — `_LEVEL_LABEL`'s dict key type was inferred as the `ScopeType` enum instead of `str`, and `_levels_cell()` indexes it with a `str` from `ScopeType.values`; fixed with an explicit `dict[str, str]` annotation (`ScopeType` members are `str` subclasses at runtime, so this changes no behaviour).
- [p07/review_fix1] Re-ran `make css`: this round's edits touched many `.py` files under Tailwind's scan globs, staling `static/css/.build-manifest.sha256` again (same class of issue as `p07-test_fix1`, not a new one). `git diff --stat static/css/` confirms only the manifest hash changed, `app.css` bytes did not — no visual class was added or removed by this round's fixes.


## p07-review_fix2

- [p07/review_fix2] `mark_dirty()` now also nominates `day_of(merged_at)` for the PR its `reverts_pr` points at, and `github_sync/pipeline.py::process_pull_request` captures the PR's `reverts_pr_id` *before* `derive_pull_request()` runs and passes it as `mark_dirty(pull_request_id, previous_reverts_pr_id=...)` — why: REVIEW-r2 (MAJOR) proved by probe that `revert_rate`'s numerator is a property of the *reverted* PR's own merge day, which `mark_dirty()` never touched, so an incremental sync left that day's rollup stale until a full recompute; the `previous_reverts_pr_id` parameter additionally covers the case where re-derivation repoints or clears an existing revert link, dirtying the day of the PR it used to target too — alternatives: only fix the direct case and leave the repoint/clear case undocumented (the review only proved the direct case; the repoint case is the same class of bug, so fixed proactively rather than left for a future review round).
- [p07/review_fix2] `DirtyDay.marked_at` is now bumped on every `mark_dirty()` call via `update_or_create(date=day, defaults={"marked_at": now})` instead of a no-op `get_or_create`, and `rollups.rebuild_dirty()` snapshots each row's `(id, marked_at)` before `_build_rows_for_day()` reads that day's data, deleting the marker inside the transaction only if `marked_at` still matches the snapshot — why: REVIEW-r2 (MINOR) showed a `mark_dirty()` landing between `rebuild_dirty()`'s read and its delete was silently discarded (the old `get_or_create` never moved `marked_at` on an existing row, so there was nothing to detect the race with); a marker that moved during the window now survives to be retried by the next `rebuild_dirty()` call instead of the day being left permanently wrong — alternatives: a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`-style lock around the whole read-then-delete window (SQLite has no row-level locking primitive to hang that on), moving `_build_rows_for_day()` inside the transaction (would hold the write lock for the full day computation, worse for a single-writer SQLite setup).
- [p07/review_fix2] Moved the `metrics`-group cache/versioning explanatory paragraph in `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` from between two table rows to after the settings table entirely — why: REVIEW-r2 (MINOR) proved a blank line plus prose mid-table breaks GitHub-flavoured Markdown table parsing, turning every row after it (`policy`, `churn`, `ui`, `export`) into literal pipe-delimited text instead of a table — alternatives: none; this is a pure Markdown-syntax bug with only one correct fix (move the prose outside the table).
