- [p07/review_fix2] `test_registry.py::test_every_registered_metric_has_a_test` now discovers its test-module list via `tests_dir.glob("test_metrics_*.py")` instead of a hardcoded list, and its string match is scoped to `get_metric("<key>")`/`get_metric('<key>')` call sites (plus `test_metrics_quality.py`'s local `_ratio("<key>"` helper, which indirects through `get_metric` for its ratio metrics) instead of a bare quoted-key-anywhere-in-the-file match — why: REVIEW-r2 (NIT) found the old gate would pass on a key mentioned only in a comment, an unrelated list or an import, and wouldn't count a metric-test module written under a seventh filename — alternatives: require every ratio metric's test to call `get_metric()` directly and drop the `_ratio()`-helper allowance (would force rewriting nine passing, correct `test_metrics_quality.py` tests for a NIT-level gate change).
- [p07/review_fix2] Re-ran `make css` after this round's edits (`apps/metrics/services.py`, `apps/metrics/rollups.py`, `apps/github_sync/pipeline.py` — all non-test `.py` files under Tailwind's scan globs) staled `static/css/.build-manifest.sha256` again, same as the prior round's fix — confirmed via `git diff --stat static/css/` that only the manifest hash changed and `app.css` bytes did not.

- [orchestrator/commit] kept `tests/test_logging.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.

## p08-plan

- [p08/plan] Tables read metrics through a new `metrics.services.compute_many(metric_keys, scope_type, scope_ids, access, …) -> dict[int, MetricResultSet]` rather than one `compute()` call per row — why: a 50-repository table × 6 metrics would otherwise be 50 separate `DailyRollup` queries (RISKS row 10), while `compute_many` reads every scope's counter/ratio rows in one `scope_id__in` query and falls back to the existing per-scope path for distributions and for a restricted `ScopeFilter`; it lives inside `apps/metrics`, returns exactly the same `MetricResultSet` values (guarded by an equality test against `compute()` per scope), so dashboards still never touch `DailyRollup` — alternatives: N `compute()` calls (simple but the N+1 the phase's own acceptance criterion forbids), a denormalised fact table (ADR 0007's documented escape hatch, far too large a change for this phase), letting `dashboards` query `DailyRollup` directly (breaks CLAUDE.md's "compute() is the only read entry point").
- [p08/plan] `ARCHITECTURE.md` names `compute()` as *the* read entry point; `compute_many()` is an additive batching wrapper beside it, not a second entry point — why: the contract that matters is "no dashboard, chart, table or export queries a domain model or `DailyRollup` itself", and `compute_many` preserves it while fixing the query count — alternatives: amend the architecture doc's contract block (deferred: the doc's intent is unchanged), give `compute()` an optional `scope_ids` parameter (would make the single-scope signature, which every existing caller and test uses, harder to read).
- [p08/plan] Chart JSON carries CSS **token names** (`"color_token": "--series-ai"`) instead of resolved colours, and `charts.js` resolves them with `getComputedStyle` on load and on `themechange` — why: it is the only way to satisfy both spec §10.5's runtime re-colouring and `tests/test_no_hardcoded_colors.py`, which fails the build on any `#hex`/`rgb(` outside `tokens.css` — alternatives: server-side resolution of the token to a literal (would put colour literals in Python and in the JSON, failing the grep test), a duplicated JS colour map (two sources of truth for the palette).
- [p08/plan] Chart 4 (PR size distribution) is a stacked bar **per cohort for the whole period** rather than stacked over time, while chart 6 (new violations by rule) is stacked **over time buckets** with a `CHART_MAX_BUCKETS = 31` clamp that coarsens the granularity for that chart alone — why: a breakdown metric has no per-bucket series in `MetricResult`, so time-stacking it costs one `compute()` per bucket; the size mix is read as "what does the mix look like" (no time axis needed) while violations are read as "how did the mix move", so only the second one pays that cost, and the clamp stops a user-forced `granularity=day` over a year from issuing 365 `compute()` calls (RISKS row 10) — alternatives: time-stack both (unbounded cost), time-stack neither (loses the spec's reading of the violations chart), extend `MetricResult` with per-bucket breakdowns (a phase 7 contract change for one chart).
- [p08/plan] Dashboard scope URLs use integer pks (`/projects/<int:pk>/`, `/repos/<int:pk>/`) rather than `Project.slug` — why: `Scope.scope_id` is an int everywhere in `metrics`, `Repository` has no slug at all, and the existing `/prs/<int:pk>/` route already sets the convention; a mixed slug/pk scheme would need a slug→id resolution step in the one view that serves all three scopes — alternatives: slugs for projects only (inconsistent), adding a slug to `Repository` (a migration for a cosmetic URL).
- [p08/plan] `seed_demo` builds its data with plain ORM creates instead of the apps' `factories.py` — why: `factory_boy` is a dev-only dependency and a management command listed in `CLAUDE.md` must run in a production install — alternatives: import the factories (breaks `uv sync --no-dev`), move `factory_boy` to the runtime dependencies (ships a test library to every install).
- [p08/plan] The "data as of <last successful sync>" banner is a template inclusion tag in `apps/dashboards/templatetags/`, not a context processor — why: it costs one query and is only meaningful on dashboard pages; a context processor would run it on the login page, the settings pages and every htmx fragment — alternatives: a context processor (one wasted query per request), passing it through every view's context dict by hand (repetition across six views).
- [p08/plan] The canonical scoped population selectors (`projects_in_scope`, `repositories_in_scope`, `people_in_scope`) move to `apps/catalog/selectors.py`, with `apps/policy/selectors.py`'s existing two delegating to them — why: they read `catalog` models and are now needed by `dashboards` too; leaving them in `policy` would make `dashboards` import from `policy` for a `Project` queryset, or duplicate the scope logic and give RISKS row 3 a second place to get wrong — alternatives: import them from `policy` (wrong ownership), duplicate in `dashboards` (two authorization code paths).
- [p08/plan] KPI sparklines are server-rendered inline SVG, not Chart.js canvases — why: twelve extra canvases per page would each need a fetch and a re-init after every htmx swap and every `themechange`, for a decoration with no axes or tooltips; an inline SVG using `currentColor` themes itself and survives a swap for free — alternatives: Chart.js sparklines (cost above), no sparkline (spec §10.2 mandates one under each card).

## p08-implement


- [p08/implement-T2] `DashboardParams.to_query_dict()` omits every field at its default and `parse()` re-derives them from what's absent, rather than serializing every resolved field explicitly — why: that is what makes the round-trip test (`parse(params.to_query_dict()) == params`) hold without special-casing, and it is also what keeps a shared link short (`?preset=30d` not `?mode=period&preset=30d&granularity=day&cohort=all&...`) — alternatives: serialize every field always (round trip still holds but links are noisy and every UI default becomes load-bearing in the URL).
- [p08/implement-T2] Day mode forces `granularity="day"`/`granularity_is_auto=True` unconditionally in `parse()`, ignoring any stray `granularity=` query param — why: a single day has no series buckets to choose a bucket size for, and `to_query_dict()` never emits `granularity` in day mode, so without this the round trip would break for a hand-edited URL like `?mode=day&day=...&granularity=week` — alternatives: let day mode carry whatever granularity was requested (round-trip bug, and a meaningless value to render).
- [p08/implement-T2] `DashboardFilterForm` fields that read query-string values needing leniency (`_LenientDateField`, `_LenientIntegerField`, `_LenientModelMultipleChoiceField`) override `to_python()`/`clean()` to swallow `ValidationError` and keep the field's key present in `cleaned_data`, rather than mirroring `policy.forms`'s pattern of a plain field plus a `clean_<field>` fallback — why: Django's `Form._clean_fields` never calls `clean_<field>()` when the field's own `.clean()` raises, so a plain `DateField`/`IntegerField` with a malformed value would drop the key from `cleaned_data` entirely and `params.parse()`'s direct `cleaned["from_date"]` lookup would `KeyError` — alternatives: `cleaned.get(...)` everywhere in `parse()` (works, but leaves the leniency contract implicit and easy to violate on the next field added).
- [p08/implement-T3] `_counter_ratio_result()` (value/previous/series for one counter-or-ratio metric from already-fetched rollup rows) is extracted out of `compute()`'s per-scope loop and shared verbatim with `compute_many()`'s batched loop — why: it is what makes the "compute_many()[id] equals compute() field by field" contract hold by construction instead of by two hand-synchronized implementations — alternatives: duplicate the value/series/delta logic in `compute_many` (the exact drift risk the contract test exists to catch).
- [p08/implement-T3] `compute_many()`'s widened `DailyRollup` fetch (`_fetch_counter_ratio_rows_many`) is a full second helper next to `_fetch_counter_ratio_rows`, not a `scope_ids: list|None` parameter added to the original — why: the single-scope path's `rows_by_key` dict has no scope dimension at all (by design, `compute()` only ever needs one scope's rows), so widening it in place would mean every existing caller pays a grouping-by-scope step it doesn't need — alternatives: thread `scope_ids=None` through the original and branch internally (one function, but two return shapes gated on an argument that is `None` 95% of the time).

## p08-implement (session 2, resumed after a usage-limit reset)

- [p08/implement-T1] The background agent building `seed_demo` was interrupted mid-run by a usage-limit reset with no files landed; resumed via `SendMessage` to its stored agent id, which picked the task back up from its own transcript and delivered `apps/dashboards/management/commands/seed_demo.py` + `apps/dashboards/tests/test_seed_demo.py` in full — verified independently in this session (ruff, ruff format, `pytest apps/dashboards -q` all green) rather than trusted on the agent's own report, per the "trust but verify" rule for delegated work.

## p08-implement

- [p08/implement-T5] `USE_THOUSAND_SEPARATOR = True` added to `config/settings/base.py` — why: spec/plan §3 asks for counts through Django's locale-aware `number_format`, which is a no-op without this setting (Django defaults it to `False`) — alternatives: a hand-rolled grouping filter (reinvents what `django.utils.formats.number_format` already does correctly per-locale).
- [p08/implement-T7] Removed the now-unused `apps/dashboards/templates/dashboards/overview.html` placeholder — superseded by `dashboards/dashboard.html` (one template for Overview/Project/Repository, per plan §2); confirmed no other reference before deleting.
- [p08/implement-T10] `apps/dashboards/charts.py` (registry + six builders) is written and import-clean (`ruff`, `ruff format`, `mypy` all pass) but **not yet tested or wired up** — session ended on a context-budget handoff mid-task. No `test_charts_build.py`, no `views.chart_json`, no URL, no template renders a chart yet. Left deliberately unfinished rather than rushed; PLAN.md's T10 line has the resume pointer.
- [p08/T10] `_build_violations_by_rule` slices to the most recent `CHART_MAX_BUCKETS` buckets when even `granularity="month"` (the coarsest `bucket_ranges()` supports) still overflows the cap, instead of leaving `_clamped_granularity`'s guarantee unmet for very long custom ranges — why: `_clamped_granularity` can only coarsen day→week→month, so a custom range past ~2.6 years still exceeded the cap and the invariant the plan promises ("never issues more than CHART_MAX_BUCKETS compute() calls") silently broke; keeping the most recent buckets matches the chart's own reading ("how did the mix move recently") — alternatives: raise on an over-long range (a new error path this phase's lenient-everywhere convention doesn't otherwise have), aggregate multiple months into one bucket client-side (extra complexity for an edge case outside the default presets).
- [p08/T13] Chart cards are computed inside `services.build_dashboard()` (period mode only) and rendered by a new `partials/chart_card.html` — a server-rendered `<canvas data-chart-key data-chart-url>` plus a `sr-only` data table built from the same `ChartPayload` — rather than leaving chart wiring to a later task; `charts.js` re-fetches `/api/charts/<key>/` client-side for the actual draw — why: T10/T11 built the registry and endpoint but nothing rendered a chart yet, and the plan's own accessibility rule (every canvas ships a server-rendered data-table twin) needs the payload computed server-side anyway, so computing it once and reusing it for both the empty-state check and the table avoided a second, JS-only data path — alternatives: leave charts unwired until a later phase-8 task (none of the remaining tasks own it, and T23's smoke test needs something to assert on), compute the sr-only table from a second lighter-weight query (two sources of truth for the same numbers).
- [p08/T13] `static/js/formatting.js` (written in an earlier session, never referenced by any template) is now loaded in `base.html` alongside `charts.js`, in script order vendor Chart.js → `javascript-catalog` → `theme.js` → `formatting.js` → `charts.js` — why: `charts.js`'s duration tooltip formatter calls `window.prRadar.formatDuration`, and script order matters because all five tags are `defer` (execution follows document order, not load order) — alternatives: inline a duplicate JS duration formatter in `charts.js` (the exact JS/Python duplication `formatting.js` exists to avoid).
- [p08/T8] `apps/dashboards/selectors.py` counts a day's per-person activity with three `Counter`s over `.values_list(person_id, flat=True)` (one query each for opened/merged/reviewed) plus one query for `people_in_scope`, rather than one annotated `Person.objects.annotate(Count(...))` query or a per-person loop — why: a single annotated query would need three separate joins/subqueries through `PullRequest`/`Review` with different date fields (`created_at`/`merged_at`/`submitted_at`) and different FK paths (`author__person`/`reviewer__person`), which Django's ORM can express but only as a fragile multi-join `annotate` that double-counts across the joins without careful `distinct`/`Case` work; four flat queries with `Counter` in Python is correct by construction and still O(1) in query count regardless of row count (T18's constraint) — alternatives: one Person query with three annotated subquery counts (correct but harder to verify and to keep correct as fields change), a query per person (the literal N+1 this phase's `assertNumQueries` tests forbid).
- [p08/T8] `reviews_given` on the day activity table counts by `reviewer__person_id` directly (via `scoped_reviews(scope)`, not a new selector) rather than reusing `flow.py`'s `_reviews_given_in_scope` — why: that calculator-local helper exists for PERSON-scope narrowing (p07/T9-T10 decision), which Day mode never renders (person pages are phase 9); at GLOBAL/PROJECT/REPO level `scoped_reviews()`'s narrowing already agrees with the reviewer-based one, so no divergence — alternatives: import the calculator-private helper into `dashboards` (crosses an app boundary for behaviour that's identical at every level this phase renders).
- [p08/T14-T16] Session 3 ended on a context-budget handoff mid-task: `apps/dashboards/rows.py` and
  `apps/dashboards/exports/{columns,csv,xlsx}.py` are written and pass `ruff`/`ruff format`/`mypy`, but have zero
  tests and are not imported by any view/URL/template yet — nothing in this phase depends on them, so leaving them
  unwired does not affect the tests that currently pass. `write_xlsx()` in particular has never been executed
  against real rows or read back with openpyxl; treat it as unverified, not merely untested, until
  `test_export_xlsx.py` exists. Deliberately left unfinished rather than rushed, per the guides' "stop at a clean
  point" instruction — PLAN.md's T14/T15/T16/T17 lines carry the exact resume pointer (which files exist, which
  don't, what to verify first).
- [p08/T14] Fixed a latent bug in `exports/csv.py::_format_cell` and `exports/xlsx.py::_write_value`: the `date` column type (distinct from `datetime`) was passed through `django.utils.timezone.localtime()`, which requires a `datetime.datetime` and raises `AttributeError` on a plain `datetime.date` — why: `test_export_columns.py::test_every_column_type_is_renderable_by_both_renderers` exercises every `COLUMN_TYPES` member, including `date` (currently unused by any `TABLE_SPECS` column but part of the public vocabulary session 3 shipped) — alternatives: drop `date` from `COLUMN_TYPES` until a column needs it (removes a vocabulary member the plan documents), silently coerce `date` to `datetime` before formatting (hides the type distinction ExportColumn.type exists to express).
- [p08/T17] Sorting/search/pagination for the four `TABLE_SPECS` tables happens in Python over the row dicts `TableSpec.row_builder` already returns (including `recent_prs`'s, which fully materializes its queryset into dicts for `ExportColumn` access anyway) rather than pushing `recent_prs`'s search/sort back into the ORM — why: the plan's wording ("in the ORM for recent_prs") is aimed at row *construction* via `select_related`/`annotate` (already true), and a second, ORM-only code path for search/sort would make `recent_prs` behave differently from the other three tables for no query-count benefit, since the row list is materialized either way before pagination — alternatives: give `recent_prs` its own ORM-level `q`/`sort` handling in `rows.recent_pr_rows()` (duplicates the generic logic, and this phase's own RISKS acknowledges large-scale profiling is phase 11's job).
- [p08/T17] `DashboardParams.table` gates which single `TableSpec` on a page reads `sort`/`q`/`page` from the query string; every other table on the same page renders in its `row_builder`'s natural order — why: `DashboardParams` has one `sort`/`q`/`page` triplet for the whole query string (plan §1), and a page can render up to three tables at once (Overview: people+recent_prs; Project: +repositories), so without a discriminator a click on one table's header would silently resort another — alternatives: give each table its own query-string prefix (`people_sort=`, `recent_prs_sort=`, …), which the plan's `DashboardParams` shape does not have room for and would multiply every filter-bar/export URL by table count.
- [p08/T20] Nav's existing admin-only "Repositories" link (`connections:discover`, repo-discovery/connection settings) is relabelled "Discover repositories" to make room for the new lead-facing "Repositories" dashboard nav entry (`dashboards:repositories_index`) — why: same visible label, two different destinations, would be confusing and untestable by label alone — alternatives: rename the new dashboard link instead (but "Repositories" is the name spec §10.1/plan use for the dashboards page), nest one under a submenu (more UI than this phase's nav needs).
- [p08/T19] `export_table()`'s XLSX branch converts a `url`-type column's href to an absolute URL (`request.build_absolute_uri()`) before calling `write_xlsx()` — why: `rows.py` builds every link with Django's `reverse()`, which is host-relative (`/projects/1/`), and xlsxwriter's `write_url()` raises `ValueError: Unknown URL type` on anything without a scheme (a real bug caught by `test_export_view.py::test_xlsx_export_of_the_same_request_has_the_same_row_count`, not present in CSV since `stream_csv` never writes the href, only the display text) — alternatives: make `rows.py` build absolute URLs everywhere (couples row-building to a live request even for the HTML table, which only ever needs a relative path and would break `assertNumQueries`-style unit tests that call row builders without a request).
- [p08/T21] Fixed `test_shared_repository_is_counted_once_globally`: it created `PullRequest` rows but never called `apps/metrics/rollups.py::rebuild()`, so `compute()`'s `DailyRollup`-backed counter path saw zero rows and returned `value=None` at every scope — why: counter/ratio metrics are read from `DailyRollup`, not live from `PullRequest` (see `apps/metrics/services.py::compute()`), and every other numeric-assertion test in `apps/metrics/tests/test_compute.py` already calls `rebuild()` after seeding PRs — alternatives: none; this was a missing call, not a design choice.
- [p08/T18] `apps/catalog/services.py::get_setting()` now caches all `AppSetting` rows as one dict in the shared `FileBasedCache` (key `catalog:app_settings:v1`, no TTL, invalidated by `set_setting()`) instead of running one `AppSetting.objects.filter(key=...).first()` query per call — why: profiling T18's `assertNumQueries` tests found `catalog_appsetting` alone was 108 of 364 queries on a 2-person Overview render, since `MIN_SAMPLE`/`STALE_DAYS`/`WAITING_REVIEW_HOURS`/`DURATION_MODE` etc. are read once per metric per series bucket across KPI rows, charts and tables — a textbook N+1 CLAUDE.md's list-view query-count tests exist to catch — alternatives: a per-request cache (Django has no built-in one without new middleware, and `get_setting()` is called from management commands too, outside any request), a module-level dict (breaks pytest-django's per-test transaction rollback — a factory-created `AppSetting` in one test would leak into the next; the shared `FileBasedCache` is already given a fresh, isolated instance per test by the existing `_metrics_cache` autouse fixture in `conftest.py`, so this reuses that isolation for free and is cross-process safe since the cache is filesystem-backed). Fixed three existing tests whose exact-count/equality assertions implicitly assumed an uncached first read (`apps/catalog/tests/test_views_people.py::test_queue_query_count`, `apps/policy/tests/test_rules_registry.py::test_load_context_query_budget`, `apps/policy/tests/test_evaluate.py::test_evaluate_pull_requests_loads_policy_and_settings_once`) by adding an explicit warm-up call before the pinned measurement, and lowered `test_compute_many_counter_only_request_costs_one_rollup_query`'s budget from 2 to 1 (its fixture's `rebuild()` call already warms the cache).
- [p08/T22] `AI vs non-AI` (filter bar cohort toggle) translates to `ШІ проти не-ШІ`, `Preset` to `Пресет`, `Granularity` to `Деталізація` — why: no prior `uk` string in this codebase covers those three UI concepts, so there was no existing term to reuse (unlike `rework`/`revert`/`lead time`, which already have established `uk` translations this phase's other new strings reuse for consistency) — alternatives: leave `Preset`/`Granularity` as English loanwords like `churn` already is (rejected: those two are plain UI labels a non-technical lead reads every time, unlike `churn`, which is closer to a domain/brand term already used untranslated elsewhere in the app).
- [p08/T24] `direction_class()` (`apps/dashboards/templatetags/dashboards.py`) now returns one of three literal Tailwind class strings from a fixed dict instead of building one with `f"text-[var(--{sign})]"` — why: `make css` (run for this task) warned Tailwind's static scanner can't resolve an f-string-interpolated class name, and confirmed the `neutral` delta colour had no generated CSS rule anywhere in `app.css` (unlike `good`/`bad`, which happened to be covered by other templates' literal error-message classes) — a real latent styling bug from an earlier session, not something this task introduced — alternatives: add a Tailwind safelist entry for the three classes (extra config to keep in sync by hand vs. a dict already colocated with the function that needs it).

## p08-test_fix1

- [p08/test_fix1] `tests/test_admin.py::test_fk_heavy_changelist_query_count_does_not_grow_with_rows` now issues one untimed warm-up `client.get(url)` before the two measured requests — why: T18's `_all_setting_rows()` cache (added this phase) is warmed by `apps/connections/context_processors.py::connection_alerts()`'s `get_int("TOKEN_EXPIRY_WARNING_DAYS")` call, which runs on every templated response including the admin changelist; per-test cache is freshly empty (`_metrics_cache` autouse fixture in `conftest.py`), so the *first* of the test's two measured requests paid one extra `catalog_appsetting` query the second didn't, making 8→7 look like a false N+1 regression (row count actually stayed flat once both requests see a warm cache) — reproduced in isolation (`CaptureQueriesContext` query dump showed the `catalog_appsetting` SELECT present only in the first capture) before touching anything, per the debugging guide. Fixed the test, not the product: `_all_setting_rows()`'s cross-request caching is correct and intentional (documented in T18's decision above); the alternative product-side fix — warming the cache at process start instead of on first request — would need `AppSetting.objects.all()` to run before migrations are guaranteed applied in tests, which is unsafe. The test's actual invariant (query count does not grow per row) is unchanged and still enforced between the two measured, now-equally-warm requests — alternatives: clear/reset the cache between the two measured calls (would reintroduce the same first-request-pays-the-query effect, not fix it), assert `<=` instead of `==` (weakens the check — would silently tolerate a real one-query-per-row-batch regression as long as it stayed under the original baseline).





## p08-review_fix2

- [p08/review_fix2] BLOCKER fix: the filter bar's project/repository multi-select now actually narrows the page. Added `ScopeFilter.repository_ids` (alongside the existing `project_ids`, defaulting to `None` = "no restriction on this axis") and a new `apps/dashboards/params.py::narrow_scope(scope, params)` that turns a selected project/repository into a real, tightened `ScopeFilter` on `scope.access`, wired in at the four call sites that build a `Scope` from a request (`views.dashboard`, `views.chart_json`, `views.export_table`, `views._index_context`) — why: `compute()`/`compute_many()`/every scoped selector already read `scope.access.project_ids`, and the plan's own deviation note already treats reusing `ScopeFilter` for UI narrowing as correct (`params.py`'s "multi-select narrowing, Overview only" comment), so extending the same mechanism to `repository_ids` closes the gap symmetrically instead of leaving the repository select as a still-inert control (the review's offered fallback) — alternatives: hide the repository control this phase and defer it to phase 9 (rejected: spec §10.1 asks for both, and the mechanism generalizes cleanly with no new query-count regression since narrowing is a no-op whenever neither select has a value).
- [p08/review_fix2] Propagated `ScopeFilter.repository_ids` through `apps/activity/selectors.py::_scoped`, `apps/catalog/selectors.py::projects_in_scope/repositories_in_scope/people_in_scope`, and `apps/metrics/services.py::_access_fingerprint` (the `compute()` cache key) — why: those are every selector `scope.access` reaches from a dashboard read path; omitting the cache-key change would have let a project-filtered and a repository-filtered `compute()` call collide on the same cache entry (RISKS row 3) — alternatives: none considered safe; the cache key already fingerprints `project_ids` for the identical reason.
- [p08/review_fix2] While wiring `repository_ids` into `_scoped()`, found and fixed a fan-out bug my own first draft introduced: filtering `PullRequest.objects.filter(repository__projects__id__in=...)` directly joins through the `repository__projects` many-to-many on the `PullRequest` queryset itself, so a PR whose repository sits in two projects came back as two duplicate rows (caught by the existing `test_restricted_scope_does_not_double_count_a_pr_whose_repo_is_in_two_projects`). Reverted to the pre-existing pattern — `Repository.objects.filter(projects__id__in=...).values("id")` as a subquery, `.filter(repository_id__in=...)` on `PullRequest` — which has no fan-out since it's an `IN (subquery)`, not a join — why: this is exactly the double-counting the acceptance-criterion #8 test (T21) exists to catch, just one level down; alternatives: `.distinct()` on the joined queryset (works but pays a `DISTINCT` on every restricted-scope query instead of avoiding the join entirely).
- [p08/review_fix2] MAJOR fix: `PEOPLE_COLUMNS`' metric titles (CSV/XLSX/HTML) now always carry an "(org-wide)" suffix (`apps/dashboards/exports/columns.py::_org_wide_title`), not only when a request happens to be at project/repository scope — why: `TABLE_SPECS` columns are built once at import time and are not request-scoped, so making the label conditional on scope would require threading the current scope into column generation for this one table; a label that is always true (the people table's metric values are always organization-wide, per `rows.py::people_rows`'s own docstring) is simpler and no less honest than a conditional one — alternatives: make `TableSpec.columns` a callable taking `Scope` (a bigger refactor touching django-tables2 class generation, CSV and XLSX for a label that would read identically at every scope anyway).
- [p08/review_fix2] MINOR fix (spec §10.6): XLSX delta cells are now coloured green/red by the metric's own `MetricDef.direction` (`ExportColumn.direction`, set only on delta columns; `exports/xlsx.py::_delta_color`). The two colour literals this needs (`#E6F4EA`/`#1B7F3B` good, `#FBE9E7`/`#C0392B` bad) are exempted from `tests/test_no_hardcoded_colors.py` via a new `EXCLUDED_PY_FILES` entry for `apps/dashboards/exports/xlsx.py` — why: CLAUDE.md's token rule exists so the web UI's dark/light theming stays centralized in `tokens.css`, but an `.xlsx` file is opened in Excel, which has no notion of the app's CSS custom properties; a literal, theme-independent RGB pair is what "a light palette regardless of UI theme" (the review's own suggested fix) necessarily means for a spreadsheet cell — alternatives: parse `tokens.css` at export time to resolve a token to a literal (real complexity — CSS custom properties can nest/fallback — for a value that must be theme-*independent* anyway, defeating the purpose of asking tokens.css for it).
- [p08/review_fix2] MINOR fix: `project_rows`/`repository_rows`/`people_rows` (`apps/dashboards/rows.py`) now resolve `params.cohort` the same way `recent_pr_rows()` already did (`ai`/`non_ai` pass through, `compare` and anything else fall back to `Cohort.ALL` — a single table cell can't render two cohorts side by side) via a shared `_resolve_table_cohort()` helper — why: before this fix, switching the filter bar's cohort moved the KPI row, the charts and `recent_prs`, but silently left the projects/repositories/people tables on all-PR numbers, so two halves of one page described different populations with no visual indication — alternatives: label the metric tables as always-all-PR and leave the cohort filter not apply there (rejected: the review's own preferred fix, and cheaper than the labelling alternative since `compute_many()` already accepts a `cohort` argument).
- [p08/review_fix2] Process note, not a design decision: while chasing this step's translation freshness, I ran `git checkout -- locale/` to "revert" a bad `make messages` regeneration, without first checking that `locale/` carried real uncommitted phase-8 translation work (it did — `git status` showed it modified relative to HEAD, same as every other phase-8 file this whole review-fix step touches) — the checkout silently discarded that work back to the pre-phase-8 commit. Recovered in full: an earlier, unrelated `git stash`/`git stash pop` (done minutes prior, for an isolated mypy check) had briefly created a stash commit; `git stash pop` removes the `refs/stash` ref but not the underlying commit object, so `git fsck --unreachable` still listed it, and `git show <hash>:locale/uk/LC_MESSAGES/django.po` (etc.) recovered the exact pre-checkout content, verified against the full test suite before continuing. No data lost in the end, but logged per CLAUDE.md's git-safety protocol ("run git status before any command that could discard uncommitted work") — the protocol was violated here (no `git status` check, no stash, before a checkout on a path known to carry pending work) and the recovery was luck (an incidental recent stash), not a safety net that would exist in general.
- [p08/review_fix2_audit1] MAJOR fix (round 2 audit): the prior round's "(org-wide)" fix (immediately above) argued the marker could stay unconditional because "the people table's metric values are always organization-wide" — that premise was already false in the same diff, since the BLOCKER fix (`narrow_scope()`) makes `compute_many(ScopeType.PERSON, scope.access, ...)` genuinely read the filter bar's project/repository narrowing off `scope.access`. Reverted the unconditional suffix: `rows.people_values_are_org_wide(scope, params)` now returns `True` only when `scope.scope_type != GLOBAL` (the row set is narrowed to active people in that scope) **and** neither `params.project_ids` nor `params.repository_ids` is set (so `scope.access` itself carries no project/repository narrowing) — `apps/dashboards/exports/columns.py::export_columns()` builds the "(org-wide)"-suffixed `PEOPLE_COLUMNS` variant per request from this, and `partials/table.html`'s on-screen note now checks the same two conditions instead of `scope_type != "global"` alone — why: a project- or repository-filtered people export/table must not claim "org-wide" over numbers that are, in fact, narrowed; the alternative (thread `Scope` into column generation for every table) was rejected in the prior round as too big a refactor, but a request-time column-builder for the one table that needs it (`_people_columns(org_wide: bool)`) is the same cost the prior round already paid to build `_org_wide_title` — alternatives: drop the marker entirely and rely on the note alone (the CSV/XLSX file has no note, which is the exact gap the prior MAJOR fix existed to close).
- [p08/review_fix2_audit1] MINOR fix (round 2 audit): `apps/metrics/services.py::_access_fingerprint` now fingerprints `project_ids=None` ("no restriction on this axis") and `project_ids=frozenset()` ("restricted to nothing on this axis") differently (`_axis_fingerprint`: `"*"` vs `"-"` vs a sorted id list) — why: `narrow_scope()` can produce either (a repository-only filter leaves `project_ids=None`; an intersection with an already-restricted access can resolve to `frozenset()` when the picked project isn't in the caller's access), and the old code rendered both as `''` via `access.project_ids or frozenset()`, so two `ScopeFilter`s meaning "all projects" and "no projects" could share a cache key — not reachable today (`scope_for_user()` is still the unrestricted phase-1 stub) but live the moment phase 9 turns per-user narrowing on — alternatives: none; this is the same class of bug the existing `project_ids`/cache-key fingerprinting already guards against for the non-ambiguous case.
- [p08/review_fix2_audit1] MINOR fix (round 2 audit, second half): narrowed `tests/test_no_hardcoded_colors.py`'s colour-literal exemption for `apps/dashboards/exports/xlsx.py` from the whole file (`EXCLUDED_PY_FILES`) to the two literal-carrying lines themselves, marked with a trailing `# color-literal-exempt` comment the test greps for — why: the whole-file exemption would have silently covered any future colour literal added anywhere else in that file, not just `_DELTA_GOOD_FORMAT`/`_DELTA_BAD_FORMAT` — alternatives: none; the marker-per-line approach costs two comments and closes the gap completely.
- [p08/review_fix2_audit1] Catch-up logging only (round 2 audit MINOR #3: three round-1-review fixes were implemented in code but never recorded in this file, only in code comments/docstrings) — no code changed for these three, they were already correct: (1) `AppSetting` cache invalidation (`apps/catalog/services.py::invalidate_settings_cache`) is wired to the model's own `post_save`/`post_delete` signals in `CatalogConfig.ready()`, not to `set_setting()` alone, so a Django-admin edit invalidates the cache too — why: `set_setting()` was the only caller found by round 1's review, and the admin bypasses it entirely — alternatives: override `AppSettingAdmin.save_model()` (misses `loaddata`/direct-ORM edits the signal catches for free). (2) `EXPORT_SYNC_MAX_ROWS` is read in `views.export_table()` and an over-cap export logs a warning with the row count rather than truncating or blocking — why: no background-export path exists yet (phase 9's `ExportJob`), so refusing or truncating would break the "every matching row" acceptance criterion for the one case that most needs it — alternatives: hard-cap the export (breaks acceptance criterion #5 for a caller who legitimately has that many rows). (3) `apps/catalog/selectors.py::projects_in_scope/repositories_in_scope` gained an `include_inactive: bool = False` parameter, and `apps/policy/selectors.py`'s delegating versions pass `include_inactive=True` — why: round 1 found the new canonical selectors silently dropped archived projects/repositories from the Policy console's filter dropdowns, a behaviour change from what they replaced — alternatives: keep the active-only filter and accept the Policy console regression (rejected by round 1's review).



- [orchestrator/commit] kept `apps/connections/tests/test_crypto.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.
- [orchestrator/commit] kept `tests/test_logging.py` out of the commit — it contains what looks like a GitHub token. It is still in the working tree: gitignore it if it does not belong here, or commit it yourself if it does.

## p09-plan

- [p09/plan] `scope_for_user()` returns `unrestricted=True` when a user has no `UserProjectAccess` rows — for every user including a superuser or `admin`, and returns an empty-grant restricted filter for `AnonymousUser` — why: spec §11 makes the restriction a grant list rather than a role, so a grant on a superuser must actually restrict them, while an unauthenticated caller reaching a selector (a mis-wired view, a management command) must see nothing rather than everything — alternatives: exempt superusers/admins (would make a deliberate grant silently inert and the isolation tests unrepresentative), treat anonymous as unrestricted (fails open).
- [p09/plan] `scope_for_user()` memoises its `ScopeFilter` on the `User` instance (`user._pr_radar_scope`) — why: a dashboard page calls it a dozen times across views, selectors and `compute()`, and the memo bounds that to one query per request without introducing a cross-request cache that could outlive a revoked grant (RISKS row 10 vs row 3) — alternatives: no cache (an extra query per selector call), a request-level cache in middleware (needs a request object in a function whose whole point is that it takes only a user), a timed cache (a revoked grant would keep working).
- [p09/plan] The person page compares against the person's **primary project** median and the **organization** median, both via `metrics.compute()` at PROJECT/GLOBAL scope; `Person.team` is displayed but gets no baseline of its own — why: spec §10.3 asks for "проєкту/команди", and the only cheap team baseline would be a median of per-person medians, which ADR 0007 and RISKS row 1 forbid; an honest team median needs a `team` dimension in `Scope` (and in `DailyRollup` for counters), which is disproportionate here — alternatives: median of per-person medians (statistically wrong on the page whose numbers land in a 1:1), add `Scope.team` narrowing now (silently unnarrowed for every counter/ratio metric that reads rollups), drop the comparison block (loses the page's stated purpose).
- [p09/plan] `exports/reports.py` opens its workbook with `constant_memory=False, in_memory=True`, while table exports keep `constant_memory=True` — why: the report writes seven sheets and anchors native Excel charts against already-written ranges, which needs the sheets to stay addressable; its size is bounded by `EXPORT_SYNC_MAX_ROWS` or it runs as a background job, so the memory trade is paid where it is affordable — alternatives: constant_memory for reports too (charts and multi-sheet assembly become fragile), no native charts (spec §10.6 mandates them for throughput, AI share and lead time).
- [p09/plan] The PR list's filters (author, state, AI status, tool, size, has-violations) live on `DashboardParams` as a `PRFilters` member rather than in a separate per-view form state — why: `DashboardParams` is already the single owner of query-string state, and `TableSpec.row_builder(scope, params)`/`tables.full_rows()`/`views.export_table` all take it, so the filters reach CSV, XLSX and the report with no extra plumbing and the export cannot drift from the on-screen table — alternatives: a separate filter object threaded through every export call site (four more places to forget one), filtering in the view before the row builder (the export view would then need its own copy).
- [p09/plan] `charts.py` gains `DASHBOARD_CHART_KEYS`/`REVIEWS_CHART_KEYS` and `services.build_dashboard()` iterates the former instead of the whole `CHART_REGISTRY` — why: the Reviews page's reviewer-load chart must be reachable through the existing `/api/charts/<key>/` endpoint and `charts.js`, but must not appear on every dashboard page, and a per-page key list is clearer than a `levels=frozenset()` sentinel — alternatives: a second registry (duplicates the endpoint and the JS wiring), a `pages` field on `ChartSpec` (more surface for one call site).
- [p09/plan] An `ExportJob` file is downloadable only by its author and that refusal is **403**, whereas an out-of-scope project id in a query string stays a silent drop — why: the two are different questions — an export job is an object the caller addressed by id and does not own (an explicit, explainable refusal), while a filter value is input to be sanitised (ARCHITECTURE line 251: dropping it must not leak that the id exists) — alternatives: 404 for the export (hides a real ownership error from the user), 403 for out-of-scope filter ids (leaks existence and breaks a shared dashboard link).
- [p09/plan] A background export re-resolves `scope_for_user(job.user)` when the task runs rather than storing the resolved `ScopeFilter` in `ExportJob.params` — why: a grant revoked between enqueue and run must be honoured, and a serialised filter would be a second, stale copy of the authorization decision outside the choke point (RISKS row 3) — alternatives: store the resolved ids (stale grants produce a leaked file), re-check at download time only (the file already contains the data by then).
- [p09/plan] `ExportJob.file` uses a dedicated `FileSystemStorage(location=DATA_DIR/"exports")`, not `MEDIA_ROOT` — why: no URL should ever be able to serve an export directly; every download goes through the author-only view — alternatives: `MEDIA_ROOT` + `MEDIA_URL` (a static/media handler could serve another user's file), storing bytes in the database (bloats the SQLite file the dashboards read from, RISKS row 14).
- [p09/plan] The review heat map is a server-rendered HTML table using five new `--heat-0…--heat-4` tokens with separate light and dark scales, not a Chart.js chart — why: vanilla Chart.js has no matrix type, and spec §10.5 requires a per-theme heat scale plus non-colour-only status, which a token-driven table gives directly while keeping the no-hardcoded-colour grep test green — alternatives: the chartjs-matrix plugin (a second vendored asset for one view), an SVG built in JS (colours would have to be read at runtime for a static table).
- [p09/plan] `pr_detail.pr_metrics()` computes single-PR durations and counters directly from the `PullRequest` row instead of going through `metrics.compute()` — why: `compute()` is the entry point for *aggregates* over a scope and period; a per-row derived value is not one (the same boundary `dashboards/rows.py` and the Day-mode selectors already use) — alternatives: register per-PR metrics in the registry (the registry's contract is scope+period aggregates; `MIN_SAMPLE`, rollups and cohorts are all meaningless for one row).

## p09-implement

- [p09/T1] `scope_for_user()` real implementation lands as planned (grant list, anonymous⇒restricted-empty, per-request memo) — why: matches PLAN.md design exactly, no deviation — alternatives: none considered, plan was unambiguous.
- [p09/T1-T2] `scope_for_user(None)` used to mean "unrestricted" under the phase-1 stub; real T1 semantics make it restricted-empty (anonymous). Updated all such call sites in existing tests (apps/activity/tests/test_selectors.py, apps/dashboards/tests/{test_kpis,test_query_counts,test_charts_build,test_tables,test_scope_aggregation,test_charts_api,test_filter_narrowing}.py) to build `ScopeFilter(unrestricted=True)` directly instead of routing through a fake anonymous user — why: those tests want "no access restriction" for unrelated selector/chart/table assertions, not a test of `scope_for_user` itself — alternatives: pass a real unrestricted user fixture through the DB (extra query, no behavioural difference, `ScopeFilter(unrestricted=True)` is what they actually meant).
- [p09/T2] `assertNumQueries` pins in `apps/dashboards/tests/test_query_counts.py` bumped by exactly 1 for every full-page view (Overview 274→275, Project 275→276, Repository 257→258, Day 24→25, Projects index 24→25, Repositories index 24→25) — why: real `scope_for_user()` now issues one `UserProjectAccess` query per request (memoised, so exactly one regardless of how many times a page calls it) where the phase-1 stub issued zero — alternatives: none, this is the intended cost of turning on access control (RISKS row 10 budget still holds, +1 query is well inside it).
- [p09/verify] `uv run ruff format --check .` flags one file: `.autodev/phases/09-people-prs-and-access/PLAN.md` (ruff 0.16.8 formats Python code fences embedded in Markdown). Confirmed pre-existing via `git stash && uv run ruff format --check .` on the unmodified tree — same single file, same result, before any T1-T27 code change this session — why: not code, not written by this implement step (the plan step produced it), reformatting its illustrative snippets is out of this step's scope — alternatives: `uv run ruff format .autodev/phases/09-people-prs-and-access/PLAN.md` (would rewrite illustrative pseudocode/comments for no behavioural gain and blur plan/implementation boundaries), add `.autodev` to `pyproject.toml`'s `extend-exclude` (broader config change affecting every future phase's plan docs, left for a maintainer to decide rather than a phase-9 session).

## p09-implement (session 2)

- [p09/T7] `person.build_comparison()` excludes `reviewer_response_p50` from the project/organization `compute()` calls (it is registered `levels=frozenset({ScopeType.PERSON})` only) and reports `None` baselines for it instead of calling `compute()` at an unsupported level — why: `require_available_at_level()` raises `MetricNotAvailableAtLevel` for any other key list shared across all three scopes, discovered by reading `apps/metrics/calculators/flow.py` before writing the function rather than at test time — alternatives: register a project/org variant of the metric (no such aggregate is meaningful — reviewer response time only exists per reviewer), catch-and-ignore the exception per key (hides a real "not available" distinction the registry already encodes).
- [p09/T7] `ComparisonRow` fields match PLAN.md's dataclass shape exactly (`metric`, `person_value`, `person_sample`, `project_value`, `org_value`, `previous_value`, `below_min_sample`) rather than embedding the full `MetricResult` objects — why: the page only ever needs these seven scalars and the plan already specified this shape; `below_min_sample`/`previous_value` are read straight off the person-level `MetricResult` computed once, not recomputed — alternatives: expose the raw `MetricResult` per scope (three objects per row for a template that uses seven fields).
- [p09/T8] The notes audit entry stores `{"length": N}` before/after only, no diff marker beyond that — why: PLAN.md's "truncated diff marker" was underspecified and any marker built from the two note strings (word-level diff, changed-line count) risks leaking enough shape of private text to reconstruct fragments of it; a length pair already proves "a write happened and roughly how much changed" without that risk — alternatives: a Levenshtein-distance number (still content-shaped), a truncated unified diff (explicitly what CLAUDE.md's "never a rendered message" rules out for structured audit fields).
- [p09/T9] `PRFilters.tools` filters via `Q(ai_tools__contains=[tool])` OR-ed per requested tool (Django JSONField `contains` lookup, list-containment semantics) rather than a Python-side post-filter — why: `PullRequest.ai_tools` is a JSONField list and the project is ORM-only specifically so `DATABASE_URL` can point at PostgreSQL later (CLAUDE.md); `contains` is the one JSONField lookup Django implements equivalently on SQLite (via the JSON1 extension, present in the pinned SQLite 3.49.1) and PostgreSQL — alternatives: `queryset.filter(pk__in=[pr.pk for pr in queryset if set(tools) & set(pr.ai_tools)])` (defeats the point of a queryset-returning `apply()`, forces every export/report call site to materialize the whole table first).
- [p09/T9] `params.parse()` gained a `people=` kwarg (alongside the existing `projects=`/`repositories=`) threaded into every one of `views.py`'s four `params_module.parse()` call sites — why: `PullRequestFilterForm`'s author field needs a scoped `Person` queryset the same way `DashboardFilterForm`'s project/repository fields do, and `DashboardParams` is the single query-string owner (CLAUDE.md) so every page must build the same `pr_filters` correctly even before the PR list page (T11) exists, or a bookmarked/shared PR-list export URL touched by a non-PR page would silently drop its author filter — alternatives: a second `parse_pr_filters()` called only by the PR list/export views (two functions building one `DashboardParams`, easy to call out of step).
- [p09/verify] Full `apps/dashboards` suite (272 tests) green, `ruff check`, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` and `manage.py check` all clean after T1-T9. Full-repo `uv run pytest -q` and `uv run mypy` not re-run this session (each dashboards-app run alone took 110-280s under the sandbox's CPU limit) — next session should run the full suite once before T10's own verification, per PLAN.md's "Before finishing" step.

## p09-implement

- [p09/T10] `PRFilters.apply()`'s tool filter switched from `ai_tools__contains=[tool]` to `ai_tools__icontains=f'"{tool}"'` — why: SQLite (`connection.features.supports_json_field_contains` is False here) raises `NotSupportedError` on JSON containment lookups, and T9's own tests never exercised the tools branch on a real DB; the quoted-substring match is exact since no `Tool` enum value is a JSON-quoted substring of another — alternatives: switch the dev DB to PostgreSQL (out of scope, spec keeps SQLite as the default store), filter in Python after the query (loses the "one place the filters touch the ORM" contract `pr_filters.py` documents).
- [p09/T16] `exports/xlsx.py` split into `open_workbook()`/`write_sheet()`/`write_xlsx()` exactly per PLAN.md's signatures, no behaviour change — why: matches plan design, `write_xlsx()` is now a two-line wrapper — alternatives: none considered, plan was unambiguous. Verified: existing `test_export_xlsx.py` (8 tests) unchanged and green, plus one new test writing two sheets into one workbook via `open_workbook()`/`write_sheet()` directly.
- [p09/T17-T18] `apps/dashboards/services._TABLE_KEYS_BY_LEVEL` renamed to public `TABLE_KEYS_BY_LEVEL` so `exports/reports.py` can reuse the level→table-sheet mapping instead of duplicating it — why: the report's own table sheets (Projects/Repositories/People) must stay in step with the dashboard page's table set per level, and duplicating the dict risks the two drifting — alternatives: a private cross-module import (works in Python but hides the shared-contract intent), a second hardcoded mapping in `reports.py` (drift risk PLAN.md explicitly warns against elsewhere).
- [p09/T17-T18] `build_report()` code for both T17 and T18 written in one pass (one cohesive function reading Summary/Trends/table sheets through PRs/Violations/Metrics/Parameters) but **no test file exists yet** — a throwaway smoke test confirmed it runs without crashing on a GLOBAL scope and produces a well-formed `.xlsx` (`PK` zip header), then was deleted (scratch, not committed) — why: ran out of session budget before writing the real `test_reports.py` assertions PLAN.md's T17/T18 criteria call for (sheet names/order, a Summary value equal to a direct `compute()` call, `ws._charts` non-empty on Trends, the five-level × two-mode sheet-completeness matrix, Parameters sheet fields) — alternatives: none, this is a stopping point, not a design choice; next session should treat every design choice in this commit as unverified until that test file exists and passes.
- [p09/verify] This session (T16 + T17/T18 code) verified narrowly, not broadly: `uv run pytest apps/dashboards/tests/test_export_xlsx.py -q` green (9/9, incl. the new two-sheet test); `uv run pytest apps/dashboards --collect-only -q` succeeds (proves every module under `apps/dashboards`, including the new `reports.py`, imports cleanly under Django settings — no syntax/import errors); a throwaway `build_report()` smoke test passed once then was deleted (scratch, not committed); `uv run ruff format`/`ruff check` clean on every file touched this session. A broader `uv run pytest apps/dashboards apps/accounts apps/catalog -q` was started in the background to confirm nothing else regressed (T1-T16's own suites) but did not finish before this session's context ran out — next session must run it (or the full `uv run pytest -q`) before trusting the tree, per this phase's own "Before finishing" rule; it was not skipped by choice, just not observed to completion.
- [p09/T17-T18] `apps/dashboards/tests/test_reports.py` written per plan criteria (sheet names/order per level, native Excel charts on Trends, a Summary value equal to a direct `compute()` call, Parameters sheet fields, `report_filename()`); found and fixed a real crash while writing it — why: `build_report()` with no `base_url` (the T22 background-job call shape) left a `url`-typed cell holding an app-relative href (e.g. `/projects/1/`) untouched, and `xlsxwriter`'s `write_url()` raises `ValueError: Unknown URL type` on anything without a scheme or `internal:`/`mailto:` prefix — every report with a Projects/Repositories/People/PRs/Violations sheet would have crashed the moment `base_url=""`. Fixed in `exports/xlsx.py::_write_value()`: a `url` column now writes a plain-text cell (still the display text, matching the module's own documented intent) when the href isn't absolute, instead of calling `write_url()` unconditionally — alternatives: force every report caller to pass a `base_url` (T19's sync view can, but the background task in T22 has no request to build one from, so `base_url=""` is a real, intended call shape, not a bug to route around), absolutize with a hardcoded scheme+host default (invents a URL that may not resolve for the reader). The five-level × two-mode matrix in the plan's acceptance table narrows to the four `ScopeType` levels tested (Global/Project/Repo/Person) since Policy is not a `Scope` level yet — T19's job.
- [p09/verify] `uv run pytest apps/dashboards/tests/test_reports.py apps/dashboards/tests/test_export_xlsx.py apps/dashboards/tests/test_export_view.py -q` green (31/31) after the `_write_value` fix.
- [p09/T19] `views.export_report()` is wired only for the four `ScopeType`-based pages (Overview/Project/Repository/Person via the shared `dashboard()` view) — why: Policy's console filters through `ViolationFilterForm`, not `DashboardParams`/`Scope` (T17-18's session already flagged this gap), and mapping its filters onto a `Scope`/`DashboardParams` pair is a real design decision, not something to guess at while wiring a button — alternatives: force Policy's filters through `DashboardFilterForm` too (a bigger, unreviewed change to the console this session didn't scope), skip the button everywhere until Policy is ready (delays the other four pages for one deferred one). The "Download report (XLSX)" link lives in `filter_bar.html` behind a new `show_report_link` context flag (only `dashboard()` sets it — not `reviews_page`/`pull_requests_index`/the index pages, which share the same partial), built by a new `{% report_url %}` template tag in `apps/dashboards/templatetags/dashboards.py` (same scope+query-string contract as `services._chart_url()`).
- [p09/T21] `apps/dashboards/models.py::EXPORT_STORAGE` is a callable (`def EXPORT_STORAGE() -> FileSystemStorage`), not a `FileSystemStorage()` instance, contrary to the plan's pseudocode — why: `makemigrations` on a bare instance serialises its `location` kwarg as a literal absolute path (verified: the first generated migration embedded `/Users/korkholeh/Projects/pr-radar/data/exports`, this machine's `DATA_DIR`), which would fail `makemigrations --check --dry-run` and point at the wrong directory on any other machine or `DATA_DIR` override; a callable is recorded by Django's migration serializer as an import reference and re-evaluated against `settings.DATA_DIR` wherever the app runs — alternatives: a custom `FileSystemStorage` subclass (its `deconstruct()` still serialises the resolved `location` value, doesn't fix the problem), commit to one hardcoded `DATA_DIR` for every environment (breaks the project's own `DATA_DIR` env override, `config/settings/base.py`). `expires_at` is a plain nullable field, not a model-field default — why: computing it needs `get_int("EXPORT_RETENTION_DAYS")`, a DB-backed `AppSetting` read, and no other model in the codebase runs a query from inside a field default; `compute_expires_at(created_at)` is a plain function `T22`'s job-creation code calls explicitly instead.
- [p09/verify] This session (T17/T18 tests + fix, T19, T20, T21-partial) verified: `uv run pytest apps/dashboards tests/test_urls_login.py tests/test_pages_smoke.py -q` green (ran in the background, completed clean, confirmed via task notification exit code 0) after T17-T20's changes; `uv run pytest apps/dashboards/tests/test_reports.py apps/dashboards/tests/test_export_report_view.py apps/dashboards/tests/test_export_no_notes.py apps/dashboards/tests/test_export_xlsx.py tests/test_scope_isolation.py -q` green after adding the `ExportJob` model (confirms the new model/migration didn't break anything already passing); `manage.py check` and `manage.py makemigrations --check --dry-run` both clean; `uv run ruff check .` and `uv run ruff format --check .` clean project-wide except the pre-existing, already-logged `PLAN.md` quirk. **Not run this session**: the full `uv run pytest -q` (only targeted subsets, to fit the session's context budget) and `uv run mypy` — next session should run both once before trusting the tree, per this phase's own "before finishing" rule, and before starting new T21 work (admin registration + model tests) or T22.



## p09-review_fix1
- [p09/review_fix1] Fixed BLOCKER "Background export layer (T22-T24) not implemented": added `services.create_export_job()`/`run_export_job()`/`cleanup_exports()`, `services.export_filename()`/`report_row_count()`/`record_export_audit()`, `apps/dashboards/tasks.py` (`export_job_task`, `cleanup_exports_task`), `manage.py process_exports`/`cleanup_exports`, `/exports/` + `/exports/<pk>/download/` (403 on `job.user_id != request.user.id`, a visible "unavailable" page for a not-yet-done/expired job, never an empty body), and the `EXPORT_SYNC_MAX_ROWS` threshold branch in both `views.export_table` and `views.export_report` — why: all four were unimplemented per the review, with acceptance criteria #9-#11 unmet — alternatives: none, this was the plan's own T22-T24 scope, just finished. `ExportJob.params` stores the canonical query string plus `scope_type`/`scope_id`/`table_key`/`fmt` so `run_export_job()` re-resolves `scope_for_user(job.user)` at run time, honouring a grant revoked after enqueue (test: `test_run_export_job_reresolves_scope_for_user_at_run_time`). Tests added in `apps/dashboards/tests/test_export_jobs.py` (T21 model defaults/`compute_expires_at`, T22 threshold+enqueue+re-resolve+failure, T23 own-jobs-only/403/polling/translated-failure-reason/query-count, T24 cleanup deletes expired file+row/leaves a fresh job/sweeps a stuck `RUNNING` job).
- [p09/review_fix1] Fixed BLOCKER "No AuditEntry on any export path (T25)": `services.record_export_audit()` is called from `views.export_table` (both formats), `views.export_report`, and `services.run_export_job()` on success, each writing exactly one `AuditEntry` with kind/format/table_key/scope/filters/row count — why: acceptance criterion #12 was unmet for every export path except `person.notes` — alternatives: none, matches the plan's designed shape. A synchronous export (no persisted `ExportJob` row) uses a tiny `services.Export` stand-in (`object_type="Export"`, `object_id=<table_key or "report">`) as `record_audit()`'s subject, since `record_audit()`'s contract needs *some* object with an optional `.pk`; a background job passes its own `ExportJob` instance instead (`object_type="ExportJob"`). Tests in `apps/dashboards/tests/test_export_audit.py`, parametrized over sync CSV/XLSX, sync report, and a completed background job.
- [p09/review_fix1] Fixed MAJOR "Report's Violations sheet ignores the report's scope": `exports/reports.py::_violation_rows()` now reads `apps.metrics.selectors.scoped_violations(scope)` instead of `policy.selectors.violations_in_scope(scope.access)` — why: the latter applied only the caller's access filter, not the report's own project/repository/person narrowing, so a project- or person-scoped report listed every visible project's violations — alternatives: none, this was a straightforward selector swap to match every other sheet's pattern. Test added: `test_reports.py::test_violations_sheet_is_narrowed_to_the_report_scope` (two projects, PROJECT-scope report, asserts only the in-scope project's violation's repository name appears — had to backdate `PolicyViolation.created_at`, which is `auto_now_add`, via a second `save(update_fields=...)` to land it inside the report's period, since the factory default lands at "now").
- [p09/review_fix1] Fixed MAJOR "Ukrainian translations missing for every new phase-9 string (T26)": ran `make messages` (861-line `.po` diff), translated all 38 previously-empty and un-fuzzied all 46 fuzzy msgids by hand (programmatically via `polib`, reviewed individually), ran `make messages`'s `compilemessages` step, and confirmed `tests/test_translations.py` green (no empty/fuzzy entries, `.mo` matches `.po`, placeholders match). Extended `tests/test_pages_smoke.py`'s `_dashboard_urls()`/parametrize list with `people_index`, `person`, `pull_requests_index`, `pull_request_detail`, `reviews`, `exports_index` (en/uk × light/dark, 12 new cases × ... already covered by the existing parametrize cross-product) — why: RISKS row 8 and CLAUDE.md's same-phase i18n rule were both unmet — alternatives: none, this was pure backlog completion, not a design choice. One judgement call: "Churn" is kept untranslated (transliterated as an English loanword, `msgstr="Churn"`) to match the one existing translation (`"Churn & rework"` → `"Churn і доопрацювання"`) already in the catalogue rather than inventing a second Ukrainian term for the same English word.

No new test added beyond the existing `test_person_notes.py` round-trip — the fix is a template attribute a Python-level test can't observe (the Django test client always posts to the URL it's given, not "the URL a browser would resolve from a missing `action`"); the bug and its fix are both only visible in the rendered HTML, so this is left to the smoke test's rendering rather than adding a brittle string-in-HTML assertion.

Left `person`'s bound unusually high (321) uncommented beyond the shared docstring since it inherits the Overview page's own ~275-query baseline (KPI rows + charts + tables) plus the person-only comparison row — not a regression, just what the page already costs; phase 11 owns the latency budget, not this fix pass.

## p09-review_fix2
- [p09/review_fix2] Rejected MAJOR "Day-mode report aggregates the whole period" — the review is factually wrong for the real request path: `views.export_report()`/`views.dashboard()` always build `params` via `params_module.parse()`, whose day-mode branch (`apps/dashboards/params.py:181-189`) already forces `date_from = date_to = day` and `granularity = "day"` before `build_report()` ever runs, so `_summary_rows()`/`_trends_rows()`/`_pr_rows()`/`_violation_rows()` reading `params.date_from`/`params.date_to` are already narrowed to the single day — verified empirically (`params_module.parse(QueryDict("mode=day&day=2026-08-15"))` returns `date_from == date_to == 2026-08-15`) and with a new end-to-end regression test, `test_export_report_view.py::test_day_mode_report_only_contains_that_days_prs` (seeds a PR on day A and one on day B in the same period, requests a day-mode report for day A through the real view, asserts the PRs sheet holds only day A's PR) — why: the review's own reproduction scenario (`test_reports.py`'s `_params()` test helper, which hand-builds a `DashboardParams` with `mode="day"` but a *period*-range `date_from`/`date_to`) constructs a state `parse()` can never produce from a real request, so it doesn't demonstrate a reachable bug — alternatives: defensively re-derive `date_from`/`date_to` from `params.mode`/`params.day` inside `build_report()` anyway (rejected: would duplicate an invariant `parse()` already owns and that every other reader of `DashboardParams` — `services.build_dashboard()`, `rows.py`, `services.report_row_count()` — already relies on holding).
- [p09/review_fix2] Fixed MINOR "`export_table` materialises every row before deciding to enqueue": added `TableSpec.count_builder` (`apps/dashboards/exports/columns.py`) plus `tables.full_row_count()`, which uses the count builder when no text search is active and falls back to `len(full_rows(...))` otherwise; wired `rows.pull_request_row_count()`/`rows.recent_pr_row_count()` as the two count builders (the only tables realistically large enough to hit `EXPORT_SYNC_MAX_ROWS`) and switched `views.export_table()` to call `full_row_count()` before building columns/rows — why: mirrors `services.report_row_count()`'s existing pattern and the plan's own §6 text ("`views.export_table`/`views.export_report` count the rows first") — alternatives: a `count_only` flag on `full_rows()` (same net query shape, more branching inside one function vs. a separate declarative field on `TableSpec`). Test: `test_export_jobs.py::test_export_above_sync_cap_never_materialises_the_full_row_set` (mocks `full_rows` to raise if called at all above the cap).
- [p09/review_fix2] Fixed MINOR "'My exports' own-jobs test asserts factory state, not the response": rewrote `test_exports_list_shows_only_the_callers_own_jobs` to assert on the rendered body (`data-testid="export-job-row"` count, own job's download URL present, other user's absent) instead of `ExportJob.objects.filter(...).count()` — why: the old assertions held regardless of what the view queried — alternatives: none, this was a pure test-quality fix per the review's own suggested fix.
- [p09/review_fix2] Fixed MINOR "Background XLSX/report exports silently lose hyperlinks": `services.create_export_job()` gained a `base_url` parameter, stored in `ExportJob.params["base_url"]` at enqueue time (both `views.export_table` and `views.export_report` now pass `request.build_absolute_uri("/")`); `services.run_export_job()` reads it back and threads it into `build_report(..., base_url=base_url)` for a report job and through a new shared `exports.columns.absolutize_urls()` for a table job before `write_xlsx()` — why: the task has no request, so without storing `base_url` at enqueue time a queued XLSX export would carry only relative hrefs, which `xlsxwriter.write_url()` already silently downgrades to plain text — alternatives: none needed beyond exactly what the review's fix suggested. Moved the (previously duplicated) URL-absolutizing helper out of `exports/reports.py`'s private `_absolutize()` and `views.py`'s private `_absolute_urls()` into one public `exports.columns.absolutize_urls(rows, columns, base_url)` used by all three call sites (sync table export, sync report, background job), removing the duplication rather than adding a third copy. Test: `test_export_jobs.py::test_run_export_job_writes_absolute_hyperlinks_when_a_base_url_was_stored` (runs a real `TABLE_XLSX` job with a stored `base_url`, reads the produced workbook back with openpyxl, asserts the hyperlink target is absolute).
- [p09/review_fix2] Fixed MINOR "Export-job tests write real files into `data/exports/`": added `apps/dashboards/tests/conftest.py` with an autouse fixture that swaps `ExportJob._meta.get_field("file").storage` for a `FileSystemStorage(location=tmp_path/"exports")` for the duration of each test in this package, restoring the original afterwards — why: `EXPORT_STORAGE` is a *callable* storage that Django's `FileField.__init__` resolves once, at class-definition/import time (verified by reading `django.db.models.fields.files.FileField.__init__`), so overriding `settings.DATA_DIR` in a fixture has no effect on the already-constructed storage instance; the field itself has to be re-pointed — alternatives: `override_settings(DATA_DIR=tmp_path)` alone (verified ineffective, per the above), monkeypatching `apps.dashboards.models.EXPORT_STORAGE` (doesn't help either, since the `FileField` already called it once at import time and cached the result on the field).
- [p09/review_fix2] Fixed MINOR "`PRFilters.tools` uses `ai_tools__icontains`, SQLite-only in practice": branched `pr_filters.py::PRFilters.apply()`'s tools filter on `connection.features.supports_json_field_contains` — real `Q(ai_tools__contains=[tool])` when the backend supports JSON containment (PostgreSQL), the existing quoted-substring `Q(ai_tools__icontains=f'"{tool}"')` fallback on SQLite — why: a `LIKE`-family lookup against a real `jsonb` column does not do containment either, so picking one lookup for both backends was wrong for whichever backend didn't get picked; branching keeps the ORM-only single-choke-point rule (CLAUDE.md) while being correct on both backends this project targets — alternatives: always use `icontains` (silently wrong/needlessly slow on PostgreSQL's native `jsonb`), always use `contains` (breaks SQLite, `p09/T10`'s original bug). Test: `test_pr_filters.py::test_apply_uses_real_json_contains_when_the_backend_supports_it` (mocks the feature flag true, inspects the compiled SQL string for `JSON_CONTAINS` without executing it, since this checkout's SQLite has no such function to run against).
- [p09/review_fix2] Fixed NIT: renamed `test_20000_rows_still_streams_synchronously` to `test_an_export_at_the_sync_cap_still_streams_synchronously` (it sets `EXPORT_SYNC_MAX_ROWS=3` and creates 3 rows, not 20000) — why: matches what the test actually exercises — alternatives: none.
- [p09/verify] `uv run ruff check .`, `uv run ruff format --check .`, `uv run mypy` (clean cache), `uv run python manage.py makemigrations --check --dry-run` and `uv run python manage.py check` all clean. `make css` re-run and committed (`static/css/app.css`/`.build-manifest.sha256`/`tokens.css`) since editing non-test `.py` files under the Tailwind scan invalidates the build manifest even with no template changes — `tests/test_css_tokens.py::test_app_css_is_not_stale` caught this before it was missed.
