## p10-plan

- [p10/plan] Churn rules live in `apps/churn/services.py`, not spec §9's parenthetical `churn/service.py` — why: every other app in this codebase puts rules in `services.py` and CLAUDE.md states the convention; the spec's parenthetical is a pointer, not an interface — alternatives: `service.py` (one app out of ten spelled differently), a `churn/` package with submodules only (no obvious home for `run_churn`).
- [p10/plan] `followup_fix_rate` is materialised into a new derived cache field `PullRequest.has_followup_fix` (set by `apps/activity/followup.py` at sync/recompute time) rather than computed as a join inside the metric — why: the metric is a `RatioCalc`, so its batch function runs inside `rollups.rebuild()`; computing it there would mean a `PRFile`×`PRFile` self-join on `path` per day per cohort, the one quadratic query in the registry (RISKS row 10), and the attribute belongs to the *original* PR's merge day, exactly the shape `revert_rate` already has — alternatives: an on-read `DistributionCalc` (changes the metric's published kind and loses the rollup), a `FollowupFix` link table (a whole model for a boolean the metric needs), a raw-SQL overlap annotation in the batch function (unmaintainable and still quadratic).
- [p10/plan] The follow-up pattern reuses `PullRequest.is_hotfix` (phase 4's `^\s*(hotfix|fix)\b` on title, `^(hotfix|fix)/` on head ref) instead of a second regex — why: the deliverable's "hotfix/fix pattern" is the same pattern, and two copies would drift — alternatives: a dedicated `FOLLOWUP_FIX_TITLE_RE` setting (a second tunable nobody asked for).
- [p10/plan] Follow-up overlap is `|paths(A) ∩ paths(B)| / |paths(A)|` over non-excluded `PRFile` rows, window half-open `(merged_at, merged_at + FOLLOWUP_FIX_WINDOW_DAYS]`, both PRs merged and in the same repository; `paths(A)` empty ⇒ `False` — why: the spec fixes ≥50 % and 14 days but not the denominator or the boundary; anchoring on A's own files makes "half of what this PR touched got fixed again" the claim, and the half-open window stops a PR being its own follow-up — alternatives: Jaccard overlap (a large fix PR would never reach 50 %), overlap over B's files (a one-file hotfix would flag every PR it touches).
- [p10/plan] `merge_method == 'unknown'` is resolved by parent count of `merge_commit_sha` (`git rev-list --parents -n 1`): ≥2 parents ⇒ merge-commit commit set, else squash — why: GitHub's payload leaves the field absent often enough that treating `unknown` as unsupported would erase most of the churn metric, and the clone answers the question authoritatively — alternatives: `unsupported_merge_method` for `unknown` (silently guts the metric), always squash (wrong line attribution on real merge commits).
- [p10/plan] `ChurnResult(status='error')` rows are retried by the next `compute_churn` run; `ok`, `too_large` and `unsupported_merge_method` are terminal — why: an error is a statement about git or the network at one moment, not a verdict about the PR, and a terminal error row would permanently blank that PR's churn — alternatives: terminal errors plus a `--retry-errors` flag (an operator step nobody will remember), a retry-after timestamp column (a schema change for the same effect).
- [p10/plan] Churn parallelism is a `ThreadPoolExecutor` over repositories doing **git work only**; every `ChurnResult` write happens on the main thread after a future resolves — why: this is the most parallel component in a single-writer SQLite system (RISKS row 14), and keeping writes on one thread removes the contention rather than relying on `busy_timeout` — alternatives: writing from workers (concurrent `database is locked`), a process pool (no shared ORM connection anyway, and higher startup cost for a nightly job).
- [p10/plan] Three new `churn` AppSettings: `CHURN_MAX_WORKERS`=4, `CHURN_GIT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`=120, `CHURN_REPO_TIME_BUDGET_SECONDS`=600 — why: ARCHITECTURE's budget table states "≤ 4 concurrent git workers, ≤ 10 min per repository per nightly run" as prose, and the operator of a slow or huge repository needs to change it without a release — alternatives: module constants (unchangeable without a code edit), env vars (inconsistent with every other tunable, which is an `AppSetting`).
- [p10/plan] `git` gets `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` in addition to `GIT_ASKPASS` and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` — why: a developer's global credential helper (osxkeychain on this machine) would otherwise be offered our token to store, which ADR 0004 forbids — alternatives: `credential.helper=` on the command line only (does not stop a system-level config from being read).
- [p10/plan] `remote_url_for(repository)` is a module-level function (`https://github.com/<full_name>.git`) so tests can monkeypatch it to a `file://` temporary origin — why: churn is the one component that cannot be tested against JSON fixtures, and the alternative is either a live clone (forbidden) or never exercising the real clone/fetch code path — alternatives: a settings-based URL template (a production knob that exists only for tests), injecting the URL through every call signature (noise on the production path).
- [p10/plan] Surviving-line resolution follows renames by trying the original path at `snapshot_sha` first, then walking `R<score>` entries from `git log --name-status -M --find-renames <merge_sha>..<snapshot_sha>`; a path that ends deleted contributes 0 surviving lines — why: spec §9.5 requires rename awareness, and a deleted file is genuinely 100 % churned, not an error — alternatives: `git log --follow` per file (single-path-only and slower), ignoring renames (a repository-wide move would report 100 % churn for every PR in it).
- [p10/plan] `followup_fix_rate` is surfaced in `PERSON_COMPARISON_METRIC_KEYS` (Person page comparison table), which renders `MetricDef.title` — "Follow-up fix rate (heuristic)" — why: the acceptance criterion requires the label to be visible, and spec §10.3's KPI row 3 is already fixed at four metrics (rework, CI first-pass, revert, churn), so adding a fifth card would contradict the spec — alternatives: a fifth KPI card (contradicts §10.3), a new column on the people table (changes every export column set and its `assertNumQueries` baseline for one metric).
- [p10/plan] `ChurnResult.error` stores a reason code (`no_snapshot`, `fetch_failed`, `blame_failed`, `no_pr_commits`, `connection_unusable`, `timeout`) plus a masked git detail; the PR page renders the code in the reader's language and shows the git detail as raw diagnostic text — why: CLAUDE.md forbids storing rendered English user-visible text, but a git stderr fragment is a diagnostic, not a sentence to translate — alternatives: storing only the code (the operator loses the git reason entirely), storing the raw stderr as the whole message (untranslatable and a leak surface).

## p10-implement

- [p10/implement] Added `apps.catalog.services.get_float()` alongside the existing typed accessors — why: `FOLLOWUP_FIX_FILE_OVERLAP` is the first `float`-typed AppSetting a service needs to read, and every other typed setting already has its own `get_*` accessor — alternatives: `float(get_setting(...))` inline at each call site (drift risk, no type-checked accessor).
- [p10/implement] `_register_ratio_over_merged_prs()` grew an optional `params` kwarg instead of registering `followup_fix_rate` with a bare `_register(MetricDef(...))` call — why: every other quality ratio in the file goes through the shared helper, and duplicating the `RatioCalc`/levels/cohorts wiring for one metric would drift from the pattern — alternatives: a bare `_register` call for this one metric (inconsistent with its siblings in the same file).
- [p10/implement] `apps/metrics/docs.py` now renders a metric's `params` mapping as a `Params:` line under its Definitions entry — why: the field existed on `MetricDef` but nothing read it, so `followup_fix_rate`'s heuristic thresholds (`window_days`, `file_overlap`) would otherwise never reach `docs/METRICS.md` despite CLAUDE.md's generated-docs-must-be-fresh rule — alternatives: leaving `params` unused and stating the thresholds only in prose inside `description` (already done, but then the field is dead code), a params column in the metrics table (widens every row for one metric).
- [p10/implement] `followup_fix_rate`'s `params` values are read from `apps.catalog.setting_defs.SETTING_DEFS`'s code defaults at module import (a static dict lookup, not a DB read) — why: settings are DB-backed and the metrics-registry module is imported before migrations necessarily exist in every context (e.g. a fresh `manage.py` invocation), so a `get_int()`/`get_float()` call at import time would be a hidden DB dependency of module import — alternatives: calling `get_int`/`get_float` at import time (couples import order to DB readiness), hardcoding the literals `14`/`0.5` again in `quality.py` (a second copy that can drift from `setting_defs.py`).
- [p10/implement] `test_rebuild_matches_the_per_scope_calculator_for_every_additive_metric` (existing, generic, iterates `REGISTRY`) is treated as satisfying the "rollup round-trip" acceptance criterion for both `ci_first_pass_rate` and `followup_fix_rate` — why: it already asserts, for every `RatioCalc` in the registry including these two, that `rebuild()`'s `__num`/`__den` rollup rows equal the calculator's own `numerator()`/`denominator()` — a second, metric-specific round-trip test would just re-run the same assertion machinery — alternatives: a bespoke round-trip test per metric in `test_metrics_quality.py` (duplicate oracle logic already centralised in `test_rollups.py`).
- [p10/implement] Session 1 stopped mid-T10 (clone lifecycle) with context nearly exhausted — `apps/churn/clones.py` is written and imports cleanly under `manage.py check`, but `apps/churn/tests/test_clones.py` was never created, so T10 stays unchecked in PLAN.md with a `STATUS` note pointing session 2 at exactly what's missing (a `settings.DATA_DIR` override plus the already-built `origin_remote` fixture) — why: CLAUDE.md/the task's own rules forbid checking a task done without its tests, and a full `uv run pytest -q` was still running in the background when the context budget ran out, so it could not be confirmed green before handoff — alternatives: checking T10 done anyway on the strength of `manage.py check` alone (violates "implement together with its tests"), deleting the unfinished `clones.py` (throws away working, lint-clean code for no reason).
- [p10/implement] Fixed two full-suite failures surfaced only when running the whole suite (not caught by narrow per-task runs): `test_followup_fix_rate_is_registered_as_a_heuristic` now asserts the English title inside `translation.override("en")` — why: `MetricDef.title` is a `gettext_lazy` string rendered in whatever language an earlier test in the run left active, and the bare assertion was order-dependent, not actually broken heuristics wiring — alternatives: resetting `translation.activate("en")` globally in a fixture (papers over the same order-dependency for other tests too, out of this phase's scope). Also reran `make css` (the cached `.tools/tailwindcss` binary, no network) to refresh `static/css/.build-manifest.sha256` — why: the manifest hashes every scanned `.py`/template file, not just `static/css/app.css`'s own content, and this session added several new `.py` files under `apps/churn/` and `apps/activity/`, so the committed manifest was stale through no template change of its own — alternatives: none; this is exactly what CLAUDE.md's generated-file-freshness convention expects.
- [p10/implement] `run_churn`'s worker threads never issue a fresh ORM query: `compute_churn_for_pull_request` reads `pull_request.files.all()`/`pull_request.pull_request_commits.all()` (cache-safe on a `prefetch_related` instance) instead of `.filter()`/`.values_list()` (which always hit the DB, bypassing any prefetch cache), and `run_churn` prefetches both relations before submitting PRs to the `ThreadPoolExecutor` — why: a first version called `.filter(is_excluded=False)`/`.values_list("commit__sha", flat=True)` straight from the worker thread and every threaded test failed with `sqlite3.OperationalError: database table is locked` — pytest-django's per-test transaction is held on the main thread's connection, and a second thread opening its own connection to the same file deadlocks against it, exactly the RISKS row 14 hazard the design doc's "no ORM connection in workers" line was warning about — alternatives: a thread-local DB connection per worker (defeats the single-writer point of the design), computing the PR-commit set and file list inside `eligible_pull_requests` as plain tuples instead of prefetching Django objects (equivalent, more code for no benefit).
- [p10/implement] Session 2 completed T10 through T19 (clone lifecycle, blame/rename resolution, eligibility, the §9 algorithm, run orchestration, the token-leak test, the `compute_churn` command + nightly task, the PR-detail churn section, the `followup_fix_rate` heuristic label on the Person comparison table, and all uk translations for the new strings) and stopped cleanly before T20 (docs) and T21 (e2e) for context-budget reasons — why: T20/T21 are pure-documentation/e2e-scaffolding work with no risk of touching the churn/dashboards code just finished, so stopping at this boundary leaves nothing half-edited — alternatives: pushing partway into T20 and risking a mid-file handoff, which is worse for the next session than a clean task boundary.
- [p10/implement] `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` already documents all five churn settings and both follow-up settings (a session-1 addition) — re-verified accurate against the current `SETTING_DEFS`/`get_int` defaults before leaving T20 marked not-started, so the next session does not re-discover and re-add them — why: avoids duplicate work from a PLAN.md note that undersells what already exists — alternatives: leaving T20's note generic ("write CONFIGURATION.md entries"), which would cost the next session a re-grep to find out they're already there.
- [p10/implement] `docs/PROGRESS.md` gained a "Phase 10" section but phases 4–9 stay missing from it (last real entry before this session was phase 3) — why: phase 10's task is to keep this file current for phase 10's own changes, and backfilling six prior phases' narrative summaries is a much larger, separate task with no test gating it (`tests/test_docs.py` gates settings/metrics/policy/derived-field/check-code coverage, not this file's phase list) — alternatives: backfilling phases 4-9 now (out of this phase's scope and budget), leaving phase 10 out too (perpetuates the same gap for no reason).
- [p10/e2e] `make vendor` never fetched `chart.umd.js.map`, though the vendored `chart.umd.js` ends with `//# sourceMappingURL=chart.umd.js.map`; whitenoise's `collectstatic` manifest post-processor resolves that reference and raises `MissingFileError: The file 'vendor/chart.umd.js.map' could not be found`, which made `make e2e-up` fail outright (`make e2e-up 2>&1` → `MissingFileError` after `Post-processing 'vendor/chart.umd.js' failed!`, exit 1) — this is the same failure PROGRESS.md records as "e2e-up — failed: exit 2" for both p08 and p09, i.e. the whole e2e suite had not actually run end-to-end since before this bug was introduced. Fixed by adding the `.map` fetch to the `vendor` Makefile target and committing the fetched `static/vendor/chart.umd.js.map` (955 KB, from the same `chart.js@4.4.4` unpkg release already pinned) — why: a one-line Makefile omission, not a design question, and the fix is exactly what `make vendor`'s own docstring/intent already promises ("re-fetches static/vendor" plural) — alternatives: strip the `sourceMappingURL` comment from the vendored file (loses working source maps for anyone debugging the vendored chart code in a browser for no reason), ignore the file in whitenoise's manifest scan (papers over a real missing asset rather than fetching it).
- [p10/e2e] With e2e actually able to run for the first time since phase 9, found one stale spec: `e2e/web/test_people.py::test_lead_cannot_reach_people` asserted zero links whose accessible name contains "People", written before phase 9 added `dashboards:people_index` (labelled exactly "People" in `templates/partials/nav.html`, open to every authenticated user including leads) alongside the pre-existing admin-only "People settings" (`catalog:people`, gated on `perms.catalog.manage_settings`). The product is correct — a lead legitimately sees the dashboards People page and legitimately cannot reach People settings — the test's locator just became ambiguous once a second, differently-scoped "People"-named link existed. Fixed by narrowing the assertion to the exact link text "People settings" — why: this is what the test always meant to check (the direct-GET-403 assertion two lines below already targets the settings page specifically), not a product change — alternatives: leaving the test failing (masks every future e2e run's real signal behind one always-red case), asserting on the settings-page-only DOM region instead of link text (more code for the same specificity `get_by_role(name="People settings")` already gives for free).


## p10-review_fix1

- [p10/review_fix1] A PR whose `lines_at_merge == 0` now gets a settled `ChurnResult` row (`status=ok`, `lines_at_merge=0`, `churn_ratio=None`) instead of no row at all, deviating from spec §9.6's literal "no `ChurnResult` row is written" — why: `eligible_pull_requests` treats a missing row as still-eligible, so "no row" meant this PR was re-cloned and re-blamed on every future nightly run forever (review MINOR finding); `churn_21d`'s `median()` already drops `None` values, so the KPI is unaffected, and `_write_outcome` now always `update_or_create`s, which as a side effect also clears any stale `error` row left by an earlier failed run for the same PR — alternatives: a separate "skipped PR ids" tracking table (a schema addition for the same settling effect the existing `status` column already gives for free), leaving the spec text as literal law and accepting permanent re-work (rejected — CLAUDE.md's "never a fake 0%" is honoured by `churn_ratio=None`, and the spec's real intent is "don't show a fabricated ratio", which this preserves).
- [p10/review_fix1] `_sum_blame` now catches `GitOperationError` per path and contributes 0 instead of letting one missing path abort the whole PR into `status=error` — why: `PRFile` rows for a deleted file are non-excluded and have a path absent at `merge_commit_sha`, so any PR that deletes a file (routine) previously always errored (review MAJOR finding); a deleted file genuinely contributed 0 surviving lines at merge, which is the correct answer per the same reasoning §9.5 already applies to a file deleted *after* merge — alternatives: pre-checking existence with `_path_exists_at` before every blame call (one extra `cat-file` subprocess per path for no benefit over just catching the failure), skipping only paths matching `PRFile.status == "deleted"` (couples the fix to GitHub's changeType vocabulary instead of the git ground truth).
- [p10/review_fix1] `_compute_repository`'s per-repository deadline is now computed as its own first statement from a `repo_budget` duration passed in, not eagerly as an absolute `time.monotonic() + repo_budget` computed in the submitting thread for every repository at once — why: the eager form charged a repository's queue-wait time (behind `CHURN_MAX_WORKERS` other repositories) against its own work budget, so the 5th+ repository on an install with more than a handful of repos could pay for a full `ensure_clone()` and then immediately hit an already-expired deadline and return no rows, silently (review MAJOR finding) — alternatives: raising `CHURN_MAX_WORKERS` as a workaround (does not fix the underlying measurement, just delays when it bites), tracking per-repository start time separately from the deadline (equivalent, more state for no benefit).
- [p10/review_fix1] `CHURN_MAX_FILES`, `CHURN_GIT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and `CHURN_REPO_TIME_BUDGET_SECONDS` are now read exactly once by `run_churn` on the main thread and threaded explicitly into `_compute_repository`/`compute_churn_for_pull_request`/`ensure_clone`/`blame_counts`/`resolve_path_at`, none of which call `get_int()` themselves any more — why: `get_int()` falls through to `AppSetting.objects.all()` on a cold `FileBasedCache` entry, so up to `CHURN_MAX_WORKERS` worker threads could each open their own SQLite connection concurrently — the exact RISKS row 14 contention this component's whole "workers do git only, main thread does every write" design exists to avoid, and the resulting `OperationalError` was not a `GitOperationError` so it escaped `_compute_repository` uncaught (review MAJOR finding) — alternatives: warming the cache before submitting workers (fragile — nothing stops a later cache eviction mid-run), a thread lock around `get_int()` (serializes workers on every settings read for no benefit over reading once upfront).
- [p10/review_fix1] `run_churn` wraps each `future.result()` in `try/except Exception`, logging and recording `error`/`worker_failed` outcomes for that repository's still-outstanding PRs instead of letting an unexpected exception (e.g. `OSError` from a full disk in `ensure_clone`'s `mkdir`/`rmtree`) propagate out of `run_churn` and abort `bump_data_version()` plus every other repository's already-collected results — why: PLAN §6 and RISKS row 11 both promise "never an exception escaping `run_churn`", and only `GitOperationError` was being caught before this — alternatives: catching only `OSError` specifically (narrower than the promise; any other unforeseen worker bug would still abort the run), moving disk-exhaustion handling into `ensure_clone` itself (already deferred out of scope per PLAN §11, and does not cover other possible worker exceptions).
- [p10/review_fix1] `blame_counts`/`resolve_path_at`/`_path_exists_at` and every `run_git` call site in `services.py` (`_pr_commit_set`'s `rev-list`, `_snapshot_sha`) now accept and forward an explicit `timeout` instead of defaulting to `timeout=None` (block forever) — why: only `clone`/`fetch` were ever bounded by `CHURN_GIT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`; a pathological `git blame` on a worker thread could hang that thread forever, and `ThreadPoolExecutor.__exit__` then waits for it forever too, so the nightly huey task would never finish (review MINOR finding) — alternatives: a global `signal.alarm`-based timeout wrapper (doesn't compose with threads; `SIGALRM` only fires on the main thread), leaving blame/log/rev-list unbounded and relying on the per-repository time budget alone (review already notes the budget is only checked *between* PRs, not inside a single git call).
- [p10/review_fix1] `run_git` now also replaces the literal credential value with `***<last 4 chars>` in a masked error detail, on top of `mask_secrets`'s prefix-shape matching — why: `mask_secrets` only recognises `ghp_`/`gho_`/`ghu_`/`ghs_`/`ghr_`/`github_pat_` prefixes, so any differently-shaped credential (a GitHub Enterprise PAT, a future token format) would pass through unmasked into `GitOperationError` → `ChurnResult.error` → the PR detail page, the newest path from a token to a DB column and a template (review MINOR finding) — alternatives: extending `mask_secrets`'s prefix list preemptively (still shape-dependent, still misses anything unanticipated), masking the whole stderr text whenever `credentials` is set (destroys the diagnostic value of a real git error message that happens to share no text with the token).
- [p10/review_fix1] `ChurnResult.error` now also carries the `CHURN_MAX_FILES` value in force at write time for `too_large` outcomes (previously empty), and the PR detail page renders that stored limit instead of the live `get_int("CHURN_MAX_FILES")` — why: `too_large` is terminal, so a row written under limit 50 would otherwise silently start claiming "more than 200 files" after an operator raises the setting, which is false for that specific historical PR (review NIT finding) — alternatives: leaving the live-setting render (simpler, but wrong for any terminal row after any limit change — rejected because it is cheap to fix correctly), a new dedicated `too_large_limit` column (a schema change for one integer already expressible in the existing free-text `error` field).
- [p10/review_fix1] `apps/activity/followup.py::update_followup_fixes`'s cascade over `originals` is now batched (one query for `originals` filtered to `state=MERGED`, one query for every candidate `is_hotfix` PR across all of their windows, one grouped `PRFile` query for all involved PRs' paths, then in-memory overlap checks) instead of one `compute_has_followup_fix()` call — and therefore one `_non_excluded_paths()` query per candidate — per original (review MINOR finding: thousands of queries per synced PR on a busy repository). The review's third suggested optimization ("skip the cascade entirely when the freshly synced PR is not `is_hotfix`") was **not** applied — why: it would leave a stale `has_followup_fix=True` on an `original` forever if the PR that earned it later stops being `is_hotfix` (e.g. a title edit) without any other PR syncing in that original's window afterward to re-trigger a full recompute; the batching alone already collapses the query count from O(originals × candidates) to O(1) regardless of repository size, which is what the finding's "thousands of queries" complaint is actually about — alternatives: applying the hotfix short-circuit anyway for a marginal further speedup (rejected: introduces a real, if rare, correctness gap for a saving that is already dwarfed by the batching), a `FollowupFix` link table recording who-fixed-whom so flips are event-driven instead of recomputed (out of phase scope per PLAN's "Out of scope" list).
- [p10/review_fix1] Dropped `apps/dashboards/tests/test_person_page.py::test_person_comparison_table_query_count_does_not_regress` (review NIT finding) rather than tightening it — why: `apps/dashboards/tests/test_query_counts.py::test_person_page_query_count` already pins the same page at an exact `django_assert_num_queries(321)` and was untouched by this phase, so the new test's 330-query ceiling added nine queries of slack next to an exact assertion with no signal of its own — alternatives: converting it to an exact assertion for the comparison-table fragment specifically (redundant with the existing exact whole-page pin; two exact assertions of the same underlying query count is duplication, not coverage).



## p10-review_fix2

- [p10/review_fix2] `_sum_blame` no longer catches every `GitOperationError` from `blame_counts` as "path missing, contribute 0" — it re-raises when `exc.reason == "timeout"`, or when the path genuinely exists at `rev` (checked with `blame.path_exists_at`, the renamed-public `_path_exists_at`), so `compute_churn_for_pull_request` turns the failure into a retryable `error`/`blame_failed` outcome instead of silently under-counting `lines_at_merge` and settling the PR as a terminal `ok`/`skip` row that is never retried — why: review r2 MAJOR finding; the r1 fix's blanket `except GitOperationError: continue` conflated a genuinely deleted path (a correct 0) with a transient failure like a blame timeout on a huge file (an incorrect 0) — alternatives: distinguishing purely by `exc.reason` (impossible — a missing-path `git blame` failure and a corrupt-object failure both exit non-zero with `reason="git_error"`, so `reason` alone cannot tell them apart), catching `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` one layer up instead of inside `_sum_blame` (loses the per-path granularity — one slow path would then abort the whole `_sum_blame` loop rather than just that path's contribution failing the PR).
- [p10/review_fix2] `compute_churn_for_pull_request`'s steps 4 and 6 (`_sum_blame`, `_sum_surviving`) are now wrapped in the same per-step `try/except GitOperationError` pattern already used for steps 3 and 5 (`_pr_commit_set`, `_snapshot_sha`) — why: review r2 MAJOR finding; `_sum_surviving` was the one unguarded git step, so its failure escaped the function entirely, then escaped `_compute_repository`'s loop (also unguarded), landed in `run_churn`'s broad `except Exception`, and turned *every* PR of that repository into `error`/`worker_failed`, contradicting PLAN §6 step 9 and RISKS row 11's "one PR's failure never aborts its repository" — alternatives: wrapping the whole function body in one outer `try/except` (would also swallow the intentionally distinct `no_pr_commits`/`no_snapshot` reason codes into one generic message, losing diagnostic detail already tested for).
- [p10/review_fix2] `_compute_repository`'s per-PR loop also gained its own `try/except Exception` around the `compute_churn_for_pull_request` call, converting an unexpected exception into `error`/`worker_failed` for that PR alone — why: review r2 MAJOR finding's fix suggestion, applied as defense in depth on top of the two bullets above; `compute_churn_for_pull_request`'s docstring promises "never raises", and this loop is the boundary that must hold even if a future change to the function breaks that promise, matching the pattern `run_churn` already uses per-repository — alternatives: relying solely on the function-level fix (correct today, but reintroduces the whole-repository blast radius the instant a future bug adds an unguarded git call inside `compute_churn_for_pull_request`).
- [p10/review_fix2] `pull_request_rows()` now annotates `churn_ratio` with a `Subquery` against `ChurnResult.objects.filter(pull_request=OuterRef("pk"), window_days=get_int("CHURN_WINDOW_DAYS"), status=OK).values("churn_ratio")[:1]` instead of hardcoding `None` — why: review r2 MAJOR finding; phase 10 computes `ChurnResult` rows but the PR list/export's `Churn` column (`ExportColumn("churn_ratio", ...)`, spec §12) was never wired to read them, and phase 11 (the only later phase) does not claim this, so it would have shipped as a permanently blank column with the docstring's own "until phase 10" pointer becoming false — alternatives: a `select_related`/prefetch join (a `ChurnResult` row is looked up per-`window_days`, not a simple FK, so a `Prefetch` with a filtered queryset is equivalent complexity to the `Subquery` for one scalar column and less idiomatic for django-tables2/CSV/XLSX row dicts), computing it in Python after the query (one query per row — the N+1 this fix exists to avoid).
- [p10/review_fix2] The r2 MINOR finding on `lines_at_merge == 0` conflating "measured" with "nothing measurable" is treated as resolved by the MAJOR fix above, not given a separate status code — why: the conflation the reviewer flagged was specifically that a swallowed blame failure could *also* produce `lines_at_merge == 0`, making that settled `ok` row ambiguous between "genuinely zero" and "blame silently failed"; now that `_sum_blame` re-raises every failure that is not a genuinely-missing path, `lines_at_merge == 0` can only be reached when every blame call for every path succeeded and truly attributed no lines — the ambiguity is gone without needing a new `error`/`blame_failed` branch for the zero case, and the r1 decision's reasoning for settling zero-lines PRs as terminal `ok` (never re-cloned forever) still stands — alternatives: the review's own suggested fix of a distinct status for "some blame calls failed" (redundant once those failures raise into `error`/`blame_failed` directly, per the bullet above, rather than reaching the zero-lines branch at all).
- [p10/review_fix2] `docs/user/churn.md` updated to list six PR-detail states (was five) and rewritten to state that a zero-measurable-lines PR gets a recorded result with no ratio, not "not computed yet" forever — why: review r2 MINOR finding; the doc still described the pre-r1-fix behaviour and omitted the sixth template branch (`pull_request_detail.html`'s `status == "ok"` with `churn_ratio is None`) entirely — alternatives: none; this is a factual doc correction with no design choice involved.




## p10-e2e

- [p10/e2e] Full QA pass: `uv run pytest -q` green (all unit tests), `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=config.settings.e2e uv run pytest e2e -q` green (55 cases including both `e2e/web/test_churn.py` cases against the already-running `make e2e-up` surface at :8100). Verified every acceptance criterion in this step's brief against the actual code (not the decision log alone): squash/merge-commit hand-counted ratios, rebase → `unsupported_merge_method`/no ratio, `too_large` at `CHURN_MAX_FILES`, the failing-fetch-deletes-and-reclones-once path (`test_clones.py::test_failing_fetch_deletes_and_reclones_once` + `test_churn_run.py::test_a_repository_with_a_failing_fetch_does_not_stop_the_other_repository`), the token-leak assertions (argv/remote URL/.git/config/log/ChurnResult.error), and both `ci_first_pass_rate`/`followup_fix_rate` positive+negative tests plus the "heuristic" UI label — all present and passing as claimed. Found one real letter-of-the-spec gap, not a bug: the literal criterion "lines_at_merge == 0 records no ChurnResult" no longer matches the shipped, twice-reviewed behaviour (a settled `status=ok, churn_ratio=None` row, decided in `p10-review_fix1` specifically to avoid re-cloning/re-blaming the same PR forever) — why not revert: two review rounds already weighed this exact tradeoff and chose it deliberately, `churn_21d`'s `median()` already drops `None` so the KPI is unaffected, and CLAUDE.md's real concern (never a fabricated ratio) is preserved; reopening it a third time would just re-litigate a settled, well-reasoned call. Fixed instead by correcting PLAN.md's acceptance-criteria table row (it still named a nonexistent test, `test_zero_lines_at_merge_writes_no_churn_result`) to point at the real, current tests and state the actual contract — alternatives: reverting to "no row" (reintroduces the infinite-reprocessing bug the review fixed), leaving the stale table row uncorrected (misleads the next reader into thinking a test exists that does not).
- [p10/e2e] `e2e/RESULTS.md` had not been regenerated since phase 9 — the two churn e2e cases (`test_pr_detail_shows_a_computed_churn_percentage`, `test_pr_detail_shows_unsupported_merge_method_sentence`), already authored and green, were missing from the table entirely, and the pass-count footer still read "53 passed" against a suite that now collects 55. Fixed by appending both rows and a phase-10 findings paragraph summarizing the three real bugs the two prior code-review rounds found and fixed (blame-failure swallowing, the unguarded `_sum_surviving` step, the hardcoded-None PR-export Churn column) plus the zero-lines letter-vs-spirit gap above — why: this file is this project's durable regression/QA record, so a stale table would understate coverage to the next reader — alternatives: none; regenerating it is this step's explicit job.

## p11-plan


- [p11/plan] `seed_demo --scale large` (50 repos / 20 000 PRs) writes with `bulk_create` and then calls the batch functions `recompute` already uses (`derive_pull_requests` → `update_followup_fixes_for` → `detect_pull_requests` → `evaluate_pull_requests` → `rollups.rebuild` → `bump_data_version`), instead of `pipeline.process_pull_request` per PR — why: measured `uv run pytest apps/dashboards/tests/test_seed_demo.py` at ≈14 s per 250-PR seed, i.e. ≈18 minutes at 20 000 PRs, which no gate can carry, and the batch functions are the same production code path rather than a second implementation — alternatives: per-PR pipeline at volume (18 min inside `pytest`), a hand-written fast path that sets derived fields itself (duplicates `derive`/`detect`/`evaluate` logic and would drift), a fixture file of pre-baked rows (opaque and stale on any model change).
- [p11/plan] `--scale large` is create-only and raises `CommandError` on a database that already holds demo rows, telling the operator to pass `--reset`; idempotency stays a `demo`-scale guarantee — why: `get_or_create` per child row is exactly the cost that makes the per-PR path unusable at 20 000 PRs, and `test_seed_demo.py` already covers idempotency at the scale where it matters — alternatives: `ignore_conflicts=True` on every `bulk_create` (silently half-seeds), keeping `get_or_create` at large scale (defeats the purpose).
- [p11/plan] The contrast test asserts a **declared pair table** with two named exemptions rather than every possible token×token combination: `--border` and `--grid` are decorative dividers/gridlines, outside WCAG 2.1 SC 1.4.11 (which covers UI-component boundaries and state indicators), and `--heat-1..4` fills are a redundant encoding because every heat cell prints its count as text — so what is asserted there is the *text on* the heat colours — why: a full cross-product would assert ratios for pairs that never touch on screen and would force meaningless palette changes; the exemptions are explicit test entries with their reasoning, so they are reviewable instead of invisible — alternatives: full cross-product (false failures), a screenshot-based audit (no stack for it here, not reproducible in CI).
- [p11/plan] Two new tokens instead of retuning the existing ones for everything: `--border-strong` (≥3:1, used by form controls, buttons and focus rings) beside today's decorative `--border`, and `--on-heat` (text on heat levels ≥3) — why: current `border/bg` is 1.39 (light) / 1.48 (dark) and `text` on `heat-4` is 3.89 / 1.97, so the honest fix is a semantic split plus a paired foreground, not darkening every divider in the product — alternatives: darken `--border` globally (visibly heavier UI everywhere for a rule that only applies to controls), lighten the heat scale (loses the hot/cold signal).
- [p11/plan] Light-theme `accent`, `neutral`, `good` and `warning` get darkened until 4.5:1 holds on `--surface-2` (measured today: 4.23 / 3.95 / 4.37 / 4.19 on the relevant backgrounds), with `tests/test_token_contrast.py` as the oracle — why: those four are used as *text* colours (`direction_class`, badges, the small-sample note), so 4.5:1 is the applicable requirement, and `surface-2` is the darkest of the three backgrounds they land on — alternatives: forbidding them on `surface-2` (a rule no test can enforce in templates), accepting 3:1 for "large text" (the note text is 12 px).
- [p11/plan] The page-level empty state is driven by a new existence selector `period_has_pull_requests(scope, params)` (one `SELECT 1 … LIMIT 1` from the scoped queryset), not by inspecting KPI values — why: a metric of `None`/`0` cannot distinguish "nothing in scope" from a real zero, and the two need different sentences; `assertNumQueries` expectations move by exactly +1 per page with the reason recorded in the test docstring — alternatives: `sample_size == 0` on a chosen KPI (couples the empty state to one metric's definition), an `EXISTS` inside every selector (repeats the query per surface).
- [p11/plan] `below_min_sample` reaches metric table cells as an extra row-dict key `f"{key}__low"` rather than a new `ExportColumn` or a wrapper value object — why: the export layer reads cells by `ExportColumn.key`, so extra dict keys are inert in CSV/XLSX (which already carry their own `below_min_sample` column in the report), and a wrapper object would break `sort_rows`/`search_rows` and every existing export test — alternatives: a `MetricCell` value object (touches sorting, searching, CSV, XLSX and every table test), recomputing the flag in the template (a second `MIN_SAMPLE` read outside `metrics.services`).
- [p11/plan] The timed 1.5 s test stays inside the default `uv run pytest -q` gate (module-scoped `--scale large` seed, registered `slow` marker for local deselection only), and asserts a **cold** render plus a separate warm `< 0.3 s` assertion — why: the acceptance criterion is a gate criterion, ARCHITECTURE line 333 states both budgets, and splitting them makes a cache regression distinguishable from a SQL regression — alternatives: an opt-in marker excluded from the gate (the criterion would never run), measuring only the warm render (would pass while cold SQL rots).
- [p11/plan] Long Ukrainian strings are proven by a Playwright spec (`e2e/web/test_layout_uk.py`, 1280×800 and 390×844, `scrollWidth <= clientWidth` on the page and on KPI/table-header elements) rather than by a Django-level assertion — why: clipping and overflow are layout facts only a real browser with the compiled CSS can measure — alternatives: asserting a maximum `msgstr` length in `.po` (a proxy that fails good translations and passes bad layout), screenshot diffing (no baseline infrastructure here).


## p11-implement

- [p11/implement] `connections:list`'s empty row and `connections:discover`'s two dead ends (no connection selected; no connections at all) never rendered the shared `partials/empty_state.html`, unlike every other list page — found by a targeted survey while building the T3 sweep test, not by the plan's own inventory — why: `discovery_content.html`'s only empty branch lived inside `{% if connection %}`, so it was unreachable exactly when there is nothing to discover from; fixed with two new branches (`{% elif connections %}` "choose a connection", `{% else %}` "create a connection first" with an action link to `connections:create`) — alternatives: leaving it (fails the sweep test's own premise that every list page explains an empty state), a single generic message for both dead ends (loses the distinction between "you have connections, pick one" and "you have none yet").
- [p11/implement] `tests/test_empty_states.py` sweeps 16 list-shaped named pages only (dashboards index pages + catalog/ai_detection/connections/github_sync/policy list/settings pages), excluding every PK-addressed page (`dashboards:person`, `pull_request_detail`, edit forms) — why: there is no object to address on a truly empty database, so those URLs either 404 or need a seeded PK, and their own tests already cover the "object not found" path; the plan's own T3 wording ("every named page URL") is read as "every page reachable with no id", matching what's actually testable on an empty DB — alternatives: seeding one row per object type just to reach the detail pages (defeats the point of an *empty*-database sweep; that's what `test_pages_smoke.py` already does with `seed_demo`).
- [p11/implement] The table-cell small-sample marker (`tables.py::_make_render`) is a distinct, more compact glyph (`≈` + `title`/`aria-label`, `data-testid="cell-small-sample"`) rather than reusing `{% small_sample_note %}`'s full "Small sample" sentence — why: plan §2 spells out this exact glyph+testid for the table-cell surface specifically, separate from the KPI/person-comparison/policy-KPI sentence, since a table row has many columns and a full sentence per low-sample cell would be unreadable — alternatives: reusing `small_sample_note()` verbatim in every cell (verbose, wrong testid, breaks the sweep's declared-list expectation for T5).
- [p11/implement] `tests/test_token_contrast.py` computes WCAG luminance/contrast itself (a ~30-line pure-Python implementation of the sRGB relative-luminance formula) rather than depending on a third-party contrast library — why: the repo has no existing colour-math dependency, and the formula is small, stable and exactly what CLAUDE.md/ADR 0008 need verified against `tokens.css` directly, not against rendered output — alternatives: adding a new dependency (`wcag-contrast-ratio` or similar) for a formula this small, computing it once by hand outside the test (not reviewable/re-runnable when a token changes).
- [p11/implement] `--heat-3` needed retuning in *both* themes, not only a new `--on-heat` token — measured: white-on-old-light-`heat-3` was 3.25:1 (needs 4.5), near-black-on-old-dark-`heat-3` was 4.00:1 — why: `--on-heat` is one token per theme shared by heat levels 3 and 4, so it has to clear the worse of the two; `heat-4` already cleared 4.5:1 with the chosen `on-heat` values (white/near-black per the plan's own §3 design note) so only `heat-3` moved — alternatives: a per-level `on-heat-3`/`on-heat-4` pair (two more tokens for a distinction the design doesn't otherwise need), leaving `heat-3` failing (fails T6, the acceptance criterion).
- [p11/implement] Light-theme `heat-3` (`#4174c5`) and `heat-4` (`#2f6feb`, unchanged) end up at nearly identical luminance (0.176 vs 0.180) after the retune — accepted rather than further separated — why: every heat cell prints its own count as text (`reviewer_heat_map.html`), so the fill is a redundant encoding (this phase's own named exemption for `--heat-1..4`); forcing more separation would mean darkening `heat-3` further, which risks under-shooting `on-heat`'s margin again — alternatives: also retune `heat-4` to open a gap (defeats the point of leaving an already-passing token alone), a five-stop heat scale (a bigger change than a contrast fix warrants).
- [p11/implement] T8's control-boundary swap treats a bordered element with no explicit `border-[var(--border)]` class (the many bare `<select>`/`<input type="date">` in `filter_bar.html`, the checkbox/select/textarea/button in the policy bulk-action form) as out of scope rather than adding a border to give it one — why: CLAUDE.md's colour-literal rule governs this codebase's own styling decisions, not the browser's UA default chrome on an otherwise unstyled control, and there is no existing literal or token reference to move; adding a border where none exists today would be new design scope beyond "every control that already draws a `--border` moves to `--border-strong`" — alternatives: giving every native control an explicit `border-[var(--border-strong)]` (a real design change — visual weight added everywhere — not something T8's contrast fix asked for), leaving the task note's ambiguity unresolved (would recur next time someone re-reads the survey).
- [p11/implement] `seed_demo --scale large` uses its own `pr-radar-large-*`/`LARGE_*` namespace (organizations, connection name, project slugs, person display names) entirely separate from the demo-scale constants — why: this keeps `large` genuinely create-only and independently resettable without touching or being confused with demo-scale rows if both are ever seeded into the same database, and it lets `_reset_large()` mirror `_reset()` exactly (delete the large orgs, cascade does the rest) — alternatives: reusing `ORG_DEFS`/`CONNECTION_NAME` with a suffix (more collision surface for the same isolation), a single shared reset that wipes both scales together (defeats the point of two independent scales).
- [p11/implement] `seed_demo --scale large`'s four post-write steps (`derive_pull_requests`, `update_followup_fixes_for`, `detect_pull_requests`, `evaluate_pull_requests`) are called directly against `PullRequest.objects.filter(pk__in=pull_request_ids)` rather than through `manage.py recompute` — why: `recompute` is a thin CLI wrapper around the exact same four functions plus its own date-range queryset construction, and `seed_demo` already knows the precise PK set it just wrote (call_command indirection would just re-derive the same filter from dates) — alternatives: shelling out to `call_command("recompute", ...)` (extra indirection, and its date-range filter is a weaker proxy for "the PRs this run just created" than the PK list already in hand).
- [p11/implement] Measured `seed_demo --scale large` at 222.8s (50 repos, 20 000 PRs, full derive/detect/evaluate/rollup/bump_data_version pipeline) on a scratch sqlite db, via `call_command('seed_demo', scale='large', days=120)` timed with `time.time()` — why recorded: this is the reference number T10's profiling and T12's timed-test module-scope budget need; it is above the plan's own "~3 min" soft target (§4/Risks: "gate wall-clock"), so if T12's full-suite wall-clock later proves it a problem, the documented lever is child density or the `--days` window of the perf module specifically, never the 50/20 000 volume or the 1.5s dashboard threshold — alternatives considered at this session but not applied: further trimming child density (files/commits/reviews) below the plan's own §4 numbers (would under-shoot the "realistic enough to profile" intent), parallelizing the seed loop (SQLite single-writer, RISKS row 14 — the same reasoning `churn`'s design already documents).
- [p11/implement] `test_seed_demo_scale.py::test_demo_scale_is_unchanged_by_the_scale_argument` rewritten to compare the large-repository count before/after `--scale demo` instead of asserting it is zero — why: the module-scoped `django_db_setup` fixture commits the `--scale large` rows outside any test's own transaction so the other tests in the file can share one seed, so by the time the last test runs those rows are already present and "must be absent" is the wrong invariant — alternatives: function-scoped fixture for this one test (defeats the shared-seed point of the module fixture), a separate test module (duplicates the ~230s large-scale seed).
- [p11/implement] Ukrainian proofread (T13) normalized "рев'ю" (ASCII apostrophe, 4 new occurrences) to "ревʼю" (U+02BC, the file's existing 56 occurrences) and left the file-wide ви/Ви casing split (154 vs 26) untouched — why: the apostrophe glyph is a real inconsistency introduced this session; the pronoun casing split predates phase 11 and a full sweep is a separate, larger proofreading pass outside this phase's declared scope — alternatives: fixing the casing split too (out of scope creep), leaving the apostrophe inconsistent (fails the "one choice, applied consistently" instruction in the plan).
- [p11/implement] T10 profiling against real `--scale large` data required two prerequisite fixes before it could run at all: the dev DB (`data/db.sqlite3`) was three migrations behind (`activity.0002/0003`, `ai_detection.0002`, `catalog.0004-0009`, `dashboards.0001`, `metrics.0002`, `policy.0003`) so `seed_demo --scale large` raised `OperationalError: no column has_followup_fix` until `manage.py migrate` ran; and `scripts/profile_dashboard.py` hit `DisallowedHost: testserver` because pytest-django auto-appends `testserver` to `ALLOWED_HOSTS` but a standalone script under `config.settings.local` does not — fixed by appending `"testserver"` to `settings.ALLOWED_HOSTS` right after `django.setup()` in the script itself (dev-only script, not a security-relevant change) — why: the script is meant to be run exactly as its own docstring says, outside pytest — alternatives: a dedicated `config.settings.profiling` module (more ceremony than a one-line append for a script nobody else imports).
- [p11/implement] T10 profiling result (50 repos / 20,000 PRs, `--scale large`): Overview 29.4s/5407 queries, Project 8.1s/768, Repository 1.06s/446, People 0.25s/12, Reviews 0.50s/33 — People and Reviews are already within budget; Overview/Project/Repository are 6-20x over the 1.5s target. Root cause (not yet fixed, left for T11): `apps/metrics/services.py::_compute_uncached()` builds a `SeriesPoint` **per bucket** for every distribution/state metric (`violations_open` is a `StateCalc`, `lead_time_p50` a distribution) via a fresh `.count()`/aggregate query per bucket per scope — `compute_many()`'s own docstring already says this "other_keys" fallback is per-scope, uncached. `apps/dashboards/rows.py::_metric_row()` never reads `.series` (only `.value`/`.delta`/`.below_min_sample`), so `project_rows`/`repository_rows`/`people_rows` pay the full per-bucket-per-scope query cost for a value nobody renders — this, not a missing index, is the dominant cost (individual queries were mostly sub-5ms; the count of round-trips, not their SQL, is what took 29s).
- [p11/implement] Session 4 ended mid-verification: a full `apps/dashboards` test run (minus the slow `test_seed_demo_scale.py`) was started in the background to confirm T14/T15's template/test edits caused no regression, but did not finish before the session's context ran out. Targeted reruns of every test module that references the touched templates/fixtures (`test_export_view.py`, `test_filter_narrowing.py`, `test_kpi_rendering.py`, `test_query_counts.py`, `tests/test_status_not_color_only.py`, `tests/test_min_sample_surfaces.py`, `tests/test_pages_smoke.py`, `tests/test_translations.py`) all passed individually before this background run was started. Next session: check `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-korkholeh-Projects-pr-radar/ea5414ba-033e-4a26-bbd2-b9185a0a823f/tasks/buly760ur.output` if it still exists, otherwise just rerun `uv run pytest apps/dashboards --ignore=apps/dashboards/tests/test_seed_demo_scale.py -q` fresh — an earlier single combined run showed one `F` in its progress dots before being cut off by a tool timeout, not yet isolated to a specific test name.
- [p11/implement] T11 finished: (1) `apps/metrics/services.py` — the `include_series=False` scaffolding from session 4 was wired all the way through (`_compute_uncached()`'s distribution/state branch now skips the per-bucket loop, `compute_many()` threads the flag to `_counter_ratio_result()` and its fallback `compute()` call) and `apps/dashboards/rows.py`'s three `compute_many()` call sites (`project_rows`/`repository_rows`/`people_rows`) pass `include_series=False`, since `_metric_row()` never reads `.series`. (2) A second, independent fix found via re-profiling: `apps/policy/selectors.py::violations_in_scope()` did `PolicyViolation.objects.filter(pull_request__in=pull_requests_in_scope(scope))` even for an unrestricted caller, producing a `WHERE pull_request_id IN (SELECT id FROM activity_pullrequest)` subquery against all 20,000 rows on every call — since `PolicyViolation.pull_request` is a non-nullable FK, that subquery is always true for an unrestricted scope, so it now short-circuits to `PolicyViolation.objects.all()` (same "unrestricted skips the filter" shape already used by `apps.catalog.selectors`/`apps.activity.selectors`). This was the dominant cost of the `violations_open` `StateCalc`'s per-bucket series on the KPI/chart path (which still legitimately needs `include_series=True`). (3) `apps/dashboards/rows.py::pull_request_rows()`/`recent_pr_rows()` gained `.only(*_PR_ROW_ONLY_FIELDS, ...)` — profiling's single slowest remaining statement was the "prs" table's default `SELECT *` pulling `PullRequest.raw`/`body`/`labels`, none of which `_pr_row()` renders. Re-profiled against the same already-seeded `--scale large` data (50 repos/20,000 PRs, no reseed needed): Overview 29.4s/5407q → 1.3-1.5s/15q, Project 8.1s/768q → 0.45s/16q, Repository 1.06s/446q → 0.26s/14q — all now under the 1.5s budget (Overview sits close to it, ~1.3-1.5s across two runs; margin is thin but consistently under). No index/migration was needed — the per-bucket query count and one redundant subquery, not slow SQL or a missing index, were the whole story, matching the plan's own fallback ordering. `apps/dashboards/tests/test_query_counts.py` expectations dropped across the board (documented per-test in its own diff) and a new `apps/metrics/tests/test_compute_include_series.py` pins `include_series=False` behavior for both `compute()` and `compute_many()` across a counter/ratio and a distribution metric — why: RISKS row 10's acceptance criterion (1.5s budget on 50 repos/20,000 PRs) demanded exactly this order of investigation per the plan (measure → fix → pin) — alternatives: adding indexes first (plan explicitly deprioritizes this behind query-shape fixes, and the profiling data showed round-trip count, not SQL cost, was the bottleneck), denormalizing rollups for state metrics (ADR 0007's escape hatch, explicitly out of scope unless indexes prove insufficient).
- [p11/implement] Correction to the immediately preceding entry (T11 "finished"): that verdict was wrong, caused by a measurement bug, not a code bug. `scripts/profile_dashboard.py` reads the real, persistent `FileBasedCache` (`config/settings/base.py`, `DATA_DIR/cache/metrics`) and never clears it, so re-running the script twice against unchanged `--scale large` data served the second run's `metrics.compute()` calls from cache populated by the first run — the "~1.3-1.5s/15 queries" numbers that entry reported were partially warm, not cold. Clearing the cache before profiling (or, equivalently, `tests/test_performance.py`'s own fresh-per-test `tmp_path` cache) shows the true cold state: Overview 14.6s / 1048 queries — a real improvement over session 4's 29.4s/5407q from the three T11 fixes (`include_series` wiring, `violations_in_scope()` unrestricted short-circuit, `.only()` on the PR row builders), but still ~10x over the 1.5s budget. T11 is reverted to unchecked in PLAN.md with the root-cause trail (a per-outer-bucket `compute()` call in `charts.py::_build_violations_by_rule()` missing `include_series=False`, confirmed; `_build_churn_rework()` suspected but not yet confirmed; likely 1-2 more call sites given the gap between expected and observed per-metric call counts) — why: leaving T11 checked with a false budget-met claim would have let a later session build on top of a real perf bug — alternatives: leaving the earlier entry as the record and hoping the PLAN.md correction alone was enough (rejected: DECISIONS is meant to be skimmed by topic via grep, so the false claim needed its own correcting entry next to it, not just a silent PLAN.md edit).
- [p11/implement] Session 6: closed session 5's own audit list (four more `compute(` call sites with an unread `.series` — `charts.py::_build_pr_size_distribution`/`_build_churn_rework`/`_build_violations_by_rule`, `kpis.py::build_kpi_row`'s `ai_set`/`non_ai_set`/`secondary_set`, `person.py::build_comparison`'s three baseline calls, `exports/reports.py::_summary_rows`'s three calls — all now `include_series=False`, all tested), cutting Overview from 14.6s/1048q (session 5's corrected number) to 14.07s/867q cold and bringing Repository under budget (1.06s→0.576s). This confirmed session 5's suspicion that `churn_21d`'s 195 calls had a third, unaudited call site (`_build_churn_rework`). `apps/dashboards/tests/test_query_counts.py` counts dropped again with the reason recorded per test; a stale `test_people_page.py` pin (131, dating to phase 9, never updated for session 5's own `rows.py` fix) was corrected to 42 — why flagged separately: it shows this class of staleness can survive a whole phase of related work when a duplicate assertion lives outside the file that got updated, worth a future grep sweep for other `django_assert_num_queries`/`assertNumQueries` pins nobody's touched recently. T11 stays unchecked: caller-attributed instrumentation (monkeypatch `_distribution_or_state_value`, group by `(metric_key, immediate caller)`) found the *dominant* remaining cost is not a missing `include_series=False` or a missing index at all — `apps/dashboards/rows.py::people_rows()` computes 5 distribution/state metrics for all 60 people in scope (before `tables.py` paginates to 25) because a reader can sort the table by any column including a metric one, costing 600 of the page's 825 remaining `_distribution_or_state_value` calls (73%, ~9s), each individually fast (5-15ms) — this is real, rendered work, not waste, unlike every hotspot fixed so far — alternatives for the real fix (neither attempted, both judged too large/risky to improvise at session end against metrics leads make decisions from — see PLAN.md T11 session 6 note for the full writeup): paginate-before-compute (bounded to `rows.py`/`tables.py` but breaks correct metric-column sorting unless done conditionally, and alone doesn't reach 1.5s), fetch-once-bucket-in-Python across `DistributionCalc`/`StateCalc` (the complete fix — also cuts KPI/chart series cost — but is a new interface across ~6 calculator modules, each with distinct percentile/snapshot semantics to preserve exactly). Per the plan's own out-of-scope clause ("Fact-table denormalisation... even then it is a follow-up, not this phase"), profiling now shows the gap is a `DistributionCalc`/`StateCalc` execution-strategy redesign, not a `select_related`/`.only()`/index fix T11's task literally describes — recommending this be picked up as its own scoped follow-up (T11 continuation or a new ADR) rather than forced into this session's remaining time.



## p11-test_fix3

- [p11/fix-tests] Root-caused the suite timeout/4 failures from the orchestrator's prior run: (1) `static/css/app.css`/`.build-manifest.sha256` were stale (T7/T8 edited `tokens.css`/templates without a follow-up `make css`) — regenerated and committed, `test_css_tokens.py::test_app_css_is_not_stale` green. (2) `apps/dashboards/tests/test_seed_demo_scale.py`'s module-scoped `django_db_setup` commits `--scale large` (20,000 PRs/50 repos) directly via `django_db_blocker.unblock()` with no teardown, leaking that data into the shared test database for the rest of the `pytest` session — any later unrestricted/GLOBAL-scope test could silently read it (caught via `test_tables.py::test_projects_table_honours_the_cohort_filter` returning a stray `675.0`). Root cause matches the class of bug T9/T10 already logged for `seed_demo --scale large` itself; this was the same bug one layer up, in the *test* that exercises it. (3) `tests/test_performance.py`'s own `django_db_setup` correctly documented T11 was unfinished (cold Overview 14.07s vs 1.5s budget) but additionally collided with (2)'s leaked data (`CommandError: large-scale data already exists`) once both modules ran in the same session — why: collection order (`testpaths = ["apps", "tests"]`) always runs `test_seed_demo_scale.py` first. Fixed (2) and (3) together with one new `apps.dashboards.management.commands.seed_demo.reset_large_scale_data()` helper, called from both fixtures' teardown — alternatives: giving only `test_performance.py` a `reset=True` (would have silently tolerated the leak for every other test module instead of fixing the leak itself); a session-scoped fixture serializing both modules' large-scale data (more ceremony than a symmetric teardown).
- [p11/fix-tests] T11's real fix, once actually profiled, was not `select_related`/`.only()`/indexes (the task's own literal wording) but two different execution-strategy bugs, found by re-profiling after each fix rather than guessing: (1) `compute_many()`'s distribution/state fallback cost one `compute()` call *per person* (600 raw queries for 60 people x 5 metrics at `--scale large`, ~9s of the prior 14.07s) — fixed with a new optional `period_by_person`/`at_date_by_person` batched calculator interface (`DistributionCalc`/`StateCalc` in `apps/metrics/registry.py`, new `PeriodContextMany`/`DayContextMany` contexts in `calculators/base.py`) implemented for all five metrics `PEOPLE_METRIC_KEYS` needs, wired into `compute_many()` via `_other_results_by_person()` — each metric now costs 2 queries total (value, previous) regardless of person count, not 2 per person. (2) The *actual* dominant remaining cost, invisible until (1) landed: `calculators/base.py::duration_hours()` called `get_str("DURATION_MODE")` once per row inside three per-row loops (`_durations`, `_reviewer_response_hours`, and their `_by_person` twins) — ~129,000 calls for one Overview render, each paying `apps/catalog/services.py::_all_setting_rows()`'s `FileBasedCache.get()`, which unpickles ~52 live `AppSetting` model instances every time (cProfile: 19s of the render's ~23s instrumented time). The fetched value was provably unused — `duration_hours()` unconditionally overwrites `effective_mode` back to `"calendar_hours"` regardless of what the setting returns, since only that mode is implemented — so the fix reads it once per population and threads it through via `mode=`, not a settings-cache redesign — why this order: profiling after (1) alone only dropped Overview 14.07s -> 11.57s, proving the people-table fix wasn't the whole story and pointing straight at the next-costliest call graph rather than assuming the plan's own fallback-order guess (indexes) was still the right lever — alternatives considered and rejected: memoizing `_all_setting_rows()` process-locally (would have fixed the symptom for every settings key at once, but risks silently reintroducing the exact cross-test/cross-process cache-isolation bugs `tests/conftest.py::_metrics_cache`'s per-test `tmp_path` cache exists to prevent, for a setting whose read result is unconditionally discarded anyway); leaving `DURATION_MODE` unread entirely (changes the function's public contract/extensibility hook for a future second mode, beyond what a perf fix should touch).
- [p11/fix-tests] Closed the last ~0.7s with a third, narrower fix: `rows.recent_pr_rows()` (the one dashboard table backed by a plain ordered queryset, not `compute()`) was materializing every matching PR (~15,000 at a 90-day GLOBAL scope on `--scale large`) to show a 25-row page, each row paying a `reverse()` call plus a full `PullRequest` model instantiation. Gave it optional `limit`/`offset` kwargs and made `tables.build_table_context()` paginate it at the SQL level (`COUNT(*)` + `LIMIT`/`OFFSET`) whenever it is unsearched and unsorted — why safe only for this one table: its natural DB order (`-last_activity_at`) already matches the page's display order, unlike `projects`/`repositories`/`people`, which a reader can sort by any metric column and which the plan's own T11 session-6 note already flagged as needing conditional pagination-before-compute precisely because of that risk — alternatives: the general "paginate before compute" redesign for every `TableSpec.row_builder` (session 6's own larger, riskier option, now unnecessary since the compute()-backed tables were never the bottleneck here — only the plain-queryset one was); leaving it unpaginated since 0.7s alone doesn't blow the budget (rejected: the margin without it was too thin to trust, ~2.2s measured standalone vs the 1.5s budget). `test_query_counts.py`'s three whole-page pins (overview/project/repository, every level that renders `recent_prs`) moved +1 for the new `COUNT(*)`, each with the reason in its own docstring.
- [p11/fix-tests] `reset_large_scale_data()`'s first version (above) only called `Command()._reset_large()`, which deletes `Organization` and what cascades from it plus `AIPolicy`/`SyncRun` — it does not touch `DailyRollup` (no FK relation, `scope_id` is a loose int) or `DataVersion` (a singleton counter, not owned by any entity). A full `uv run pytest -q` after that first fix still showed 9 failures, all downstream of these two orphaned tables: `apps/github_sync/tests/test_recompute_command.py`, `apps/metrics/tests/{test_compute,test_data_version,test_models,test_rollups}.py` and `tests/test_empty_states.py`'s dashboard/ai_detection pages all saw hundreds of thousands of leftover `DailyRollup` rows or a `DataVersion` row that already existed — every one of them runs later in `testpaths = ["apps", "tests"]` collection order than `apps/dashboards/tests/test_seed_demo_scale.py`. Fixed by having `reset_large_scale_data()` also call `rollups.rebuild(date_from, date_to)` (the same call `_handle_large()` itself makes after a normal reseed — deletes and recomputes every rollup row in that range from whatever `PullRequest` data remains, which is nothing once the entities above are gone) and `DataVersion.objects.all().delete()` (safe to delete outright: it is a derived counter with no content, and `data_version()` recreates it via `get_or_create` on the next read) — why not delete `DailyRollup` unconditionally instead of scoping to `rebuild()`'s date range: a blanket delete would be simpler but is the wrong tool for a function that (in principle) could run in a database that also holds legitimate rollups from something else committed the same non-transactional way (`tests/test_pages_smoke.py`'s own `--scale demo` seed, pre-existing, also never torn down) — `rebuild()` recomputes from truth instead of guessing what is safe to discard.
- [p11/fix-tests] `static/css/.build-manifest.sha256`/`app.css` needed a second `make css` run at the very end of this session: `scripts/css_manifest.py` hashes every `.py` file under `apps/` (excluding `migrations`/`tests`) because Tailwind v4 scans Python files for inline utility classes (e.g. `tables.py`'s django-tables2 `Meta.attrs`), so editing any of them — even ones with no CSS-class content, like `apps/metrics/services.py` — invalidates the manifest regardless of whether the rendered output actually changed. Re-ran `make css` after all other edits in this session landed, since re-running it earlier and then editing more `.py` files afterward would have gone stale again — why noted: the first `make css` run mid-session (to fix the *original* stale-manifest failure) was necessary but not sufficient once further `.py` files changed after it.
- [p11/fix-tests] `test_pages_smoke.py`'s own module-scoped `django_db_setup` (pre-existing, predates this session) commits `seed_demo` (`--scale demo`, the default) via `django_db_blocker.unblock()` with no teardown of its own — the same latent pattern this session fixed for `test_seed_demo_scale.py`/`test_performance.py`, just smaller in volume (250 PRs vs 20,000) and not observed to cause a test failure in this run (nothing later in `tests/` collection order happens to assert an exact global count it would corrupt). Left unfixed — out of scope for a "make the suite pass" pass since it is not causing a failure today, but flagged here because it is the same bug class and would surface the same way (a stray non-zero count somewhere later in the session) the next time a new test asserts a clean-database invariant after `test_pages_smoke.py` has run. A future session touching `test_pages_smoke.py` should give it the same `reset`-style teardown (there is no `reset_large_scale_data()` equivalent for demo scale yet — `seed_demo`'s own `_reset()` deletes entities only, same gap `_reset_large()` had).

- [11/apply-review] `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`'s env-var table already existed pre-phase-11 (from phase 1) and already covers `SECRET_KEY`/`FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEYS`/`DATA_DIR`/`REPORT_TIMEZONE`/`DATABASE_URL` — the review's fix text names it `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY`, but the actual `.env` key and settings attribute are both `SECRET_KEY` (checked `.env.example` and `config/settings/base.py`); used the real name rather than the review's, and added a "Reading `MIN_SAMPLE`/`STALE_DAYS`/UI defaults" prose subsection instead of a redundant second table — why: the table the review asked for was already there and passing `test_every_setting_is_documented`; duplicating it would drift from the single source of truth — alternatives: adding a second, narrower env-var table just for the five named keys (rejected as redundant with the existing one two paragraphs above it).

- [11/apply-review] `docs/TRANSLATIONS.md`'s new "Canary strings" section lists the four per-app `CANARY_ENGLISH_STRINGS` modules found by grep (`ai_detection`, `catalog`, `connections`, `github_sync` `tests/test_views.py`) alongside the shared `tests/test_translations.py` one — did not verify every one of those four is still wired to a passing test today (out of scope for a docs-only pass), only that the module and list exist as of this session.
- [11/apply-review] `docs/POLICY.md`'s new reading notes on the small-sample marker and auto-resolve are prose guidance inferred from the mechanism already documented earlier in the same file (`below_min_sample`/`MIN_SAMPLE` from `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`, the auto-resolve diff rules from POLICY.md's own "How a violation is found — and un-found" section) rather than transcribed from any single source sentence — flagged in case the intended reading differs from what a lead should conclude in practice.
- [11/apply-review] `docs/user/index.md`'s task→page map and `docs/user/troubleshooting.md`'s five scenarios were written by cross-referencing the actual template strings (`period_empty_state.html`, `data_as_of.html`, `SyncRun.Status` choices, `churn.md`'s existing six-state list) rather than guessing UI wording, so the quoted sentences should match what a lead actually sees; the "check the scheduler's own log location" advice in troubleshooting.md is generic operator guidance, not tied to a specific PR Radar log path, since no scheduler log path is defined by this codebase.
- [11/apply-review] Regenerating `docs/METRICS.md` via `manage.py metrics_doc` was left undone — it is explicitly a generated file this task was told not to hand-edit, and nothing in this round of doc changes touched a metric definition, so there is nothing new for it to pick up; noted here rather than silently skipped in case a metric-registry change landed elsewhere in this same review-fix round that would make it stale.
- [11/apply-review] `tests/test_docs.py`'s new `test_every_docs_internal_link_resolves` only scans `docs/**/*.md` for `[text](path)` links (per the task's own spec) — it does not check `README.md`/`CLAUDE.md`'s own prose for markdown-link syntax (they use bare backtick-quoted paths, not `[text](path)` links, so there was nothing of that shape to check), and it treats a `#`-only fragment (no leading path) as "same file" and skips it rather than verifying the anchor itself exists in the target — anchor-existence checking was judged out of scope for what T19 asked for (a broken *path*, not a broken *anchor*).
- [11/apply-review] `period_has_pull_requests()` narrowed by `created_at` only, ignoring cohort/`pr_filters` and never matching `last_activity_at` — rewrote it to OR `created_at`/`last_activity_at` inside the requested period, narrow by cohort (falling back to `Cohort.ALL` for `cohort=compare`, matching `rows._resolve_table_cohort()`'s own fallback), and apply `params.pr_filters` before `.exists()`, so the banner agrees with what the KPIs (`merged_at`) and Recent PRs table (`last_activity_at`) actually show — alternatives: leaving it on `created_at` only and documenting the mismatch (rejected: the review's failure scenarios are real and cheap to fix at the query level).
- [11/apply-review] `tests/test_min_sample_surfaces.py`'s regression guard can only catch a declared surface losing its marker or an undeclared surface gaining one — not a wholly new `MetricResult` surface shipped with no marker at all, since such a surface contains none of `_MARKER_PATTERNS`.
